### W5-50 — graph correctness fix (THE MAIN AUDIT TARGET)
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/stripes.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
  - New `formula_to_stripe_keys` reverse index field.
  - `insert` function — does it correctly track the reverse mirror when the forward HashSet already contains the formula?
  - `clear_for_formula` — does it correctly prune empty stripes? Idempotent? Handles unseen NodeId?
  - `pub fn range_contains_rowcol` — promoted from `pub(crate)`. Are all callers safe with public visibility?
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
  - `AdjacencyVectors::clear_outgoing` — drains outgoing[from] but only returns drained list; does NOT touch incoming. Caller (Graph::clear_outgoing) walks targets to clear back-pointers. Symmetric invariant?
  - `remove_back_pointer` — `retain` removes ALL matching back-pointers. Is "all" the right semantics? What if `add_edge(F, T)` was called twice (Phase 0 allows duplicates) — `clear_outgoing` would drain BOTH outgoing entries, then `remove_back_pointer` would remove BOTH incoming entries. Consistent? Or could there be a case where one was added by a DIFFERENT formula and gets wrongly removed?
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
  - `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` — calls edges.clear_outgoing then walks targets to remove_back_pointer. Bumps revision once. Idempotent claim?
  - `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)` — clears formula_to_range_deps + calls stripes.clear_for_formula. Bumps revision once. Idempotent?
  - `Graph::range_deps_for(node)` — returns &[RangeRef] or empty slice. Used by session-side supplemental builder.
  - `Graph::range_ref_sheet(range)` — static helper. Correct for all three RangeRef variants (Cells, WholeColumn, WholeRow)?
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
  - `schedule_with_supplemental` — new public entry point. Now also called by the wrapper `schedule(graph, dirty_subset)`. The new signature accepts a `&HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>`.
  - `tarjan_dfs` modified to iterate `outgoing + supplemental` via index split (children index < outgoing.len() uses real graph; >= uses supplemental). Off-by-one? Indexing safety?
  - Self-loop classification at SCC pop: now checks BOTH `graph.outgoing(scc[0])` AND `supplemental.get(&scc[0])`. Is the OR correct? Should it be AND? Either present?
  - Determinism: the supplemental map's iteration order is HashMap-randomized, BUT we don't iterate the map — we look up by key. So order of supplemental_for_v depends on the Vec inside, which is built in `build_range_supplemental` from a sorted iteration. Check end-to-end determinism.
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` — read lines 410-720 (the rebind path + supplemental builder + schedule_dirty). Focus on:
  - `extract_and_register_deps` now calls `self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` and `self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` AFTER `remove_formula_deps`. Order matters? Is `remove_formula_deps` redundant now or still needed for session-side reverse indices?
  - `build_range_supplemental` — for each dirty formula F with range deps, scan all dirty formulas G. The inner check: `gs != resolved_sheet` skips cross-sheet; `range_contains_rowcol(range, gr, gc)` checks bounds. Does NOT exclude `g == f` — self-range cycles intentional. Is the self-edge for a formula whose range covers itself but isn't really cyclic (e.g., A1 = SUM(A1:A10) where A1 is the FIRST cell — Excel treats this as a cycle) handled correctly?
  - Determinism of the supplemental map: dirty_vec is sorted by NodeId. dirty_addrs is built from dirty_vec in order. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in sorted order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up in sorted-by-G order. Confirm.
  - **A potential subtle bug**: `schedule_dirty` drains `self.dirty` into `dirty_vec`. THEN calls `build_range_supplemental(&dirty_vec)`. But by this point, `self.dirty` is EMPTY. `build_range_supplemental` looks at `cell_address_for(n)` which uses `self.cell_index` — that's fine. But it does NOT actually look at `self.dirty`. So that's OK. Verify.
  - Does the supplemental include ALL dirty formula nodes including non-cell-address nodes (e.g., a hypothetical RangeNode or FormulaRegionNode in the dirty set)? The `cell_address_for` filter handles that — non-Cell nodes are filtered out. But should the supplemental be considered there at all? FormulaRegion nodes might be in the dirty set in Phase 4.7. For now, filtering out is correct.

### W5-51 — trig batch
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs` lines 715-855 (the new SIN/COS/TAN/ASIN/ACOS/ATAN/ATAN2 definitions). Focus on:
  - TAN(π/2): claim is "huge-finite, not error". IEEE 754 says (π/2).tan() is finite because π/2 isn't exactly representable. Verify with actual `f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2.tan()` value. Test `tan_near_half_pi_is_huge_finite_not_error` asserts `v > 1e10` — is that tight? Actual value is ≈1.6e16.
  - ASIN/ACOS domain check uses `if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n)`. Does this correctly reject NaN? `RangeInclusive::contains` for NaN returns false (NaN is not <= or >= anything), so `!contains(NaN)` is true → returns #NUM!. But Rust's `f64::asin(NaN)` returns NaN, which `sanitize_f64` would also surface as #NUM!. Either way OK, but the redundancy means the explicit check is somewhat defensive.
  - ATAN2 arg-order claim: comment says "Excel arg order is `(x, y)` historically — but the W3C / OOXML standard and modern Excel use `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)`." Then the code does `y.atan2(x)` where `x = args[0]`, `y = args[1]`. So `ATAN2(args[0]=x, args[1]=y)` → `y.atan2(x)`. **Verify this matches Excel canon.** Excel docs: `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)` returns arctangent of y_num/x_num. Rust's `f64::atan2(y, x)` returns arctangent of y/x. So `args[1].atan2(args[0])` = y_num.atan2(x_num) = arctan(y/x). ✓
    - Test `atan2_basic_quadrants`: ATAN2(1, 0) → 0 (along +x). atan2(0_y, 1_x) = arctan(0/1) = 0. ✓
    - ATAN2(0, 1) → π/2 (along +y). atan2(1_y, 0_x) = arctan(1/0) = π/2. ✓
    - ATAN2(-1, 0) → π (along -x). atan2(0_y, -1_x) = arctan(0/-1) → π (since x<0, y=+0). ✓
    - ATAN2(1, 1) → π/4. atan2(1, 1) = π/4. ✓
  - ATAN2(0, 0) → #DIV/0!. IronCalc: confirm this matches.
- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs` — 7 new entries; expected-count test updated to 59.
- `quantbook-engine/docs/compat/excel-matrix.md` — trig row flipped to ✅, Tests=13. Does the script's coverage stay at 40%?

## Specific concerns to evaluate

### CONCERN-1: Function-signature scope discovery (Phase 4.3 V2)

After W5-51 shipped, the operator surfaced a hidden scope issue: the function signature `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` does NOT support range-aware functions (SUMIF/COUNTIF/VLOOKUP). The W5-49 decision doc did not mention this. Is this a:
- (a) Legitimate scope discovery — W5-49 was about graph correctness, not function-call infrastructure.
- (b) Doc gap — the implementation outline should have called out that SUMIF would need additional infra beyond just Option C.

Codex's pre-decision review listed stop conditions including "Phase 4.3 V2 actually requires FormulaRegion for SEMANTICS (not just perf)". Did Codex implicitly assume the function infra would just work? If so, that's a Codex miss too.

### CONCERN-2: Determinism of build_range_supplemental

`schedule_dirty` drains `self.dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` into `dirty_vec` then sorts. Good. But `dirty_addrs` is built from `dirty_vec.iter().filter_map(...)`. Iteration order preserved. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in the same order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up with G's in sorted-by-NodeId order.

BUT: the OUTER `for &(f, fs, _, _) in &dirty_addrs` is also in sorted order. So the HashMap's insertion order is sorted-by-F-NodeId. Determinism downstream of HashMap depends on iteration order (HashMap is randomized). The CONSUMER of `supplemental` is `tarjan_dfs`, which looks up by key (`supplemental.get(&v)`) — so the randomized iteration order doesn't matter. Only the per-entry Vec order matters, and that IS sorted. ✓

But there's a subtle point: in Tarjan, the order of children visited at each frame affects the resulting SCC discovery order. For a deterministic schedule output, the per-frame child order must be deterministic. Real outgoing edges come from `Vec<NodeId>` (insertion order — deterministic). Supplemental edges come from `supplemental[v]` which IS sorted. So combined order = (real edges in insertion order) ++ (supplemental in sorted order). Deterministic.

UNLESS: real edges themselves are NOT in deterministic insertion order across runs (e.g., the extract_and_register_deps walk order depends on a HashMap iteration). The walk_plan_for_deps walker is recursive over ExprPlan AST — fully deterministic. Then `deduplicate` uses `HashSet` + `retain` — retain preserves order. So insertion order is the AST walk order. Deterministic. ✓

But hold on — re-bind: `clear_outgoing` drains and `extract_and_register_deps` re-adds. So if a formula's plan is identical, the new outgoing list should equal the old. Determinism test (`determinism_rebind_same_plan_same_edges` in graph.rs::tests) verifies this. ✓

OK determinism looks correct. **But please double-check this independently — I might be missing a subtle case.**

### CONCERN-3: Self-edge in supplemental

`build_range_supplemental` does NOT exclude `g == f`. This is intentional for `A1 = SUM(A1:A1)` self-range cycles. Test `self_range_cycle_via_supplemental` confirms this works.

But what about `A1 = SUM(A1:A10)` where A1 is the cell that contains the SUM formula and A1 is inside the SUM range? Excel: this is a circular reference. My implementation: supplemental[A1] = [A1] (since A1 is in A1:A10). Tarjan emits A1 as cycled. ✓

What about a case where the range covers F but F shouldn't be self-referencing? E.g., F is at C1 with `=SUM(A1:Z1)` — F is NOT in the range (column C is in A1:Z1 columns). The range covers (row 1, cols A..Z). F is at (row 1, col C) → IS in the range. supplemental[F] = [F] if F is dirty. Tarjan emits F as cycled. **WAIT** — is that wrong? Excel would treat `=SUM(A1:Z1)` at C1 as a self-cycle because C1 is in A1:Z1. ✓ this is correct.

OK self-edge handling looks correct.

### CONCERN-4: Cross-sheet range deps

`build_range_supplemental` resolves the range's sheet to `range_ref_sheet(range).unwrap_or(fs)` (F's owning sheet). Then checks `gs == resolved_sheet`. For ranges with `sheet: Some(other_sheet)`, this correctly skips G's on F's sheet.

BUT: today the binder always sets `sheet: Some(_)` per Phase 3.3 comment. Cross-sheet refs aren't fully wired (Phase 4.6). So in practice this matters mostly for the (likely) future when cross-sheet ranges become real. Until then, all range deps are intra-sheet. **Is this still correct for the future cross-sheet case?**

### CONCERN-5: W5-51 trig — what's NOT covered

- SINH / COSH / TANH (hyperbolic) — deferred to wave 2 per matrix. Fine.
- ASINH / ACOSH / ATANH — same. Fine.
- Are there any trig edge cases the tests miss?
  - Negative zero: TAN(-0.0) → should be -0.0 or 0.0? Test doesn't cover.
  - Subnormals: SIN(f64::MIN_POSITIVE) → ~0. Test doesn't cover.
  - Infinity input: SIN(f64::INFINITY) → NaN → sanitize_f64 → #NUM!. Test doesn't cover.
- Coverage script still at 40% — is this honest? The 7 trig functions are one row in the matrix; the matrix has 217 rows total. Adding 7 functions in one row doesn't move the row count, but it does change the count of ✅ rows. Was the matrix-coverage-reporter script run after the matrix update? Verify.

### CONCERN-6: Existing test failures in light of new code

W5-50 adds `clear_outgoing` to extract_and_register_deps. This means EVERY on_set_formula (including re-binds to the SAME plan) now does a clear-then-reinsert. Performance: for a single-formula rebind this is negligible. For a workbook with 1M formulas being initially loaded via rebuild_from_workbook (which calls extract_and_register_deps per formula), each formula now does an O(1) reverse-index drop on a HashMap entry that doesn't exist yet (idempotent no-op). Should be fast.

But: does the FIRST-EVER bind of a formula trigger a no-op clear path that's actually cheap? Verify by reading the code:
- `clear_outgoing(formula_node)` calls `edges.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` which does `std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[from.index()])` — drains a `Vec<NodeId>` that's empty → returns empty vec. Then walks the empty drained list → no back-pointer removal. Bumps revision once.
- `clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` removes from `formula_to_range_deps` (a HashMap remove — fast even if absent). Then calls `stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node)` which does `formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node)` — fast remove of absent key.

OK fast. No perf concern.

The commit messages claim "cargo audit ✓ (1 pre-existing allowed warning)". Verify that the actual current `cargo audit` output matches. The previous run (in W5-50) said this; W5-51 should not have changed dependencies. But verify.

     1	//! Stripe index — `(sheet, axis, index) → set of dependent NodeIds`. The A5 acceptance
     2	//! piece: given a cell-coordinate write, find all formulas that reference a range
     3	//! containing that cell, in time proportional to the number of distinct stripes touched
     4	//! (not the number of formulas).
     5	//!
     6	//! ## Pattern (CORR-21)
     7	//!
     8	//! Per the Week 3 Day 0 reference deep-read (`docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md`),
     9	//! Formualizer's `engine/graph/range_deps.rs:36-192` is the closest precedent. The
    10	//! pattern:
    11	//!
    12	//! - `StripeKey { sheet_id, stripe_type: Row | Column, index: u32 }`.
    13	//! - `stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>`.
    14	//! - At range registration, a **shape heuristic** picks the cheaper axis to index along:
    15	//!   `if height > width → Column stripes`, `else → Row stripes`. So `SUM(A:A)` registers
    16	//!   ONE Column-stripe entry (col 0); `SUM(A1:A1000)` also ONE Column entry; `SUM(A1:Z1)`
    17	//!   ONE Row entry. A range `A1:Z1000` with height==width=tie goes to Row stripes (the
    18	//!   `else` branch).
    19	//!
    20	//! We intentionally **skip** Formualizer's optional 256×256 Block stripes for Phase 0.
    21	//! They add a third stripe axis only when `enable_block_stripes` is set AND the range
    22	//! is 2D; the A5 falsifier doesn't need them. Revisit in Week 4 if A1 acceptance
    23	//! reveals 2D-region perf cliffs.
    24	//!
    25	//! ## Precision-check requirement
    26	//!
    27	//! The stripe map is **coarser** than the range. `SUM(A1:A10)` registers under Column 0,
    28	//! but a write to `A500` would falsely match. Callers MUST cross-check each candidate
    29	//! returned by `dependents_for_cell` against the formula's actual `RangeRef` (stored on
    30	//! `Graph::formula_to_range_deps`) before considering it a true dependent.
    31	//!
    32	//! `Graph::dependents_for_cell` (added in this commit) wraps both steps.
    33	
    34	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    35	
    36	use ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef;
    37	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    38	
    39	use crate::node::NodeId;
    40	
    41	/// Axis discriminator for `StripeKey`. Phase 0 has only Row and Column; Block is
    42	/// deferred (see module doc).
    43	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    44	pub enum StripeType {
    45	    Row,
    46	    Column,
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// A single stripe identifier: `(sheet, axis, index)`. For `StripeType::Row`, `index` is
    50	/// a `RowId`; for `StripeType::Column`, a `ColId`. Stored as `u32` since both type-alias
    51	/// to `u32` upstream.
    52	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    53	pub struct StripeKey {
    54	    pub sheet_id: SheetId,
    55	    pub stripe_type: StripeType,
    56	    pub index: u32,
    57	}
    58	
    59	/// Sparse per-stripe index of which formulas depend on each stripe.
    60	///
    61	/// Use via `Graph::register_range_dependency` and `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — those
    62	/// wrap the stripe insertion and precision-check together.
    63	///
    64	/// ## Reverse index (Phase 4 / W5-50)
    65	///
    66	/// `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>` records, per formula
    67	/// node, every stripe key it has been registered against. Used by
    68	/// `clear_for_formula` to revoke a formula's stripe membership on re-bind in
    69	/// O(stripes-this-formula-is-in) instead of scanning the whole stripe map. The
    70	/// reverse vector is append-on-newly-inserted (matches the forward
    71	/// `HashSet::insert` returning `true`), so a re-registration of the same
    72	/// `(formula, range)` does not duplicate the reverse entry.
    73	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    74	pub struct StripeIndex {
    75	    stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>,
    76	    /// Reverse lookup: which stripes does `formula_node` appear in? Mirror of
    77	    /// `stripe_to_dependents` keyed by formula. Kept in lock-step with the
    78	    /// forward map via the `insert` helper.
    79	    formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>,
    80	}
    81	
    82	impl StripeIndex {
    83	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    84	        Self::default()
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    /// Distinct stripe keys present in the map. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
    88	    pub fn stripe_count(&self) -> usize {
    89	        self.stripe_to_dependents.len()
    90	    }
    91	
    92	    /// Total `(stripe_key, formula_node)` insertions across all stripes. The A5 acceptance
    93	    /// test asserts this scales linearly with formula count (1 insert per formula via the
    94	    /// shape heuristic), NOT with range size.
    95	    pub fn total_insertions(&self) -> usize {
    96	        self.stripe_to_dependents.values().map(|s| s.len()).sum()
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    /// Test-only read access for A4 structural assertions (CORR-24 typed accessor pattern).
   100	    #[cfg(test)]
   101	    pub(crate) fn raw_map(&self) -> &HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>> {
   102	        &self.stripe_to_dependents
   103	    }
   104	
   105	    /// Register `formula_node`'s dependency on `range`. Picks the cheaper-to-index axis
   106	    /// via the shape heuristic; inserts one entry per touched stripe index along that
   107	    /// axis. Resolves the `RangeRef`'s optional sheet to `sheet_id` if `None`.
   108	    ///
   109	    /// **Panics** if the `range` has reversed bounds (start > end on any axis). Per the
   110	    /// no-fallbacks rule (audit finding H1, 2026-05-12): malformed ranges previously
   111	    /// silently no-op'd the stripe insert because `for c in start..=end` is an empty
   112	    /// range when `start > end`. The formula would have appeared registered (entry in
   113	    /// `formula_to_range_deps`) but be unreachable via writes — silent dependency loss.
   114	    /// Now: loud panic at the boundary. Caller is responsible for normalizing before
   115	    /// calling.
   116	    pub fn register(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId, range: &RangeRef, sheet_id: SheetId) {
   117	        match range {
   118	            RangeRef::Cells {
   119	                sheet,
   120	                start_col,
   121	                start_row,
   122	                end_col,
   123	                end_row,
   124	                ..
   125	            } => {
   126	                assert!(
   127	                    start_col <= end_col,
   128	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   129	                );
   130	                assert!(
   131	                    start_row <= end_row,
   132	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   133	                );
   134	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   135	                let height = end_row - start_row + 1;
   136	                let width = end_col - start_col + 1;
   137	                if height > width {
   138	                    for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   139	                        self.insert(
   140	                            StripeKey {
   141	                                sheet_id: s,
   142	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   143	                                index: c,
   144	                            },
   145	                            formula_node,
   146	                        );
   147	                    }
   148	                } else {
   149	                    for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   150	                        self.insert(
   151	                            StripeKey {
   152	                                sheet_id: s,
   153	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   154	                                index: r,
   155	                            },
   156	                            formula_node,
   157	                        );
   158	                    }
   159	                }
   160	            }
   161	            RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   162	                sheet,
   163	                start_col,
   164	                end_col,
   165	                ..
   166	            } => {
   167	                assert!(
   168	                    start_col <= end_col,
   169	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeColumn has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   170	                );
   171	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   172	                for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   173	                    self.insert(
   174	                        StripeKey {
   175	                            sheet_id: s,
   176	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   177	                            index: c,
   178	                        },
   179	                        formula_node,
   180	                    );
   181	                }
   182	            }
   183	            RangeRef::WholeRow {
   184	                sheet,
   185	                start_row,
   186	                end_row,
   187	                ..
   188	            } => {
   189	                assert!(
   190	                    start_row <= end_row,
   191	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeRow has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   192	                );
   193	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   194	                for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   195	                    self.insert(
   196	                        StripeKey {
   197	                            sheet_id: s,
   198	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   199	                            index: r,
   200	                        },
   201	                        formula_node,
   202	                    );
   203	                }
   204	            }
   205	        }
   206	    }
   207	
   208	    fn insert(&mut self, key: StripeKey, formula_node: NodeId) {
   209	        let newly_inserted = self
   210	            .stripe_to_dependents
   211	            .entry(key)
   212	            .or_default()
   213	            .insert(formula_node);
   214	        // Mirror into the reverse index ONLY when the forward HashSet
   215	        // accepted the entry as new. Re-registering the same
   216	        // `(formula, range)` is a no-op on both sides — the original A5
   217	        // stripe-inserts counter semantics rely on this.
   218	        if newly_inserted {
   219	            self.formula_to_stripe_keys
   220	                .entry(formula_node)
   221	                .or_default()
   222	                .push(key);
   223	        }
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    /// Revoke every stripe membership held by `formula_node`. Called by
   227	    /// `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` on re-bind so a formula whose
   228	    /// range deps changed (`SUM(A:A) → SUM(B:B)`) no longer falsely dirty
   229	    /// from writes to the OLD range.
   230	    ///
   231	    /// O(stripes-this-formula-is-in) thanks to the reverse index. A
   232	    /// formula with no stripe membership (e.g., one that never called
   233	    /// `register_range_dependency`) is a no-op.
   234	    ///
   235	    /// Empties stripe buckets are pruned: if `formula_node` was the last
   236	    /// dependent on a stripe, the `StripeKey` entry is removed entirely.
   237	    /// This keeps `stripe_count()` honest.
   238	    ///
   239	    /// Idempotent: calling twice on the same node yields the same final
   240	    /// state.
   241	    pub fn clear_for_formula(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   242	        let Some(keys) = self.formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node) else {
   243	            return;
   244	        };
   245	        for key in keys {
   246	            if let Some(set) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get_mut(&key) {
   247	                set.remove(&formula_node);
   248	                if set.is_empty() {
   249	                    self.stripe_to_dependents.remove(&key);
   250	                }
   251	            }
   252	        }
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    /// Test-only typed accessor for the reverse index (CORR-24 pattern).
   256	    /// Returns the list of stripe keys the formula appears under, or
   257	    /// `None` if it's never been registered.
   258	    #[cfg(test)]
   259	    pub(crate) fn stripe_keys_for_formula(&self, formula_node: NodeId) -> Option<&Vec<StripeKey>> {
   260	        self.formula_to_stripe_keys.get(&formula_node)
   261	    }
   262	
   263	    /// Candidate dependents for a cell write at `(sheet, row, col)`. Returns the union of
   264	    /// the Row-stripe-at-row and Column-stripe-at-col bucket contents. **Coarse**:
   265	    /// candidates must be precision-checked against their `formula_to_range_deps` to
   266	    /// drop false positives. `Graph::dependents_for_cell` performs the precision check.
   267	    pub fn candidates_for_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> HashSet<NodeId> {
   268	        let mut out = HashSet::new();
   269	        let row_key = StripeKey {
   270	            sheet_id: sheet,
   271	            stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   272	            index: row,
   273	        };
   274	        if let Some(deps) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get(&row_key) {
   275	            out.extend(deps.iter().copied());
   276	        }
   277	        let col_key = StripeKey {
   278	            sheet_id: sheet,
   279	            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   280	            index: col,
   281	        };
   282	        if let Some(deps) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get(&col_key) {
   283	            out.extend(deps.iter().copied());
   284	        }
   285	        out
   286	    }
   287	}
   288	
   289	/// Test whether `range` (sheet-resolved) contains the cell at `(row, col)`. The sheet
   290	/// check is implicit (caller already filtered by sheet via the stripe key); this only
   291	/// validates row/col bounds.
   292	///
   293	/// Public (W5-50) so the Phase 3 runtime can reuse it when building the
   294	/// supplemental adjacency for `topo::schedule_with_supplemental`.
   295	pub fn range_contains_rowcol(range: &RangeRef, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> bool {
   296	    match range {
   297	        RangeRef::Cells {
   298	            start_col,
   299	            start_row,
   300	            end_col,
   301	            end_row,
   302	            ..
   303	        } => row >= *start_row && row <= *end_row && col >= *start_col && col <= *end_col,
   304	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   305	            start_col, end_col, ..
   306	        } => col >= *start_col && col <= *end_col,
   307	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   308	            start_row, end_row, ..
   309	        } => row >= *start_row && row <= *end_row,
   310	    }
   311	}
   312

   314	mod tests {
   315	    use super::*;
   316	
   317	    fn cells(start_col: u32, start_row: u32, end_col: u32, end_row: u32) -> RangeRef {
   318	        RangeRef::Cells {
   319	            sheet: None,
   320	            start_col,
   321	            start_row,
   322	            end_col,
   323	            end_row,
   324	            abs_start_col: false,
   325	            abs_start_row: false,
   326	            abs_end_col: false,
   327	            abs_end_row: false,
   328	        }
   329	    }
   330	
   331	    fn whole_col(start: u32, end: u32) -> RangeRef {
   332	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   333	            sheet: None,
   334	            start_col: start,
   335	            end_col: end,
   336	            abs_start: false,
   337	            abs_end: false,
   338	        }
   339	    }
   340	
   341	    fn whole_row(start: u32, end: u32) -> RangeRef {
   342	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   343	            sheet: None,
   344	            start_row: start,
   345	            end_row: end,
   346	            abs_start: false,
   347	            abs_end: false,
   348	        }
   349	    }

   351	    #[test]
   352	    fn empty_index_zero_stripes() {
   353	        let idx = StripeIndex::new();
   354	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 0);
   355	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 0);
   356	    }
   357	
   358	    #[test]
   359	    fn whole_column_registers_one_column_stripe() {
   360	        // A:A → 1 Column stripe at col 0.
   361	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   362	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   363	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   364	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   365	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0);
   366	        assert_eq!(candidates, [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect());
   367	    }
   368	
   369	    #[test]
   370	    fn whole_row_registers_one_row_stripe() {
   371	        // 1:1 → 1 Row stripe at row 0.
   372	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   373	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_row(0, 0), 0);
   374	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   375	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 0, 99);
   376	        assert_eq!(candidates, [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect());
   377	    }
   378	
   379	    #[test]
   380	    fn tall_cells_range_uses_column_stripes() {
   381	        // A1:A1000 (height=1000, width=1, h>w) → 1 Column stripe at col 0.
   382	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   383	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &cells(0, 0, 0, 999), 0);
   384	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   385	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   386	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0);
   387	        assert_eq!(candidates, [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect());
   388	    }
   389	
   390	    #[test]
   391	    fn wide_cells_range_uses_row_stripes() {
   392	        // A1:Z1 (height=1, width=26, h<=w) → 1 Row stripe at row 0.
   393	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   394	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &cells(0, 0, 25, 0), 0);
   395	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   396	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   397	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 0, 13);
   398	        assert_eq!(candidates, [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect());
   399	    }
   400	
   401	    #[test]
   402	    fn square_cells_range_ties_to_row_stripes() {
   403	        // A1:B2 (height=2, width=2, h==w → else branch) → 2 Row stripes (rows 0 and 1).
   404	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   405	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &cells(0, 0, 1, 1), 0);
   406	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 2);
   407	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 2);
   408	        // Both rows 0 and 1 should find the formula
   409	        assert_eq!(
   410	            idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 0, 0),
   411	            [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect()
   412	        );
   413	        assert_eq!(
   414	            idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 1, 0),
   415	            [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect()
   416	        );
   417	        // Row 2 is outside the range
   418	        assert!(idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 2, 0).is_empty());
   419	    }
   420	
   421	    #[test]
   422	    fn whole_column_spans_multiple_cols() {
   423	        // A:C → 3 Column stripes at cols 0, 1, 2.
   424	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   425	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 2), 0);
   426	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 3);
   427	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 3);
   428	        for col in 0..=2 {
   429	            assert_eq!(
   430	                idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 100, col),
   431	                [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect()
   432	            );
   433	        }
   434	        assert!(idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 100, 3).is_empty());
   435	    }
   436	
   437	    #[test]
   438	    fn many_formulas_one_stripe_bucket() {
   439	        // The A5 motivation: 100 formulas all referencing A:A share the SINGLE Column-0
   440	        // stripe — total stripe entries stay at 1 regardless of formula count.
   441	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   442	        for i in 1..=100u32 {
   443	            idx.register(NodeId(i), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   444	        }
   445	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   446	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 100);
   447	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0);
   448	        assert_eq!(candidates.len(), 100);
   449	    }
   450	
   451	    #[test]
   452	    fn cross_sheet_isolation() {
   453	        // Same range registered on two different sheets → 2 stripe entries (different
   454	        // sheet_ids). Cell write on sheet 0 doesn't match sheet 1 formula.
   455	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   456	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   457	        idx.register(NodeId(2), &whole_col(0, 0), 1);
   458	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 2);
   459	        assert_eq!(
   460	            idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0),
   461	            [NodeId(1)].into_iter().collect()
   462	        );
   463	        assert_eq!(
   464	            idx.candidates_for_cell(1, 500, 0),
   465	            [NodeId(2)].into_iter().collect()
   466	        );
   467	    }

   469	    #[test]
   470	    fn range_contains_rowcol_for_cells_variant() {
   471	        let r = cells(0, 0, 1, 9); // A1:B10
   472	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 0, 0));
   473	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 5, 1));
   474	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 9, 1));
   475	        assert!(!range_contains_rowcol(&r, 10, 0)); // beyond end_row
   476	        assert!(!range_contains_rowcol(&r, 0, 2)); // beyond end_col
   477	    }
   478	
   479	    #[test]
   480	    fn range_contains_rowcol_for_whole_column() {
   481	        let r = whole_col(0, 2); // A:C
   482	                                 // Any row in cols 0..=2 is contained.
   483	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 0, 0));
   484	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, u32::MAX, 2));
   485	        assert!(!range_contains_rowcol(&r, 0, 3)); // col 3 outside
   486	    }
   487	
   488	    #[test]
   489	    fn range_contains_rowcol_for_whole_row() {
   490	        let r = whole_row(5, 7);
   491	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 5, 0));
   492	        assert!(range_contains_rowcol(&r, 7, u32::MAX));
   493	        assert!(!range_contains_rowcol(&r, 4, 0));
   494	        assert!(!range_contains_rowcol(&r, 8, 0));
   495	    }
   496	
   497	    /// A5 ACCEPTANCE: register 100k formulas `SUM(A1:A_n)` with `n` varying. Total
   498	    /// stripe entries (sum across all formulas' insertions) ≤ 2 × formula_count.
   499	    ///
   500	    /// With Formualizer's shape heuristic, each `Cells { 0, 0, 0, n-1 }` for n ≥ 2 has
   501	    /// height=n, width=1, h>w → 1 Column stripe at col 0. For n=1 (h==w==1, tie → else
   502	    /// branch) → 1 Row stripe at row 0. So all 100k formulas land in 2 buckets total
   503	    /// (one Column at col 0 + one Row at row 0). `total_insertions == 100_000`.
   504	    ///
   505	    /// The 2× upper bound is the spec's guard against quadratic growth — actual scaling
   506	    /// is far better (linear with formula count).
   507	    #[test]
   508	    fn a5_acceptance_100k_formulas_linear_scaling() {
   509	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   510	        const N: u32 = 100_000;
   511	        for i in 1..=N {
   512	            idx.register(NodeId(i), &cells(0, 0, 0, i - 1), 0);
   513	        }
   514	        // Two distinct stripe keys: Column at col 0 (for n>=2), Row at row 0 (for n=1).
   515	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 2);
   516	        // Exactly N insertions — one per formula.
   517	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), N as usize);
   518	        // The bound from the spec:
   519	        assert!(idx.total_insertions() as u64 <= 2 * (N as u64));
   520	
   521	        // Cell write at A500 → all 100k-1 formulas (except n=1) match the Column stripe.
   522	        // Plus the n=1 formula matches the Row 0 stripe (since 500 is the row but 0 is
   523	        // the row of the n=1 range — wait, candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0) checks Row 500
   524	        // and Column 0. Row 500's stripe is empty; Column 0's stripe has all 99999
   525	        // formulas (n>=2). The n=1 formula registered Row 0, not Row 500, so it
   526	        // doesn't appear in the Row 500 stripe.
   527	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 500, 0);
   528	        assert_eq!(candidates.len(), (N - 1) as usize); // n=2 through n=100_000
   529	    }
   530	
   531	    #[test]
   532	    fn duplicate_register_idempotent() {
   533	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   534	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   535	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &whole_col(0, 0), 0); // duplicate
   536	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   537	        // HashSet dedupes; total_insertions counts distinct formulas per stripe.
   538	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   539	    }

   541	    // ===== Audit H1 fix (2026-05-12): malformed ranges panic instead of silently no-op =====
   542	
   543	    #[test]
   544	    #[should_panic(expected = "reversed cols")]
   545	    fn register_panics_on_cells_reversed_cols() {
   546	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   547	        let bad = RangeRef::Cells {
   548	            sheet: None,
   549	            start_col: 5,
   550	            start_row: 0,
   551	            end_col: 2,
   552	            end_row: 9,
   553	            abs_start_col: false,
   554	            abs_start_row: false,
   555	            abs_end_col: false,
   556	            abs_end_row: false,
   557	        };
   558	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &bad, 0);
   559	    }
   560	
   561	    #[test]
   562	    #[should_panic(expected = "reversed rows")]
   563	    fn register_panics_on_cells_reversed_rows() {
   564	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   565	        let bad = RangeRef::Cells {
   566	            sheet: None,
   567	            start_col: 0,
   568	            start_row: 9,
   569	            end_col: 1,
   570	            end_row: 2,

   575	        };
   576	        idx.register(NodeId(1), &bad, 0);

   613	        let map = idx.raw_map();

   616	            sheet_id: 0,
   617	            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   618	            index: 0,
   619	        };

   621	            map.get(&key).unwrap(),
   622	            &[NodeId(1), NodeId(2)].into_iter().collect::<HashSet<_>>()
   623	        );
   624	    }

   632	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_row(5, 5), 0); // Row 5 stripe
   633	        let keys = idx.stripe_keys_for_formula(NodeId(7)).expect("present");

   643	            index: 5,
   644	        }));
   645	    }

   648	    fn reverse_index_dedups_same_formula_same_stripe() {
   649	        // Two ranges that share a stripe (`A:A` and `A1:A10` both hit Col 0)
   650	        // for the same formula must NOT create two reverse-index entries —
   651	        // the forward HashSet dedups; the reverse vector mirrors that.
   652	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   653	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   654	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &cells(0, 0, 0, 9), 0); // A1:A10 → Col 0 (height>width)
   655	        let keys = idx.stripe_keys_for_formula(NodeId(7)).expect("present");
   656	        assert_eq!(keys.len(), 1, "two ranges, same stripe → one reverse entry");
   657	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   658	    }
   659	
   660	    #[test]
   661	    fn clear_for_formula_removes_from_all_stripes() {
   662	        // Formula registered on Col 0 and Row 5; clearing must drop both.
   663	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   664	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   665	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_row(5, 5), 0);
   666	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 2);
   667	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 2);
   668	
   669	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(7));
   670	
   671	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 0, "both stripes were singleton-pruned");
   672	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 0);
   673	        assert!(idx.stripe_keys_for_formula(NodeId(7)).is_none());
   674	        // Cell write at A5 finds no candidates.
   675	        assert!(idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 5, 0).is_empty());
   676	    }
   677	
   678	    #[test]
   679	    fn clear_for_formula_preserves_other_formulas() {
   680	        // 100 formulas share Col 0 stripe; clearing one drops only that one.
   681	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   682	        for i in 1..=100u32 {
   683	            idx.register(NodeId(i), &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   684	        }
   685	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 100);
   686	
   687	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(42));
   688	
   689	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 99, "only 1 removed");
   690	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1, "stripe still has 99 deps");
   691	        // The cleared formula no longer matches.
   692	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 0, 0);
   693	        assert_eq!(candidates.len(), 99);
   694	        assert!(!candidates.contains(&NodeId(42)));
   695	    }

   701	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(7));
   702	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(7)); // no-op
   703	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(999)); // never registered, no-op
   704	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 0);
   705	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 0);

   711	        // post-clear state must accept the new registration cleanly.
   712	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   713	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_col(0, 0), 0); // A:A
   714	        idx.clear_for_formula(NodeId(7));
   715	        idx.register(NodeId(7), &whole_col(1, 1), 0); // B:B
   716	
   717	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1);
   718	        // A5 no longer dirties; B5 does.
   719	        assert!(idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 5, 0).is_empty());
   720	        assert_eq!(
   721	            idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 5, 1),
   722	            [NodeId(7)].into_iter().collect()
   723	        );
   724	    }

   726	    #[test]

   728	        // Col 0 has [B1, C1]; clearing B1 leaves [C1] and the stripe stays.
   729	        let mut idx = StripeIndex::new();
   730	        let b1 = NodeId(1);
   731	        let c1 = NodeId(2);
   732	        idx.register(b1, &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   733	        idx.register(c1, &whole_col(0, 0), 0);
   734	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 2);
   735	
   736	        idx.clear_for_formula(b1);
   737	
   738	        assert_eq!(idx.stripe_count(), 1, "stripe survives (C1 still in it)");
   739	        assert_eq!(idx.total_insertions(), 1);
   740	        let candidates = idx.candidates_for_cell(0, 5, 0);
   741	        assert_eq!(candidates, [c1].into_iter().collect());
   742	    }

     1	//! Per-node adjacency storage for the calc graph.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per spec Decision A (T1-D02): hand-rolled compact adjacency vectors. petgraph would be
     4	//! convenient but its `StableGraph` allocates a `HashSet<EdgeIndex>` per node — orders of
     5	//! magnitude more memory than we can afford at 25M-cell scale.
     6	//!
     7	//! Phase 0 uses plain `Vec<NodeId>` per direction per node. The Week 3 plan flagged
     8	//! `SmallVec<[NodeId; 4]>` as an option (most cells depend on ≤4 things, inline storage
     9	//! avoids the allocation). That optimization is a Week 4 perf concern, NOT a Week 3
    10	//! requirement — Phase 0 ships with plain `Vec`s so we don't add a new workspace dep.
    11	//!
    12	//! The opus-arch audit + the reference deep-read both flagged that HyperFormula's
    13	//! `Array.includes()`-based dedup on edge insertion is O(out-degree) per add
    14	//! (`Graph.ts:177-181`, internally acknowledged at `Graph.ts:25-27`). Quantbook avoids the
    15	//! dedup cost entirely by NOT deduplicating on insert — callers must not double-add the
    16	//! same edge. If profiling later shows duplicate edges are a problem, switching to
    17	//! `FxHashSet<NodeId>` per node is a localized change.
    18	
    19	use crate::node::NodeId;
    20	
    21	/// Per-node outgoing + incoming adjacency vectors.
    22	///
    23	/// Outgoing edge `u -> v` means "u depends on v" — when v changes, u must recompute.
    24	/// Incoming is the reverse, for the dirty-propagation walk (W3-2).
    25	///
    26	/// Invariants:
    27	/// - `outgoing.len() == incoming.len() == graph.node_count()`. The `Graph` constructor
    28	///   maintains this via `push_node()` calls below.
    29	/// - For every `v in outgoing[u]`, `u in incoming[v]` (symmetry). `add_edge` maintains.
    30	/// - Phase 0 has NO removal. `remove_edge` is a Phase 3+ feature when row/col delete lands.
    31	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    32	pub struct AdjacencyVectors {
    33	    outgoing: Vec<Vec<NodeId>>,
    34	    incoming: Vec<Vec<NodeId>>,
    35	}
    36	
    37	impl AdjacencyVectors {
    38	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    39	        Self::default()
    40	    }
    41	
    42	    pub fn node_count(&self) -> usize {
    43	        debug_assert_eq!(self.outgoing.len(), self.incoming.len());
    44	        self.outgoing.len()
    45	    }
    46	
    47	    /// Reserve adjacency slots for a newly-allocated node. The `Graph` calls this once per
    48	    /// `add_*_node` so adjacency lookups by `NodeId` are always in bounds.
    49	    pub fn push_node(&mut self) {
    50	        self.outgoing.push(Vec::new());
    51	        self.incoming.push(Vec::new());
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    /// Add a directed edge `from -> to` ("from depends on to"). Updates both adjacency
    55	    /// vectors so reverse lookups (incoming) are O(1) after construction.
    56	    ///
    57	    /// Panics if either id is out of bounds — the graph constructor is responsible for
    58	    /// calling `push_node()` before any edge that references the new id.
    59	    ///
    60	    /// No dedup: caller must not double-add. Phase 0 callers (range registration,
    61	    /// formula-region wiring) add each edge exactly once at construction time.
    62	    pub fn add_edge(&mut self, from: NodeId, to: NodeId) {
    63	        let from_idx = from.index();
    64	        let to_idx = to.index();
    65	        assert!(
    66	            from_idx < self.outgoing.len(),
    67	            "add_edge: from NodeId({from_idx}) out of bounds (have {} nodes)",
    68	            self.outgoing.len()
    69	        );
    70	        assert!(
    71	            to_idx < self.incoming.len(),
    72	            "add_edge: to NodeId({to_idx}) out of bounds (have {} nodes)",
    73	            self.incoming.len()
    74	        );
    75	        self.outgoing[from_idx].push(to);
    76	        self.incoming[to_idx].push(from);
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    /// All nodes that `node` depends on (`node -> *`). Borrowed slice — Phase 0 won't mutate
    80	    /// during a dirty walk.
    81	    pub fn outgoing(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
    82	        &self.outgoing[node.index()]
    83	    }
    84	
    85	    /// All nodes that depend on `node` (`* -> node`). Reverse adjacency for dirty propagation.
    86	    pub fn incoming(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
    87	        &self.incoming[node.index()]
    88	    }
    89	
    90	    pub fn outgoing_count(&self, node: NodeId) -> usize {
    91	        self.outgoing[node.index()].len()
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    pub fn incoming_count(&self, node: NodeId) -> usize {
    95	        self.incoming[node.index()].len()
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    /// Total edge count (sum of all outgoing lists). Used by the A5 acceptance assertion
    99	    /// that range-node prefix-SUM produces <2× formula count edges.
   100	    pub fn edge_count(&self) -> usize {
   101	        self.outgoing.iter().map(Vec::len).sum()
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    /// Drain every outgoing edge from `from` and return the prior target list.
   105	    /// Caller is responsible for removing the back-pointer in each target's
   106	    /// `incoming` slot (see `Graph::clear_outgoing` for the wrapper that does
   107	    /// both halves of the symmetric invariant).
   108	    ///
   109	    /// Phase 4 / W5-50: introduced for the per-formula edge revocation API
   110	    /// that fixes GAP-G-01 (rebind staleness). Append-only is no longer the
   111	    /// global invariant — the revocation path is the documented exception.
   112	    ///
   113	    /// Returns the drained `Vec<NodeId>` so the caller can iterate to clean
   114	    /// up `incoming`. The internal storage is left as an empty `Vec`, which
   115	    /// matches the layout of a brand-new node and preserves the
   116	    /// `outgoing.len() == node_count` invariant.
   117	    pub fn clear_outgoing(&mut self, from: NodeId) -> Vec<NodeId> {
   118	        let idx = from.index();
   119	        assert!(
   120	            idx < self.outgoing.len(),
   121	            "clear_outgoing: NodeId({idx}) out of bounds (have {} nodes)",
   122	            self.outgoing.len()
   123	        );
   124	        std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[idx])
   125	    }
   126	
   127	    /// Remove a single `from -> target` back-pointer from `incoming[target]`.
   128	    /// Used by `Graph::clear_outgoing` after `clear_outgoing` returns the
   129	    /// drained outgoing list — for each target, walk `incoming` once and
   130	    /// retain only entries that are NOT `from`.
   131	    ///
   132	    /// Phase 0's append-only contract had no caller-driven dedup, so a
   133	    /// `(from -> target)` edge added twice has two back-pointers in
   134	    /// `incoming[target]`. `retain` removes all of them, which is the
   135	    /// intended behavior for the per-formula revocation path: clearing
   136	    /// a formula's outgoing list must drop every back-pointer for that
   137	    /// formula, not just one.
   138	    pub fn remove_back_pointer(&mut self, target: NodeId, from: NodeId) {
   139	        let idx = target.index();
   140	        assert!(
   141	            idx < self.incoming.len(),
   142	            "remove_back_pointer: NodeId({idx}) out of bounds (have {} nodes)",
   143	            self.incoming.len()
   144	        );
   145	        self.incoming[idx].retain(|&n| n != from);
   146	    }
   147	}
   148

   157	        assert_eq!(a.edge_count(), 0);

 succeeded in 271ms:
     1	//! The top-level `Graph` — append-only node + adjacency container.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 0 scope (Week 3):
     4	//! - Insert nodes (Cell, Range, FormulaRegion). No delete; vertex IDs append-only.
     5	//! - Insert directed edges between existing nodes.
     6	//! - Revision counter bumped on every structural change (salsa-inspired) so callers can
     7	//!   detect "did the graph change since I last looked?" cheaply.
     8	//!
     9	//! Out of W3-1 scope (later commits):
    10	//! - W3-2 `dirty.rs` — per-chunk dirty bitmap + propagation walk.
    11	//! - W3-3 `topo.rs` — iterative Tarjan SCC scheduler (CORR-23).
    12	//! - W3-4 `fingerprint.rs` — Expr → u64 hash for formula-region memoization.
    13	//! - W3-5 `stripes.rs` — stripe-index registration per CORR-21.
    14	//! - W3-6 typed `#[cfg(test)]` accessors for the A4 assertions (CORR-24).
    15	//!
    16	//! ## Node + edge semantics (recap)
    17	//!
    18	//! Outgoing edge `u -> v` means **"u depends on v"** — recomputing u requires v's value.
    19	//! When v changes, the dirty walk traverses `incoming(v)` (the reverse direction) to mark
    20	//! dependents.
    21	//!
    22	//! ## Same-sheet only in Phase 0
    23	//!
    24	//! Every node carries a concrete `SheetId`. AST nodes (`Expr::CellRef`, `RangeRef::*`)
    25	//! may carry `Option<SheetId>` for sheet-qualification deferred to Phase 3+; the graph
    26	//! constructor (callers in `ql-exec` Week 4) is responsible for resolving the optional
    27	//! to a concrete sheet before calling `add_*_node`.
    28	
    29	use std::collections::HashMap;
    30	
    31	use ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef;
    32	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    33	
    34	use crate::edges::AdjacencyVectors;
    35	use crate::node::{CellNode, FormulaRegionNode, Node, NodeId, RangeNode};
    36	use crate::stats::GraphStats;
    37	use crate::stripes::{range_contains_rowcol, StripeIndex};
    38	
    39	/// The calc-graph container.
    40	///
    41	/// Append-only in Phase 0 (no removal). Borrows nothing — every node payload is owned by
    42	/// the graph (Range/FormulaRegion carry their full payload by value). This keeps lifetime
    43	/// management trivial at the cost of cloning small structs when callers query nodes; the
    44	/// payload sizes are bounded (Cell = 12 bytes; FormulaRegion ≈ 36 bytes) so the cloning
    45	/// cost is negligible compared to the hash/compare work in the dirty walk.
    46	///
    47	/// ## Range-dependency tracking (W3-5)
    48	///
    49	/// Two sidecar structures alongside nodes + edges:
    50	///
    51	/// - `stripes: StripeIndex` — per CORR-21, the per-row / per-column stripe map that lets
    52	///   `dependents_for_cell` answer in O(touched_stripes) instead of O(formula_count).
    53	/// - `formula_to_range_deps: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<RangeRef>>` — the canonical range list
    54	///   per formula node. Used by `dependents_for_cell` for the precision-check that drops
    55	///   stripe-match false positives (the stripe map is coarser than the actual range).
    56	///
    57	/// `register_range_dependency` populates BOTH atomically. Don't call them separately.
    58	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    59	pub struct Graph {
    60	    nodes: Vec<Node>,
    61	    edges: AdjacencyVectors,
    62	    revision: u64,
    63	    stripes: StripeIndex,
    64	    formula_to_range_deps: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<RangeRef>>,
    65	    stats: GraphStats,
    66	}
    67	
    68	/// Count of nodes by `Node` variant. Used by `graph-profile.json` export so a reader can
    69	/// see at a glance how the graph mass distributes across leaf cells, compressed ranges,
    70	/// dense formula regions, and (Phase 3+) spill anchors.
    71	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    72	pub struct NodeCountsByVariant {
    73	    pub cell: u64,
    74	    pub range: u64,
    75	    pub formula_region: u64,
    76	    pub spill: u64,
    77	}
    78	
    79	impl NodeCountsByVariant {
    80	    pub fn total(&self) -> u64 {
    81	        self.cell + self.range + self.formula_region + self.spill
    82	    }
    83	}
    84	
    85	impl Graph {
    86	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    87	        Self::default()
    88	    }
    89	
    90	    /// Total nodes added. Always equal to `revision_after_last_node_add` minus the count
    91	    /// of edge-only revisions — but Phase 0 doesn't distinguish, since both bump revision.
    92	    pub fn node_count(&self) -> usize {
    93	        debug_assert_eq!(self.nodes.len(), self.edges.node_count());
    94	        self.nodes.len()
    95	    }
    96	
    97	    pub fn edge_count(&self) -> usize {
    98	        self.edges.edge_count()
    99	    }
   100	
   101	    /// Monotonic version counter. Bumped on every structural change (node add, edge add).
   102	    /// Callers comparing graph state across operations rely on this — e.g. the dirty walk
   103	    /// re-runs only if `graph.revision() != self.last_walked_revision`.
   104	    pub fn revision(&self) -> u64 {
   105	        self.revision
   106	    }
   107	
   108	    /// Borrow a node by id. Panics on out-of-bounds — graph callers should never construct
   109	    /// `NodeId`s by hand; ids only come from `add_*_node` return values.
   110	    pub fn node(&self, id: NodeId) -> &Node {
   111	        &self.nodes[id.index()]
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    /// Iterate `node` -> dependencies (what `node` depends on).
   115	    pub fn outgoing(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
   116	        self.edges.outgoing(node)
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    /// Iterate dependents -> `node` (what depends on `node`). The dirty walk uses this.
   120	    pub fn incoming(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
   121	        self.edges.incoming(node)
   122	    }
   123	
   124	    fn push_node(&mut self, node: Node) -> NodeId {
   125	        let id = NodeId(
   126	            self.nodes
   127	                .len()
   128	                .try_into()
   129	                .expect("Graph::push_node: node count exceeds u32::MAX"),
   130	        );
   131	        self.nodes.push(node);
   132	        self.edges.push_node();
   133	        self.revision = self
   134	            .revision
   135	            .checked_add(1)
   136	            .expect("Graph::push_node: revision counter overflowed u64");
   137	        id
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    pub fn add_cell_node(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> NodeId {
   141	        self.push_node(Node::Cell(CellNode { sheet, row, col }))
   142	    }
   143	
   144	    pub fn add_range_node(&mut self, range: RangeNode) -> NodeId {
   145	        self.push_node(Node::Range(range))
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    pub fn add_formula_region_node(&mut self, region: FormulaRegionNode) -> NodeId {
   149	        self.push_node(Node::FormulaRegion(region))
   150	    }
   151	
   152	    /// Spill nodes are Phase 3+ — panic loudly if Phase 0 callers attempt construction.
   153	    /// Per the no-fallbacks rule: don't silently accept an unreachable variant.
   154	    pub fn add_spill_node(&mut self, _anchor: NodeId, _shape: (RowId, ColId)) -> NodeId {
   155	        panic!(
   156	            "Graph::add_spill_node: Phase 3+ feature (dynamic array spill anchors); not \
   157	             constructed in Phase 0. Tracked via the shape-locked Node::Spill variant."
   158	        );
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    /// Add a directed edge `from -> to` ("from depends on to"). Both ids must already exist.
   162	    /// Caller is responsible for not double-adding (Phase 0 has no dedup; see edges.rs).
   163	    pub fn add_edge(&mut self, from: NodeId, to: NodeId) {
   164	        self.edges.add_edge(from, to);
   165	        self.revision = self
   166	            .revision
   167	            .checked_add(1)
   168	            .expect("Graph::add_edge: revision counter overflowed u64");
   169	    }
   170	
   171	    /// Register that `formula_node` depends on cells in `range`. Atomically updates BOTH
   172	    /// the stripe index AND `formula_to_range_deps`. Callers should invoke this once per
   173	    /// range reference in a formula's AST (a formula like `=SUM(A:A) + COUNT(B:B)` calls
   174	    /// register_range_dependency twice).
   175	    ///
   176	    /// The Phase 0 W3-5 callers (Week 4 `ql-exec` binder) own:
   177	    /// 1. Calling `add_cell_node` / `add_formula_region_node` for the formula's owning vertex.
   178	    /// 2. Calling `register_range_dependency` for each range the formula references.
   179	    /// 3. Optionally adding explicit edges via `add_edge` for direct cell-to-cell deps.
   180	    pub fn register_range_dependency(
   181	        &mut self,
   182	        formula_node: NodeId,
   183	        range: RangeRef,
   184	        sheet_id: SheetId,
   185	    ) {
   186	        assert!(
   187	            formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   188	            "Graph::register_range_dependency: formula_node {} out of bounds",
   189	            formula_node.index()
   190	        );
   191	        let inserts_before = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   192	        self.stripes.register(formula_node, &range, sheet_id);
   193	        let inserts_after = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   194	        // `stripe_inserts` counts the NEW insertions this call produced. HashSet dedup
   195	        // means a re-registration of the same (formula_node, range) might be a no-op,
   196	        // in which case the delta is zero — matches Formualizer's instrumentation
   197	        // semantics (`engine/graph/range_deps.rs:78-86`).
   198	        self.stats.stripe_inserts = self
   199	            .stats
   200	            .stripe_inserts
   201	            .checked_add((inserts_after - inserts_before) as u64)
   202	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: stripe_inserts overflowed u64");
   203	        self.formula_to_range_deps
   204	            .entry(formula_node)
   205	            .or_default()
   206	            .push(range);
   207	        self.revision = self
   208	            .revision
   209	            .checked_add(1)
   210	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: revision counter overflowed u64");
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    /// Borrow the instrumentation counters. Used by tests + the W3-7 graph-profile.json
   214	    /// export (OG-06 acceptance).
   215	    pub fn stats(&self) -> GraphStats {
   216	        self.stats
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure.** Revoke every outgoing edge from
   220	    /// `node`, AND the symmetric back-pointer in each former target's
   221	    /// `incoming` slot. Used by `CalcgraphSession::extract_and_register_deps`
   222	    /// on re-bind so a formula whose direct cell deps changed
   223	    /// (e.g. `=A1` → `=1`) doesn't leave stale forward edges that would
   224	    /// confuse the Tarjan scheduler into seeing a false `#CIRC!` cycle.
   225	    ///
   226	    /// Bumps `revision` exactly once per call (not per edge), matching
   227	    /// the existing semantics of `add_edge` / `register_range_dependency`.
   228	    /// A node with no outgoing edges is a no-op.
   229	    ///
   230	    /// This is the documented exception to Phase 0's append-only invariant.
   231	    /// See `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
   232	    pub fn clear_outgoing(&mut self, node: NodeId) {
   233	        assert!(
   234	            node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   235	            "Graph::clear_outgoing: node {} out of bounds",
   236	            node.index()
   237	        );
   238	        let drained = self.edges.clear_outgoing(node);
   239	        for target in drained {
   240	            self.edges.remove_back_pointer(target, node);
   241	        }
   242	        self.revision = self
   243	            .revision
   244	            .checked_add(1)
   245	            .expect("Graph::clear_outgoing: revision counter overflowed u64");
   246	    }
   247	
   248	    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure (range side).** Revoke every range-dep
   249	    /// registration held by `formula_node`. Clears both
   250	    /// `formula_to_range_deps[formula_node]` and every stripe membership
   251	    /// in `StripeIndex` (via `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula`). After
   252	    /// this call, writes to cells in the OLD ranges no longer dirty
   253	    /// `formula_node` via the precision-check path.
   254	    ///
   255	    /// Bumps `revision` once per call. Idempotent — a formula with no
   256	    /// range deps is a no-op.
   257	    ///
   258	    /// Companion to `clear_outgoing`: the session-side `extract_and_register_deps`
   259	    /// calls both before re-extracting the formula's new dep set.
   260	    pub fn clear_range_deps_for_formula(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   261	        assert!(
   262	            formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   263	            "Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula: node {} out of bounds",
   264	            formula_node.index()
   265	        );
   266	        self.formula_to_range_deps.remove(&formula_node);
   267	        self.stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node);
   268	        self.revision = self
   269	            .revision
   270	            .checked_add(1)
   271	            .expect("Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula: revision counter overflowed u64");
   272	    }
   273	
   274	    /// Bump `chunked_reduce_chunks_processed` by `n`. Phase 0 doesn't increment this
   275	    /// from inside ql-calcgraph; Week 4 ql-exec calls this on each chunk it processes
   276	    /// during a `SUM`/`COUNT`/etc. reduction. Locking the API in W3-6 means Week 4 just
   277	    /// wires the increments without a graph-shape change.
   278	    pub fn record_chunked_reduce(&mut self, chunks_processed: u64) {
   279	        self.stats.chunked_reduce_chunks_processed = self
   280	            .stats
   281	            .chunked_reduce_chunks_processed
   282	            .checked_add(chunks_processed)
   283	            .expect("Graph::record_chunked_reduce: counter overflowed u64");
   284	    }
   285	
   286	    /// True dependents of a cell write at `(sheet, row, col)`. Two-pass:
   287	    /// 1. Stripe lookup → candidate formula nodes (CORR-21 coarse-grained).
   288	    /// 2. Precision filter against each candidate's `formula_to_range_deps` — drops
   289	    ///    candidates whose actual range doesn't contain the cell.
   290	    ///
   291	    /// Returns the precise dependent set, **sorted ascending by `NodeId`** so callers
   292	    /// receive deterministic ordering across runs. Audit H2 (2026-05-12): the previous
   293	    /// implementation collected from a `HashSet` which leaked Rust's randomized hash
   294	    /// state — two identical edits on the same workbook would produce different
   295	    /// downstream scheduling order, silently breaking replay determinism.
   296	    pub fn dependents_for_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Vec<NodeId> {
   297	        let candidates = self.stripes.candidates_for_cell(sheet, row, col);
   298	        let mut out: Vec<NodeId> = candidates
   299	            .into_iter()
   300	            .filter(|formula_id| {
   301	                self.formula_to_range_deps
   302	                    .get(formula_id)
   303	                    .is_some_and(|ranges| ranges.iter().any(|r| range_contains_rowcol(r, row, col)))
   304	            })
   305	            .collect();
   306	        out.sort();
   307	        out
   308	    }
   309	
   310	    /// Total registered range-dependencies across all formula nodes. Counts each
   311	    /// `register_range_dependency` call. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
   312	    pub fn range_dependency_count(&self) -> usize {
   313	        self.formula_to_range_deps.values().map(Vec::len).sum()
   314	    }
   315	
   316	    /// W5-50 — public accessor for the per-formula range-dep list. Returns
   317	    /// an empty slice if the formula has no range deps (or doesn't exist).
   318	    /// The runtime side uses this to build the supplemental adjacency for
   319	    /// `topo::schedule_with_supplemental` (GAP-G-03 closure).
   320	    pub fn range_deps_for(&self, formula_node: NodeId) -> &[RangeRef] {
   321	        self.formula_to_range_deps
   322	            .get(&formula_node)
   323	            .map(Vec::as_slice)
   324	            .unwrap_or(&[])
   325	    }
   326	
   327	    /// W5-50 — return the sheet a `RangeRef` resolves against. `None`
   328	    /// means "use the formula's owning sheet" (the binder convention).
   329	    /// Helper so runtime code doesn't have to match on `RangeRef`
   330	    /// variants just to pull the sheet field.
   331	    pub fn range_ref_sheet(range: &RangeRef) -> Option<SheetId> {
   332	        match range {
   333	            RangeRef::Cells { sheet, .. }
   334	            | RangeRef::WholeColumn { sheet, .. }
   335	            | RangeRef::WholeRow { sheet, .. } => *sheet,
   336	        }
   337	    }
   338	
   339	    /// Count nodes by variant. Used by W3-7 graph-profile.json export (OG-06) so a
   340	    /// reader can quickly attribute total graph mass to the right node kind. O(n) over
   341	    /// the node array; not on any hot path.
   342	    pub fn node_counts_by_variant(&self) -> NodeCountsByVariant {
   343	        let mut counts = NodeCountsByVariant::default();
   344	        for n in &self.nodes {
   345	            match n {
   346	                Node::Cell(_) => counts.cell += 1,
   347	                Node::Range(_) => counts.range += 1,
   348	                Node::FormulaRegion(_) => counts.formula_region += 1,
   349	                Node::Spill(_) => counts.spill += 1,
   350	            }
   351	        }
   352	        counts
   353	    }
   354	
   355	    /// Borrow the stripe index — used by ql-profile to dump stripe-key cardinality.
   356	    /// Read-only public access; mutation goes through `register_range_dependency`.
   357	    pub fn stripe_index(&self) -> &StripeIndex {
   358	        &self.stripes
   359	    }
   360	
   361	    /// Test-only typed accessor for A4 assertions (CORR-24).
   362	    #[cfg(test)]
   363	    pub(crate) fn stripes(&self) -> &StripeIndex {
   364	        &self.stripes
   365	    }
   366	
   367	    /// Test-only typed accessor for A4 assertions (CORR-24).
   368	    #[cfg(test)]
   369	    pub(crate) fn formula_to_range_deps(&self) -> &HashMap<NodeId, Vec<RangeRef>> {
   370	        &self.formula_to_range_deps
   371	    }
   372	}

   374	#[cfg(test)]
   375	mod tests {
   376	    use super::*;
   377	    use ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef;
   378	
   379	    #[test]
   380	    fn empty_graph_has_zero_state() {
   381	        let g = Graph::new();
   382	        assert_eq!(g.node_count(), 0);
   383	        assert_eq!(g.edge_count(), 0);
   384	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), 0);
   385	    }
   386	
   387	    #[test]
   388	    fn add_cell_node_bumps_revision_and_returns_sequential_ids() {
   389	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   390	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   391	        assert_eq!(a, NodeId(0));
   392	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), 1);
   393	        assert_eq!(g.node_count(), 1);
   394	
   395	        let b = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   396	        assert_eq!(b, NodeId(1));
   397	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), 2);
   398	        assert_eq!(g.node_count(), 2);
   399	    }
   400	
   401	    #[test]
   402	    fn add_range_node_returns_correct_variant() {
   403	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   404	        let id = g.add_range_node(RangeNode {
   405	            sheet: 0,
   406	            range: RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   407	                sheet: None,
   408	                start_col: 0,
   409	                end_col: 0,
   410	                abs_start: false,
   411	                abs_end: false,
   412	            },
   413	        });
   414	        match g.node(id) {
   415	            Node::Range(rn) => assert!(matches!(
   416	                rn.range,
   417	                RangeRef::WholeColumn { start_col: 0, .. }
   418	            )),
   419	            _ => panic!("expected Range variant"),
   420	        }
   421	    }
   422	
   423	    #[test]
   424	    fn add_formula_region_node_carries_fingerprint() {
   425	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   426	        let id = g.add_formula_region_node(FormulaRegionNode {
   427	            sheet: 0,
   428	            target_start_row: 0,
   429	            target_start_col: 1,
   430	            target_end_row: 99,
   431	            target_end_col: 1,
   432	            formula_fingerprint: 0xCAFE_F00D_DEAD_BEEF,
   433	            chunk_count: 1,
   434	        });
   435	        match g.node(id) {
   436	            Node::FormulaRegion(frn) => {
   437	                assert_eq!(frn.formula_fingerprint, 0xCAFE_F00D_DEAD_BEEF);
   438	                assert_eq!(frn.chunk_count, 1);
   439	            }
   440	            _ => panic!("expected FormulaRegion variant"),
   441	        }
   442	    }
   443	
   444	    #[test]
   445	    fn add_edge_bumps_revision_and_populates_adjacency() {
   446	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   447	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   448	        let b = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
   449	        let rev_before = g.revision();
   450	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   451	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), rev_before + 1);
   452	        assert_eq!(g.edge_count(), 1);
   453	        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(a), &[b]);
   454	        assert_eq!(g.incoming(b), &[a]);
   455	        assert!(g.outgoing(b).is_empty());
   456	        assert!(g.incoming(a).is_empty());
   457	    }
   458	
   459	    #[test]
   460	    fn revision_only_bumps_on_structural_change() {
   461	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   462	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   463	        let rev = g.revision();
   464	        // Queries don't bump revision.
   465	        let _ = g.node(a);
   466	        let _ = g.node_count();
   467	        let _ = g.outgoing(a);
   468	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), rev);
   469	    }
   470	
   471	    #[test]
   472	    fn diamond_graph() {
   473	        // SUM(A1, A2) where A1 = 1, A2 = 2 — formula B1 depends on both.
   474	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   475	        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   476	        let a2 = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
   477	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   478	        g.add_edge(b1, a1);
   479	        g.add_edge(b1, a2);
   480	        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(b1).len(), 2);
   481	        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a1), &[b1]);
   482	        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a2), &[b1]);
   483	    }
   484	
   485	    #[test]
   486	    #[should_panic(expected = "Phase 3+ feature")]
   487	    fn add_spill_node_panics() {
   488	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   489	        let anchor = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   490	        g.add_spill_node(anchor, (3, 3));
   491	    }
   492	
   493	    #[test]
   494	    fn many_cell_nodes_get_sequential_ids() {
   495	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   496	        let mut ids = Vec::new();
   497	        for r in 0..100 {
   498	            ids.push(g.add_cell_node(0, r, 0));
   499	        }
   500	        for (i, id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
   501	            assert_eq!(*id, NodeId(i as u32));
   502	        }
   503	        assert_eq!(g.node_count(), 100);
   504	    }
   505	
   506	    #[test]
   507	    fn out_of_bounds_node_lookup_panics() {
   508	        let g = Graph::new();
   509	        let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(|| {
   510	            let _ = g.node(NodeId(0));
   511	        });
   512	        assert!(result.is_err());
   513	    }
   514	
   515	    #[test]
   516	    fn out_of_bounds_add_edge_panics() {
   517	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   518	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   519	        let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
   520	            // Wrap in a fresh graph since `g` is borrowed by AssertUnwindSafe
   521	            let mut g2 = Graph::new();
   522	            g2.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   523	            g2.add_edge(a, NodeId(5));
   524	        }));
   525	        assert!(result.is_err());
   526	    }
   527	
   528	    #[test]
   529	    fn edge_order_preserved() {
   530	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   531	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   532	        let b = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
   533	        let c = g.add_cell_node(0, 2, 0);
   534	        let d = g.add_cell_node(0, 3, 0);
   535	        // a depends on b, c, d in that order
   536	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   537	        g.add_edge(a, c);
   538	        g.add_edge(a, d);
   539	        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(a), &[b, c, d]);
   540	    }

   565	    fn dependents_for_cell_precision_filters_false_positives() {
   566	        // SUM(A1:A10) at B1, SUM(A:A) at C1. A write to A500 should match C1 but NOT
   567	        // B1 (whose range only covers A1:A10).
   568	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   569	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   570	        let c1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 2);
   571	        let bounded = RangeRef::Cells {
   572	            sheet: None,
   573	            start_col: 0,
   574	            start_row: 0,
   575	            end_col: 0,
   576	            end_row: 9,
   577	            abs_start_col: false,
   578	            abs_start_row: false,
   579	            abs_end_col: false,
   580	            abs_end_row: false,
   581	        };
   582	        let whole_col = RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   583	            sheet: None,
   584	            start_col: 0,
   585	            end_col: 0,
   586	            abs_start: false,
   587	            abs_end: false,
   588	        };
   589	        g.register_range_dependency(b1, bounded, 0);
   590	        g.register_range_dependency(c1, whole_col, 0);
   591	
   592	        // Both registered under Column 0 stripe.
   593	        assert_eq!(g.stripes().stripe_count(), 1);
   594	        assert_eq!(g.stripes().total_insertions(), 2);
   595	
   596	        // Write to A5 (in B1's range): both match. Output is sorted (audit H2 fix).
   597	        let deps_at_5 = g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0);
   598	        let mut expected: Vec<NodeId> = [b1, c1].to_vec();
   599	        expected.sort();
   600	        assert_eq!(deps_at_5, expected);
   601	        // Verify the returned Vec is sorted (H2 contract).
   602	        assert!(deps_at_5.windows(2).all(|w| w[0] <= w[1]));
   603	
   604	        // Write to A500 (outside B1's range, inside C1's): only C1 matches.
   605	        assert_eq!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 500, 0), vec![c1]);
   606	    }
