   608	    /// Audit H2 (2026-05-12): `dependents_for_cell` must return a Vec sorted by NodeId
   609	    /// for determinism. Previously the function collected from a HashSet which leaked
   610	    /// Rust's randomized hash state to callers, silently breaking replay determinism.
   611	    #[test]
   612	    fn dependents_for_cell_returns_sorted_vec() {
   613	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   614	        // 50 formulas in random column order — internal HashSet iteration would yield
   615	        // them in an arbitrary order. dependents_for_cell must sort before returning.
   616	        let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(50);
   617	        for col in 0..50 {
   618	            let id = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, col + 1);
   619	            ids.push(id);
   620	            g.register_range_dependency(
   621	                id,
   622	                RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   623	                    sheet: None,
   624	                    start_col: 0,
   625	                    end_col: 0,
   626	                    abs_start: false,
   627	                    abs_end: false,
   628	                },
   629	                0,
   630	            );
   631	        }
   632	        let deps = g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0);
   633	        assert_eq!(deps.len(), 50);
   634	        // Strictly increasing by NodeId.
   635	        assert!(deps.windows(2).all(|w| w[0] < w[1]));
   636	        // And: same result on the second call (deterministic).
   637	        assert_eq!(deps, g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0));
   638	    }

   728	    #[test]
   729	    fn clear_outgoing_drops_edges_and_back_pointers() {
   730	        // B1 depends on A1 and A2.
   731	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   732	        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   733	        let a2 = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
   734	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   735	        g.add_edge(b1, a1);
   736	        g.add_edge(b1, a2);
   737	        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(b1), &[a1, a2]);
   738	        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a1), &[b1]);
   739	        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a2), &[b1]);
   740	        let rev_before = g.revision();
   741	
   742	        g.clear_outgoing(b1);
   743	
   744	        assert!(g.outgoing(b1).is_empty());
   745	        assert!(g.incoming(a1).is_empty(), "back-pointer dropped");
   746	        assert!(g.incoming(a2).is_empty(), "back-pointer dropped");
   747	        assert_eq!(g.revision(), rev_before + 1, "single revision bump");
   748	    }

   817	        assert!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 1).is_empty());

   847	    #[test]
   848	    fn rebind_workflow_no_stale_false_circ() {
   849	        // GAP-G-01 / H3 acceptance: clear_outgoing breaks the false-cycle
   850	        // chain after rebind.
   851	        //   1. B1 = "=A1"           → add_edge(B1, A1)
   852	        //   2. B1 = "=1" (rebind)   → clear_outgoing(B1)
   853	        //   3. A1 = "=B1"           → add_edge(A1, B1)
   854	        // Tarjan over {A1, B1} must NOT report a cycle.
   855	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   856	        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   857	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   858	        g.add_edge(b1, a1); // step 1
   859	        g.clear_outgoing(b1); // step 2 (rebind to constant)
   860	        g.add_edge(a1, b1); // step 3
   861	
   862	        let sched = crate::topo::schedule(&g, &[a1, b1]);
   863	        assert!(sched.cycled.is_empty(), "no false #CIRC! after revocation");
   864	        // Dependency-first order: B1 then A1 (A1 depends on B1).
   865	        assert_eq!(sched.sorted, vec![b1, a1]);
   866	    }

   869	    fn rebind_workflow_no_stale_stripe_dirty() {
   870	        // GAP-G-01 / H4 acceptance: clear_range_deps_for_formula breaks
   871	        // the stale-dirty chain after range rebind.
   872	        //   1. B1 = "=SUM(A:A)"    → register_range_dependency(B1, A:A)
   873	        //   2. B1 = "=SUM(B:B)" (rebind)
   874	        //        → clear_range_deps_for_formula(B1)
   875	        //        → register_range_dependency(B1, B:B)
   876	        //   3. write A5            → dependents_for_cell(A, 5) must NOT contain B1
   877	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   878	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
   879	        let col_a = RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   880	            sheet: None,
   881	            start_col: 0,
   882	            end_col: 0,
   883	            abs_start: false,
   884	            abs_end: false,
   885	        };
   886	        let col_b = RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   887	            sheet: None,
   888	            start_col: 1,
   889	            end_col: 1,
   890	            abs_start: false,
   891	            abs_end: false,
   892	        };
   893	        g.register_range_dependency(b1, col_a, 0); // step 1
   894	        g.clear_range_deps_for_formula(b1); // step 2a
   895	        g.register_range_dependency(b1, col_b, 0); // step 2b
   896	
   897	        // step 3: A5 no longer dirties B1.
   898	        assert!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0).is_empty());
   899	        // B5 still dirties B1 (new range).

   903	    #[test]
   904	    fn determinism_rebind_same_plan_same_edges() {
   905	        // Rebinding to the same target list (same plan) must yield the
   906	        // same outgoing edge order — Tarjan's emission order depends on
   907	        // adjacency order, so this is observable downstream.
   908	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   909	        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   910	        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
   911	        let c1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 2, 0);
   912	        let f = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 3);
   913	
   914	        // Initial bind.
   915	        g.add_edge(f, a1);
   916	        g.add_edge(f, b1);
   917	        g.add_edge(f, c1);
   918	        let initial: Vec<_> = g.outgoing(f).to_vec();
   919	
   920	        // Rebind to identical targets.
   921	        g.clear_outgoing(f);
   922	        g.add_edge(f, a1);
   923	        g.add_edge(f, b1);
   924	        g.add_edge(f, c1);
   925	        let rebound: Vec<_> = g.outgoing(f).to_vec();
   926	
   927	        assert_eq!(initial, rebound, "edge order identical post-rebind");
   928	    }

 succeeded in 267ms:
     1	//! Topological scheduler — iterative Tarjan SCC over a dirty subset.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per CORR-23 (Week 3 Day 0 deep-read finding): both Formualizer (`engine/scheduler.rs:
     4	//! 88-239`) and HyperFormula (`TopSort.ts:18-92`) use iterative Tarjan SCC rather than
     5	//! Kahn's algorithm for topological scheduling. Reasons:
     6	//!
     7	//! 1. **Cycle detection in the same pass.** Tarjan partitions nodes into SCCs; SCCs of
     8	//!    size 1 with no self-loop are acyclic (go to `sorted`), everything else is cyclic
     9	//!    (goes to `cycled`). Kahn would need a separate "did all dirty nodes emit?" check
    10	//!    after the toposort to identify cycle membership.
    11	//!
    12	//! 2. **Direct API for the Phase 0 cycle policy.** The spec's Phase 0 minimum: cycled
    13	//!    cells become `CellError(Cycle)`; non-cycled cells evaluate in topological order.
    14	//!    The `(sorted, cycled)` partition is exactly what the evaluator wants.
    15	//!
    16	//! 3. **Iterative implementation handles deep graphs.** A 10K-deep dependency chain
    17	//!    would blow the default 8 MB stack with a recursive Tarjan. HyperFormula made the
    18	//!    same choice for JS call-stack reasons; ours is for arbitrary-depth Rust workbooks.
    19	//!
    20	//! 4. **Phase 0 minimum is non-iteration**: cycles are terminal errors, not states to
    21	//!    converge. Full iteration (with a `max_iterations` config) lands Phase 4+ when the
    22	//!    function library includes goal-seek / iterative-solve UDFs.
    23	//!
    24	//! ## Algorithm shape
    25	//!
    26	//! Standard iterative Tarjan with two stacks:
    27	//! - `dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)>` — explicit DFS frame: (node, next-child-index).
    28	//!   Replaces the recursion stack of the textbook algorithm.
    29	//! - `scc_stack: Vec<NodeId>` — the Tarjan SCC stack of "currently-being-explored" nodes
    30	//!   that may belong to the same SCC as the current root.
    31	//!
    32	//! Per-node state in `FxHashMap`-equivalent (we use `std::HashMap` to avoid a new dep):
    33	//! - `indices[v]: u32` — DFS discovery order; assigned on first visit.
    34	//! - `lowlinks[v]: u32` — smallest index reachable from v's subtree.
    35	//! - `on_scc_stack[v]: bool` — is v currently on `scc_stack`?
    36	//!
    37	//! When a node's lowlink equals its index after all children are processed, it's the
    38	//! root of an SCC. We pop the SCC stack down to that node.
    39	//!
    40	//! ## Edge filtering
    41	//!
    42	//! The scheduler operates ON the dirty subset only. Edges to non-dirty nodes are skipped
    43	//! (those nodes aren't being re-evaluated; their current values are read as-is). A
    44	//! `dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` membership check filters `graph.outgoing(v)`.
    45	//!
    46	//! ## Output ordering
    47	//!
    48	//! Tarjan emits SCCs in **reverse topological order of the condensation**. For our
    49	//! graph orientation (edge `u → v` means "u depends on v"), this is exactly dependency-
    50	//! first order: when we collect `sorted` by appending SCCs as Tarjan finds them, the
    51	//! result is `[dependencies, ..., dependents]`. Callers evaluate `sorted[0]` first.
    52	
    53	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    54	
    55	use crate::graph::Graph;
    56	use crate::node::NodeId;
    57	
    58	/// The output of a scheduling pass.
    59	///
    60	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first order: evaluate `sorted[0]` first, then `sorted[1]`,
    61	/// etc. `cycled` is unordered — every cycled node should be assigned a cycle error.
    62	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    63	pub struct Schedule {
    64	    pub sorted: Vec<NodeId>,
    65	    pub cycled: Vec<NodeId>,
    66	}
    67	
    68	impl Schedule {
    69	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    70	        self.sorted.is_empty() && self.cycled.is_empty()
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    pub fn total_count(&self) -> usize {
    74	        self.sorted.len() + self.cycled.len()
    75	    }
    76	}
    77	
    78	/// Schedule the given dirty subset for evaluation. Returns `(sorted, cycled)` where
    79	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first topological order and `cycled` contains all nodes in
    80	/// non-trivial strongly connected components (size > 1) plus all self-loops (size 1 + an
    81	/// edge to self).
    82	///
    83	/// Edges to nodes outside `dirty_subset` are not traversed — those nodes aren't being
    84	/// re-evaluated, just read.
    85	///
    86	/// Duplicate entries in `dirty_subset` are tolerated (deduped internally via a HashSet).
    87	pub fn schedule(graph: &Graph, dirty_subset: &[NodeId]) -> Schedule {
    88	    let empty: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
    89	    schedule_with_supplemental(graph, dirty_subset, &empty)
    90	}
    91	
    92	/// **W5-50 — GAP-G-03 closure (small Option C).** Schedule with caller-
    93	/// provided supplemental adjacency layered on top of `graph.outgoing(v)`.
    94	/// Used by `CalcgraphSession::schedule_dirty` to inject range-induced
    95	/// edges that the Phase 0 graph itself doesn't carry: for each dirty
    96	/// formula `F` with range deps and a dirty formula `G` inside one of
    97	/// `F`'s ranges, the caller pushes a temp edge `F → G` into
    98	/// `supplemental[F]` so Tarjan can see the range-induced ordering /
    99	/// cycle.
   100	///
   101	/// The supplemental map is read-only during the scheduler call.
   102	/// Entries are appended to the corresponding `graph.outgoing(v)` slice
   103	/// — they don't replace real edges. Edges to non-dirty nodes are still
   104	/// filtered out (the existing dirty-subset contract holds).
   105	///
   106	/// Self-loops via supplemental ARE recognized as cycles (e.g.
   107	/// `A1 = SUM(A1:A1)` builds a supplemental `A1 → A1` edge; this single
   108	/// dirty formula gets emitted into `cycled`).
   109	///
   110	/// Backward compatibility: `schedule(graph, subset)` is now a thin
   111	/// wrapper that passes an empty supplemental map.
   112	pub fn schedule_with_supplemental(
   113	    graph: &Graph,
   114	    dirty_subset: &[NodeId],
   115	    supplemental: &HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>,
   116	) -> Schedule {
   117	    let dirty: HashSet<NodeId> = dirty_subset.iter().copied().collect();
   118	    if dirty.is_empty() {
   119	        return Schedule::default();
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    let mut indices: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   123	    let mut lowlinks: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   124	    let mut on_scc_stack: HashMap<NodeId, bool> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   125	    let mut scc_stack: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   126	    let mut next_index: u32 = 0;
   127	
   128	    let mut sorted: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   129	    let mut cycled: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   130	
   131	    // Visit every node in the dirty subset. Iteration order is from the input slice (not
   132	    // the HashSet's randomized order) so callers get deterministic schedules from
   133	    // deterministic inputs. Deduplicated via `indices.contains_key`.
   134	    for &seed in dirty_subset {
   135	        if indices.contains_key(&seed) {
   136	            continue;
   137	        }
   138	        tarjan_dfs(
   139	            seed,
   140	            graph,
   141	            supplemental,
   142	            &dirty,
   143	            &mut indices,
   144	            &mut lowlinks,
   145	            &mut on_scc_stack,
   146	            &mut scc_stack,
   147	            &mut next_index,
   148	            &mut sorted,
   149	            &mut cycled,
   150	        );
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    Schedule { sorted, cycled }
   154	}
   155	
   156	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   157	fn tarjan_dfs(
   158	    seed: NodeId,
   159	    graph: &Graph,
   160	    supplemental: &HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>,
   161	    dirty: &HashSet<NodeId>,
   162	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   163	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   164	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   165	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   166	    next_index: &mut u32,
   167	    sorted: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   168	    cycled: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   169	) {
   170	    // Explicit DFS frame: (node, child index in outgoing(node) — within-dirty filter).
   171	    // We compute the child slice once at first visit + iterate it index-by-index across
   172	    // frames. Filtering "is this child in dirty?" happens at child-visit time so we don't
   173	    // pre-allocate filtered child vectors per node (the hot path under no-cycles is one
   174	    // outgoing-slice scan per node, period).
   175	    //
   176	    // W5-50: the "child slice" is now `graph.outgoing(v)` concatenated with
   177	    // `supplemental.get(&v)` (if any). Indices < outgoing.len() refer to the real graph;
   178	    // indices >= outgoing.len() refer to the supplemental list. Constant-time per child.
   179	    let mut dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)> = Vec::new();
   180	
   181	    // First visit of seed.
   182	    visit_first(seed, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   183	    dfs_stack.push((seed, 0));
   184	
   185	    while let Some(&(v, child_idx)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   186	        let outgoing = graph.outgoing(v);
   187	        let supp: &[NodeId] = supplemental.get(&v).map(|v| v.as_slice()).unwrap_or(&[]);
   188	        let total_children = outgoing.len() + supp.len();
   189	
   190	        // Find the next not-yet-considered child that's in the dirty subset.
   191	        let mut next_child: Option<NodeId> = None;
   192	        let mut new_child_idx = child_idx;
   193	        while new_child_idx < total_children {
   194	            let candidate = if new_child_idx < outgoing.len() {
   195	                outgoing[new_child_idx]
   196	            } else {
   197	                supp[new_child_idx - outgoing.len()]
   198	            };
   199	            new_child_idx += 1;
   200	            if dirty.contains(&candidate) {
   201	                next_child = Some(candidate);
   202	                break;
   203	            }
   204	        }
   205	
   206	        // Update this frame's child cursor regardless of whether we recurse or finish.
   207	        let frame_idx = dfs_stack.len() - 1;
   208	        dfs_stack[frame_idx].1 = new_child_idx;
   209	
   210	        if let Some(w) = next_child {
   211	            if !indices.contains_key(&w) {
   212	                // Unvisited dirty child — recurse.
   213	                visit_first(w, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   214	                dfs_stack.push((w, 0));
   215	            } else if *on_scc_stack
   216	                .get(&w)
   217	                .expect("topo invariant: every key in `indices` must also be in `on_scc_stack` — visit_first populates both atomically")
   218	            {
   219	                // Phase 2A.7 audit M13: switched from `unwrap_or(&false)` to `.expect(...)`.
   220	                // The prior fallback silently treated "missing key" as "not on stack",
   221	                // conflating "not yet visited" with "explicitly off-stack" — masking a
   222	                // Tarjan invariant violation. Per no-fallbacks rule, an invariant
   223	                // violation must panic loudly, not produce subtly-wrong SCC results.
   224	                //
   225	                // Back-edge or cross-edge to a node currently on the SCC stack — update
   226	                // v's lowlink with w's index.
   227	                let w_idx = indices[&w];
   228	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   229	                if w_idx < v_low {
   230	                    lowlinks.insert(v, w_idx);
   231	                }
   232	            }
   233	            // Else: w is in a completed SCC; ignore the cross-edge.
   234	        } else {
   235	            // All children processed — post-order completion.
   236	            dfs_stack.pop();
   237	
   238	            // If v is the root of an SCC, pop it off the SCC stack.
   239	            if lowlinks[&v] == indices[&v] {
   240	                let mut scc: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   241	                loop {
   242	                    let w = scc_stack
   243	                        .pop()
   244	                        .expect("scc_stack drained mid-pop — algorithm invariant violated");
   245	                    on_scc_stack.insert(w, false);
   246	                    scc.push(w);
   247	                    if w == v {
   248	                        break;
   249	                    }
   250	                }
   251	
   252	                // Classify the SCC. Self-loops can come from the real graph
   253	                // OR the supplemental map — both count as cycles.
   254	                let is_cycle = scc.len() > 1
   255	                    || (scc.len() == 1 && {
   256	                        let self_id = scc[0];
   257	                        let real_self_loop = graph
   258	                            .outgoing(self_id)
   259	                            .iter()
   260	                            .any(|&t| t == self_id && dirty.contains(&t));
   261	                        let supp_self_loop = supplemental
   262	                            .get(&self_id)
   263	                            .map(|s| s.iter().any(|&t| t == self_id && dirty.contains(&t)))
   264	                            .unwrap_or(false);
   265	                        real_self_loop || supp_self_loop
   266	                    });
   267	                if is_cycle {
   268	                    cycled.extend(scc);
   269	                } else {
   270	                    // Single acyclic node — append to sorted.
   271	                    sorted.extend(scc);
   272	                }
   273	            }
   274	
   275	            // Propagate v's lowlink up to its parent (post-order).
   276	            if let Some(&(parent, _)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   277	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   278	                let p_low = lowlinks[&parent];
   279	                if v_low < p_low {
   280	                    lowlinks.insert(parent, v_low);
   281	                }
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	    }
   285	}
   286	
   287	fn visit_first(
   288	    v: NodeId,
   289	    next_index: &mut u32,
   290	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   291	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   292	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   293	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   294	) {
   295	    let idx = *next_index;
   296	    *next_index = next_index
   297	        .checked_add(1)
   298	        .expect("Tarjan next_index overflow u32 — graphs > 4.3B nodes are not supported");
   299	    indices.insert(v, idx);
   300	    lowlinks.insert(v, idx);
   301	    scc_stack.push(v);
   302	    on_scc_stack.insert(v, true);
   303	}

   309	    /// Helper: ids of `n` cell nodes in row 0, distinct columns.
   310	    fn n_cells(g: &mut Graph, n: u32) -> Vec<NodeId> {
   311	        (0..n).map(|c| g.add_cell_node(0, 0, c)).collect()
   312	    }

   314	    #[test]
   315	    fn empty_dirty_returns_empty_schedule() {
   316	        let g = Graph::new();
   317	        let s = schedule(&g, &[]);
   318	        assert!(s.is_empty());
   319	        assert_eq!(s.total_count(), 0);
   320	    }

   323	    fn single_node_no_edges_goes_to_sorted() {
   324	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   325	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   326	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a]);
   327	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![a]);
   328	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   329	    }

   332	    fn single_node_with_self_loop_goes_to_cycled() {
   333	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   334	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   335	        g.add_edge(a, a);
   336	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a]);
   337	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   338	        assert_eq!(s.cycled, vec![a]);
   339	    }

   341	    #[test]
   342	    fn linear_chain_yields_dependency_first_order() {
   343	        // A -> B -> C. A depends on B; B depends on C. Eval order: C, B, A.
   344	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   345	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   346	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   347	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   348	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   349	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);
   350	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![c, b, a]);
   351	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   352	    }

   354	    #[test]
   355	    fn diamond_dependency_orders_correctly() {
   356	        // A -> {B1, B2} -> C. Eval order: C first, then B1+B2 in either order, then A.
   357	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   358	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 4);
   359	        let (a, b1, b2, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2], ids[3]);
   360	        g.add_edge(a, b1);
   361	        g.add_edge(a, b2);
   362	        g.add_edge(b1, c);
   363	        g.add_edge(b2, c);
   364	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b1, b2, c]);
   365	        // C must come first
   366	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[0], c);
   367	        // A must come last
   368	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[3], a);
   369	        // B1 and B2 must come between
   370	        let mid: std::collections::HashSet<_> = [s.sorted[1], s.sorted[2]].into_iter().collect();
   371	        assert_eq!(mid, [b1, b2].into_iter().collect());
   372	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   373	    }

   375	    #[test]
   376	    fn two_node_cycle_goes_to_cycled() {
   377	        // A <-> B. Both belong to the same SCC of size 2.
   378	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   379	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 2);
   380	        let (a, b) = (ids[0], ids[1]);
   381	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   382	        g.add_edge(b, a);
   383	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b]);
   384	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   385	        assert_eq!(s.cycled.len(), 2);
   386	        let cycled: std::collections::HashSet<_> = s.cycled.iter().copied().collect();
   387	        assert_eq!(cycled, [a, b].into_iter().collect());
   388	    }

   390	    #[test]
   391	    fn mixed_cycle_and_acyclic_in_same_dirty_subset() {
   392	        // Cycle {A, B} disjoint from acyclic chain C -> D -> E.
   393	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   394	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 5);
   395	        let (a, b, c, d, e) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2], ids[3], ids[4]);
   396	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   397	        g.add_edge(b, a); // cycle
   398	        g.add_edge(c, d);
   399	        g.add_edge(d, e);
   400	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c, d, e]);
   401	        // Cycled has both a and b.
   402	        let cycled: std::collections::HashSet<_> = s.cycled.iter().copied().collect();
   403	        assert_eq!(cycled, [a, b].into_iter().collect());
   404	        // Sorted has [e, d, c] (dependency-first).
   405	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![e, d, c]);
   406	    }

   408	    #[test]
   409	    fn dirty_subset_filters_edges_to_clean_nodes() {
   410	        // A -> B -> C in graph; dirty subset = [A, B] only. Edge B -> C should be
   411	        // ignored (C is clean / read-as-is).
   412	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   413	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   414	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   415	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   416	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   417	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b]);
   418	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![b, a]);
   419	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   420	        // C never appears.
   421	        assert!(!s.sorted.contains(&c));
   422	        assert!(!s.cycled.contains(&c));
   423	    }

   427	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   428	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   429	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, a, a, a]);

   433	    #[test]
   434	    fn deep_linear_chain_no_stack_overflow() {
   435	        // 10,000-node linear chain. A recursive Tarjan would blow the 8MB stack ~around
   436	        // depth 8k-16k depending on frame size. The iterative implementation should
   437	        // handle this trivially.
   438	        const N: u32 = 10_000;
   439	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   440	        let ids: Vec<NodeId> = (0..N).map(|i| g.add_cell_node(0, i, 0)).collect();
   441	        for i in 0..(N as usize - 1) {
   442	            g.add_edge(ids[i], ids[i + 1]); // i depends on i+1
   443	        }
   444	        let s = schedule(&g, &ids);
   445	        assert_eq!(s.sorted.len(), N as usize);
   446	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   447	        // Last node (highest index) must come first (it's the deepest dependency).
   448	        assert_eq!(s.sorted[0], ids[(N - 1) as usize]);
   449	        // Source (index 0) must come last.
   450	        assert_eq!(*s.sorted.last().unwrap(), ids[0]);
   451	    }

   462	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);

   465	    }

   470	        // C itself is not in the cycle).

   474	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   475	        g.add_edge(b, a);
   476	        g.add_edge(c, a);
   477	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);

   481	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![c]);

   487	        // happened to point to a non-dirty version of itself, the cycle detection skips
   488	        // it — but that's a contrived case since `NodeId` identity is global. Test the
   489	        // straightforward case: self-loop + dirty = cycled.
   490	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   491	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   492	        g.add_edge(a, a);
   493	        let s = schedule(&g, &[a]);

   513	    #[test]
   514	    fn schedule_with_empty_supplemental_matches_schedule() {
   515	        // Empty supplemental must behave identically to schedule().
   516	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   517	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   518	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   519	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   520	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   521	
   522	        let supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   523	        let s_with = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a, b, c], &supp);
   524	        let s_plain = schedule(&g, &[a, b, c]);
   525	        assert_eq!(s_with, s_plain);
   526	    }

   528	    #[test]
   529	    fn supplemental_edge_produces_cycle() {
   530	        // GAP-G-03 / H1 scenario #1 (cycle):
   531	        //   A1 = SUM(B1:B1)   → supplemental A → B
   532	        //   B1 = "=A1+1"      → real graph B → A
   533	        // Both dirty → 2-SCC → cycled.
   534	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   535	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 2);
   536	        let (a, b) = (ids[0], ids[1]);
   537	        g.add_edge(b, a); // real: B depends on A
   538	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   539	        supp.insert(a, vec![b]); // supplemental: A depends on B (range)
   540	
   541	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a, b], &supp);
   542	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty(), "should be all cycled");
   543	        let cycled: HashSet<_> = s.cycled.iter().copied().collect();
   544	        assert_eq!(cycled, [a, b].into_iter().collect());
   545	    }
   546	
   547	    #[test]
   548	    fn supplemental_edge_produces_correct_ordering() {
   549	        // GAP-G-03 / H1 scenario #2 (ordering):
   550	        //   A1 = SUM(B1:B1)   → supplemental A → B
   551	        //   B1 = "=C1+1"      → real: B → C
   552	        //   C1 = 10           → leaf
   553	        // Dep-first: C, B, A.
   554	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   555	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   556	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   557	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   558	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   559	        supp.insert(a, vec![b]);
   560	
   561	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a, b, c], &supp);
   562	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   563	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![c, b, a]);
   564	    }

   566	    #[test]
   567	    fn supplemental_self_loop_is_cycled() {
   568	        // Self-range cycle: A1 = SUM(A1:A1). Single dirty formula,
   569	        // supplemental edge A → A. Must be cycled.
   570	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   571	        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
   572	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   573	        supp.insert(a, vec![a]);
   574	
   575	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a], &supp);
   576	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   577	        assert_eq!(s.cycled, vec![a]);
   578	    }

   580	    #[test]
   581	    fn supplemental_edge_to_non_dirty_node_ignored() {
   582	        // Supplemental A → B but B not dirty → A emits as sorted alone.
   583	        // Matches the existing contract that edges to non-dirty nodes
   584	        // are filtered out (they're read-as-is, not re-evaluated).
   585	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   586	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 2);
   587	        let (a, b) = (ids[0], ids[1]);
   588	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   589	        supp.insert(a, vec![b]); // B is in graph but not in dirty subset
   590	
   591	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a], &supp);
   592	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![a]);
   593	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   594	    }
   595	
   596	    #[test]
   597	    fn supplemental_does_not_duplicate_existing_real_edge() {
   598	        // If a real graph edge already exists from A to B AND supplemental
   599	        // also has A → B, the duplicate edge causes Tarjan to re-traverse
   600	        // the same back-edge. The cycle classification still must be
   601	        // correct (here: no cycle, just a chain).
   602	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   603	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 2);
   604	        let (a, b) = (ids[0], ids[1]);
   605	        g.add_edge(a, b); // real A → B
   606	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   607	        supp.insert(a, vec![b]); // supplemental A → B (duplicate)
   608	
   609	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a, b], &supp);
   610	        // Dep-first: B then A.
   611	        assert_eq!(s.sorted, vec![b, a]);
   612	        assert!(s.cycled.is_empty());
   613	    }
   614	
   615	    #[test]
   616	    fn supplemental_three_node_cycle_via_mixed_edges() {
   617	        // A → B (real), B → C (real), C → A (supplemental). 3-node SCC.
   618	        let mut g = Graph::new();
   619	        let ids = n_cells(&mut g, 3);
   620	        let (a, b, c) = (ids[0], ids[1], ids[2]);
   621	        g.add_edge(a, b);
   622	        g.add_edge(b, c);
   623	        let mut supp: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
   624	        supp.insert(c, vec![a]);
   625	
   626	        let s = schedule_with_supplemental(&g, &[a, b, c], &supp);
   627	        assert!(s.sorted.is_empty());
   628	        assert_eq!(s.cycled.len(), 3);
   629	    }
   630	}

codex
The core graph changes mostly line up with the described design so far, but I’ve already spotted one documentation/semantics tension: `Graph::clear_outgoing` and `clear_range_deps_for_formula` are called “no-op”/“idempotent” while intentionally bumping revision even on empty second calls. I’m checking whether that is only wording or leaks into runtime behavior.

   411	    /// Phase 3.2: formulas that reference `name` (canonical
   412	    /// uppercase). Today populated only from `AggregateNameRef`
   413	    /// occurrences. Phase 3.3 reads this from `on_set_name` to mark
   414	    /// dependents dirty.
   415	    pub fn formulas_referencing_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&HashSet<NodeId>> {
   416	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   417	        self.name_to_formulas.get(upper.as_str())
   418	    }
   419	
   420	    /// Phase 3.2 orchestrator + Phase 3.3 graph registration: walk a
   421	    /// bound `ExprPlan`, populate the session's per-formula dep view +
   422	    /// volatile set + name→formulas reverse index + cell→formulas
   423	    /// reverse index (Phase 3.3) + the Graph's stripe-index ranges
   424	    /// (Phase 3.3). Replaces any prior entry for the same formula
   425	    /// node wholesale so re-bind cleanly drops stale session-side
   426	    /// deps even though the Phase 0 graph's edges are append-only.
   427	    ///
   428	    /// **W5-50 (GAP-G-01 closure):** graph + stripe state is wholesale
   429	    /// revoked before re-registration via `Graph::clear_outgoing` +
   430	    /// `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula`. Prior comments here
   431	    /// claimed stripe-side stale entries accumulated on re-bind; that
   432	    /// was the Phase 0 append-only trade-off and is now fixed. The
   433	    /// revocation is the documented exception to the append-only
   434	    /// invariant — see `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
   435	    ///
   436	    /// Internal — public callers go through `on_set_formula` or
   437	    /// rebuild. `formula_node` MUST already exist in the graph (call
   438	    /// `or_insert_cell_node` first if necessary). `formula_sheet` is
   439	    /// the owning sheet of the formula cell; it's the fallback the
   440	    /// `StripeIndex` uses when a range's own `sheet` is `None` (today
   441	    /// every named-range conversion sets `Some(_)` so the fallback is
   442	    /// inert, but the parameter is part of the stable graph API).
   443	    fn extract_and_register_deps(
   444	        &mut self,
   445	        formula_node: NodeId,
   446	        formula_sheet: SheetId,
   447	        plan: &ExprPlan,
   448	    ) {
   449	        // First, evict any prior deps for this formula. Required so
   450	        // re-binding a formula whose text changed (e.g. `=A1` → `=B1`)
   451	        // doesn't leave the old cell-dep stamped on the session.
   452	        self.remove_formula_deps(formula_node);
   453	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-01 closure): also revoke prior graph edges +
   454	        // stripe registrations. Without this, stale forward edges
   455	        // create false `#CIRC!` cycles (megaudit H3) and stale stripe
   456	        // entries falsely dirty the formula on writes to the OLD range
   457	        // (megaudit H4). Idempotent on first-time bind.
   458	        self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node);
   459	        self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node);
   460	
   461	        // Walk and collect.
   462	        let mut deps = FormulaDeps::default();
   463	        walk_plan_for_deps(plan, &mut deps);
   464	
   465	        // Register: deduplicate (the walker emits duplicates if the
   466	        // formula references the same cell twice; the session stores
   467	        // each cell once).
   468	        let mut seen_cells: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   469	        deps.cells.retain(|c| seen_cells.insert(*c));
   470	        let mut seen_names: HashSet<Arc<str>> = HashSet::new();
   471	        deps.names.retain(|n| seen_names.insert(Arc::clone(n)));
   472	        // named_ranges may contain the same name twice — that's
   473	        // legitimate (`SUM(Sales) + AVERAGE(Sales)` references the
   474	        // same range from two arg positions) — leave as-is.
   475	
   476	        // Phase 3.3: register direct cell deps on the session-side
   477	        // reverse index. We do NOT create CellNodes for dep cells (no
   478	        // need — the lookup at `on_set_value` is by (sheet, row, col)
   479	        // tuple, not by NodeId).
   480	        //
   481	        // Phase 3.4: ALSO add a forward `graph.add_edge(formula_node,
   482	        // dep_node)` when the dep cell is ITSELF a formula cell. The
   483	        // Tarjan scheduler walks `graph.outgoing(formula_node)` to
   484	        // discover the dep set; without this edge it can't order a
   485	        // chain like `=A1`, `=B1=A1+1`, `=C1=B1+1` correctly. The
   486	        // edge is append-only per Phase 0 contract (re-bind leaves
   487	        // stale edges — filed as GAP-G-01; impact is performance,
   488	        // not correctness, because stale edges only add constraints).
   489	        for &(s, r, c) in &deps.cells {
   490	            self.cell_to_formulas
   491	                .entry((s, r, c))
   492	                .or_default()
   493	                .insert(formula_node);
   494	            if let Some(&dep_node) = self.cell_index.get(&(s, r, c)) {
   495	                // Note: self-edges (formula_node == dep_node) are
   496	                // ALLOWED here — they're the canonical way to model
   497	                // `=A1` at A1 (self-cycle), and the Phase 0 W3-3
   498	                // Tarjan scheduler treats a self-looped single-node
   499	                // SCC as `cycled`.
   500	                self.graph.add_edge(formula_node, dep_node);
   501	            }
   502	        }
   503	        // Phase 3.3: register named-range deps with the Graph's stripe
   504	        // index. The Graph's `register_range_dependency` populates BOTH
   505	        // the stripe map AND the `formula_to_range_deps` precision-check
   506	        // map atomically. We pass the formula's owning sheet as the
   507	        // fallback (today every conversion sets `range.sheet =
   508	        // Some(_)`, so the fallback is unused).
   509	        for (_, range) in &deps.named_ranges {
   510	            let range_ref = range_to_rangeref(*range);
   511	            self.graph
   512	                .register_range_dependency(formula_node, range_ref, formula_sheet);
   513	        }
   514	
   515	        if deps.is_volatile {
   516	            self.volatile_formulas.insert(formula_node);
   517	        }
   518	        for name in &deps.names {
   519	            self.name_to_formulas
   520	                .entry(Arc::clone(name))
   521	                .or_default()
   522	                .insert(formula_node);
   523	        }
   524	        if !deps.is_empty() || deps.is_volatile {
   525	            self.formula_deps.insert(formula_node, deps);
   526	        }
   527	    }
   528	
   529	    /// Internal: remove all session-side dep state for `formula_node`.
   530	    /// Used when a formula is re-bound (different text → different
   531	    /// deps) or cleared. Cleans `formula_deps`, the volatile set, the
   532	    /// name→formulas reverse index, and the cell→formulas reverse
   533	    /// index. Does NOT touch the `Graph`'s stripe state or edges —
   534	    /// those are revoked separately by the caller via
   535	    /// `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula`
   536	    /// (W5-50). See `extract_and_register_deps` for the wiring.
   537	    fn remove_formula_deps(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   538	        if let Some(prior) = self.formula_deps.remove(&formula_node) {
   539	            for name in &prior.names {
   540	                if let Some(set) = self.name_to_formulas.get_mut(name) {
   541	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   542	                    if set.is_empty() {
   543	                        self.name_to_formulas.remove(name);
   544	                    }
   545	                }
   546	            }
   547	            for cell in &prior.cells {
   548	                if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get_mut(cell) {
   549	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   550	                    if set.is_empty() {
   551	                        self.cell_to_formulas.remove(cell);
   552	                    }
   553	                }
   554	            }
   555	        }
   556	        self.volatile_formulas.remove(&formula_node);
   557	    }

   559	    /// **G3-01 acceptance.** Deterministic rebuild from an existing
   560	    /// `Workbook`. Walks every formula cell in sorted `(sheet, row, col)`
   561	    /// order, adds a `CellNode` per cell, populates the cell index,
   562	    /// and (Phase 3.2) lex+parse+binds each formula to extract
   563	    /// dependencies.
   564	    ///
   565	    /// Determinism: same workbook → same node IDs in the same order.
   566	    /// Workbook's underlying `formula_cells` is a `HashMap` (arbitrary
   567	    /// iteration order), so we sort before adding nodes.
   568	    ///
   569	    /// Failures aggregate into [`RebuildResult::failures`] rather than
   570	    /// short-circuiting — the returned session is usable, just with
   571	    /// some formulas missing dep info. Matches Phase 2B.2's
   572	    /// `RecomputeResult` shape.
   573	    pub fn rebuild_from_workbook(wb: &Workbook) -> RebuildResult {
   574	        let mut session = Self::new();
   575	
   576	        // Snapshot + sort the formula list for determinism.
   577	        let mut formulas: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = wb
   578	            .iter_formulas()
   579	            .map(|(s, r, c, f)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(f)))
   580	            .collect();
   581	        formulas.sort_by_key(|a| (a.0, a.1, a.2));
   582	
   583	        let attempted = formulas.len();
   584	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   585	        let mut failures: Vec<RebuildFailure> = Vec::new();
   586	
   587	        // Phase 3.4: pre-pass — insert a CellNode for every formula
   588	        // before any extract runs. The Tarjan-side forward edges
   589	        // require `cell_index` to be COMPLETE at extract time so
   590	        // formula `=A1` at C1 can see A1's node and add the edge
   591	        // (otherwise late-arriving deps would be missed). Done in
   592	        // sorted order so node IDs stay deterministic.
   593	        for (sheet, row, col, _) in &formulas {
   594	            session.or_insert_cell_node(*sheet, *row, *col);
   595	        }
   596	
   597	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in formulas {
   598	            let node = session
   599	                .cell_node_for(sheet, row, col)
   600	                .expect("pre-pass inserted every formula's node");
   601	
   602	            // Phase 3.2: lex + parse + bind + extract deps. Failure
   603	            // accumulates; we keep going. Phase 3.3 passes the
   604	            // formula's owning sheet so the stripe register can use
   605	            // the correct fallback for any range with `sheet: None`.
   606	            match Self::bind_text(&text, sheet, wb) {
   607	                Ok(plan) => {
   608	                    session.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, &plan);
   609	                    succeeded += 1;
   610	                }
   611	                Err(error) => failures.push(RebuildFailure {
   612	                    sheet,
   613	                    row,
   614	                    col,
   615	                    formula_text: text,
   616	                    error,
   617	                }),
   618	            }
   619	        }
   620	        // Phase 3.3: a fresh rebuild starts with NO dirty formulas —
   621	        // the caller has presumably already evaluated every cell into
   622	        // the workbook (e.g. `load_workbook_and_recompute`), so the
   623	        // session is in a clean state.
   624	        session.dirty.clear();
   625	        // Phase 3.6: aggregate cache also starts empty. Even if the
   626	        // caller pre-populated the workbook with computed values, those
   627	        // values aren't keyed by `(range, function)` and don't unlock
   628	        // a cache hit on next eval. `clear_all` is a no-op on a fresh
   629	        // session (the Default impl initializes empty), but keeping
   630	        // it makes the contract explicit.
   631	        session.aggregate_cache.clear_all();
   632	
   633	        RebuildResult {
   634	            session,
   635	            attempted,
   636	            succeeded,
   637	            failures,
   638	        }
   639	    }
   640	
   641	    /// Internal: lex + parse + bind a formula text against the
   642	    /// workbook's NameTable. Used by `rebuild_from_workbook` and by
   643	    /// `on_set_formula`'s text-only fallback. Phase 3.3 may add a
   644	    /// cached version that integrates with `PlanCache`.
   645	    fn bind_text(
   646	        text: &str,
   647	        owning_sheet: SheetId,
   648	        wb: &Workbook,
   649	    ) -> Result<ExprPlan, RuntimeError> {
   650	        let tokens = lex(text)?;
   651	        let expr = parse(tokens)?;
   652	        Ok(bind_with_names(&expr, owning_sheet, wb.names())?)
   653	    }
   654	
   655	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 1/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   656	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_value`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): the hook
   657	    /// now marks every formula that depends on `(sheet, row, col)`
   658	    /// dirty via two parallel paths:
   659	    ///
   660	    /// 1. Range deps via `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — stripe lookup
   661	    ///    + precision check (DIR-3-02 acceptance).
   662	    /// 2. Direct cell deps via the session-side `cell_to_formulas`
   663	    ///    reverse index (formulas that hold an `ExprPlan::CellRef` to
   664	    ///    this address).
   665	    ///
   666	    /// The hook DOES NOT create a node for the written cell: nodes
   667	    /// exist for FORMULA cells only; dep lookups for plain literal
   668	    /// cells go through the (sheet, row, col) tuple in both indices.
   669	    pub fn on_set_value(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   670	        self.hook_counts.set_value = self.hook_counts.set_value.saturating_add(1);
   671	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   672	    }
   673	
   674	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 2/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   675	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula`. Ensures the cell has a node,
   676	    /// walks the bound plan to register cell + range + name + volatile
   677	    /// dependencies (Phase 3.2), AND propagates dirty to downstream
   678	    /// formulas (Phase 3.3) — set_formula at a cell that other
   679	    /// formulas already reference means those downstream formulas now
   680	    /// see a different value and must recompute.
   681	    ///
   682	    /// The runtime passes the already-bound `ExprPlan` it just
   683	    /// evaluated; this hook does NOT re-bind. The plan shape is stable
   684	    /// since 2B.4 (Phase 3.2 doesn't add new variants).
   685	    pub fn on_set_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, plan: &ExprPlan) {
   686	        self.hook_counts.set_formula = self.hook_counts.set_formula.saturating_add(1);
   687	        // Phase 3.4: distinguish first-time-formula at this cell vs
   688	        // re-bind. The first case needs RETROACTIVE forward edges
   689	        // from previously-existing formulas that already reference
   690	        // this cell (those formulas' extract didn't see a NodeId for
   691	        // this cell at the time, so no edge was added).
   692	        let is_new_formula = self.cell_node_for(sheet, row, col).is_none();
   693	        let node = self.or_insert_cell_node(sheet, row, col);
   694	        if is_new_formula {
   695	            let referencing: Vec<NodeId> = self
   696	                .cell_to_formulas
   697	                .get(&(sheet, row, col))
   698	                .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   699	                .unwrap_or_default();
   700	            for prev in referencing {
   701	                if prev != node {
   702	                    self.graph.add_edge(prev, node);
   703	                }
   704	            }
   705	        }
   706	        self.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, plan);
   707	        // Phase 3.3: dirty downstream — formulas referencing the cell
   708	        // whose formula text just changed see a (potentially) new
   709	        // value. The formula itself is NOT marked dirty (the runtime
   710	        // wrote a fresh value for it before invoking this hook).
   711	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   712	    }
   713	
   714	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 3/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   715	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::clear_formula`. Phase 3.2: drops the cell's
   716	    /// session-side dep state (formula_deps, volatile, name reverse
   717	    /// index, cell reverse index). Phase 3.3: also marks downstream
   718	    /// formulas dirty (the cleared formula no longer produces its
   719	    /// prior value; dependents must recompute) and removes the
   720	    /// cleared formula itself from the dirty set.
   721	    pub fn on_clear_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   722	        self.hook_counts.clear_formula = self.hook_counts.clear_formula.saturating_add(1);
   723	        if let Some(node) = self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)).copied() {
   724	            self.remove_formula_deps(node);
   725	            // The cleared formula doesn't compute anymore. If it was
   726	            // dirty (queued for recompute), drop it from the set.
   727	            self.dirty.remove(&node);
   728	        }
   729	        // Downstream formulas see this cell change — propagate dirty.
   730	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   731	    }
   732	
   733	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 4/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   734	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_name`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): marks all
   735	    /// formulas referencing `name` (per `name_to_formulas`) dirty.
   736	    /// Today this reverse index is populated only by
   737	    /// `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef` — see GAP-R-07 for the scalar-
   738	    /// NameRef precision gap (PlanCache `name_gen` still covers
   739	    /// correctness; per-name precision is the open follow-up).
   740	    ///
   741	    /// Phase 3.10 megaudit H2 (2026-05-12): the prior implementation
   742	    /// only marked DIRECT name-referencing formulas dirty, leaving
   743	    /// downstream-of-downstream stale. Fixed: each directly-affected
   744	    /// formula now seeds a `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` BFS at its
   745	    /// own cell address, fanning out the same way cell writes do.
   746	    pub fn on_set_name(&mut self, name: &str) {
   747	        self.hook_counts.set_name = self.hook_counts.set_name.saturating_add(1);
   748	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   749	        let dependents: Vec<NodeId> = self
   750	            .name_to_formulas
   751	            .get(upper.as_str())
   752	            .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   753	            .unwrap_or_default();
   754	        for n in dependents {
   755	            self.dirty.insert(n);
   756	            // Phase 3.10 H2 fix: transitive fanout. Whatever depends on
   757	            // this name-referencing formula must also recompute when
   758	            // the name's target changes, just like for a direct cell
   759	            // edit. mark_dirty_from_cell_write does the BFS via the
   760	            // reverse-dep + stripe path so any chain or range dep is

 succeeded in 259ms:
   680	pub fn pi(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   681	    if !args.is_empty() {
   682	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   683	    }
   684	    Value::Number(std::f64::consts::PI)
   685	}

   687	/// `DEGREES(radians)` — radians → degrees. Phase 3.10 audit M7
   688	/// (2026-05-13): sanitize the output via `sanitize_f64` so an Inf
   689	/// produced by `f64::to_degrees` on extreme inputs surfaces as
   690	/// `#NUM!` rather than a silent non-finite Value::Number.
   691	pub fn degrees(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   692	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   693	        Ok(n) => n,
   694	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   695	    };
   696	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.to_degrees()) {
   697	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   698	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   699	    }
   700	}

   715	// ===== Trigonometry (Phase 4.3 V2, W5-51) =====
   716	//
   717	// Excel canon: SIN / COS / TAN / SINH / COSH / TANH take an angle in
   718	// RADIANS. Use RADIANS(deg) if input is in degrees. ASIN / ACOS / ATAN /
   719	// ATAN2 RETURN radians.
   720	//
   721	// Domain errors → #NUM!:
   722	//   - ASIN(x), ACOS(x): |x| > 1
   723	//   - ATAN2(0, 0): both args zero (per Excel; Rust f64::atan2 returns 0)
   724	//   - SINH(x), COSH(x): very large |x| may overflow to ±Inf — sanitize
   725	//     surfaces as #NUM!.
   726	//
   727	// TAN(pi/2) does NOT error in Excel — it returns a huge but finite
   728	// float (≈1.6e16). The IEEE 754 result of `(π/2).tan()` is finite
   729	// because π/2 cannot be exactly represented; sanitize_f64 lets it
   730	// through. Matches IronCalc behavior.
   731	
   732	/// `SIN(angle_in_radians)` — sine. Standard f64 sin.
   733	pub fn sin(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   734	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   735	        Ok(n) => n,
   736	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   737	    };
   738	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.sin()) {
   739	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   740	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   741	    }
   742	}
   743	
   744	/// `COS(angle_in_radians)` — cosine. Standard f64 cos.
   745	pub fn cos(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   746	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   747	        Ok(n) => n,
   748	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   749	    };
   750	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.cos()) {
   751	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   752	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   753	    }
   754	}
   755	
   756	/// `TAN(angle_in_radians)` — tangent. Excel does NOT error at
   757	/// asymptotes; the IEEE 754 result of `(π/2).tan()` is finite (the
   758	/// argument isn't exactly π/2 in floating point). `sanitize_f64`
   759	/// surfaces a NaN or non-finite result as `#NUM!`.
   760	pub fn tan(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   761	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   762	        Ok(n) => n,
   763	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   764	    };
   765	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.tan()) {
   766	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   767	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   768	    }
   769	}
   770	
   771	/// `ASIN(value)` — arc sine. Returns radians in `[-π/2, π/2]`. Domain
   772	/// `|value| > 1` → `#NUM!` (matches Excel; Rust returns NaN which
   773	/// `sanitize_f64` would also surface as `#NUM!`, but we check
   774	/// up-front for the canonical error message and to avoid relying on
   775	/// NaN-propagation behavior).
   776	pub fn asin(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   777	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   778	        Ok(n) => n,
   779	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   780	    };
   781	    if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n) {
   782	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   783	    }
   784	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.asin()) {
   785	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   786	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   787	    }
   788	}
   789	
   790	/// `ACOS(value)` — arc cosine. Returns radians in `[0, π]`. Domain
   791	/// `|value| > 1` → `#NUM!`.
   792	pub fn acos(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   793	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   794	        Ok(n) => n,
   795	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   796	    };
   797	    if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n) {
   798	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   799	    }
   800	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.acos()) {
   801	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   802	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   803	    }
   804	}
   805	
   806	/// `ATAN(value)` — arc tangent. Returns radians in `(-π/2, π/2)`.
   807	/// Total domain: any real input.
   808	pub fn atan(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   809	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   810	        Ok(n) => n,
   811	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   812	    };
   813	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.atan()) {
   814	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   815	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   816	    }
   817	}
   818	
   819	/// `ATAN2(y, x)` — two-argument arc tangent. Returns radians in
   820	/// `(-π, π]`. Excel argument order is `ATAN2(x, y)` historically —
   821	/// but the W3C / OOXML standard and modern Excel use `ATAN2(x_num,
   822	/// y_num)`. Per IronCalc + the W3C reference, Quantbook follows
   823	/// `ATAN2(x, y)` order: first arg is x, second is y. Returns
   824	/// `#DIV/0!` if both args are zero (Excel canon; Rust's `f64::atan2`
   825	/// would return 0.0 in that case, which is wrong).
   826	pub fn atan2(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   827	    if args.len() != 2 {
   828	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   829	    }
   830	    let x = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
   831	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   832	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   833	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   834	    };
   835	    let y = match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
   836	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   837	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   838	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   839	    };
   840	    if x == 0.0 && y == 0.0 {
   841	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
   842	    }
   843	    // Excel arg order is (x, y); Rust's f64::atan2 is (y, x).
   844	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(y.atan2(x)) {
   845	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   846	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   847	    }
   848	}
   849	
   850	// ===== Text (Phase 4.3 V1) =====
   851	
   852	/// Helper: coerce a Value to its display string per Excel canon.
   853	/// Numbers print as their f64 representation; Bool as TRUE/FALSE;
   854	/// Text passes through; Blank as "" (empty string). Errors propagate.
   855	fn coerce_text(v: &Value) -> Result<String, ErrorValue> {
   856	    match v {
   857	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
   858	        Value::Text(s) => Ok(s.as_ref().to_owned()),
   859	        Value::Number(n) => Ok(format_number_for_text(*n)),
   860	        Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }.to_owned()),
   861	        Value::Blank => Ok(String::new()),
   862	    }
   863	}
   864	
   865	/// Excel-canon number → text. Integers render without a trailing `.0`;
   866	/// otherwise the default `f64` Display is good enough for V1. Phase 4.5
   867	/// (number formats) replaces this with locale-aware formatting.
   868	fn format_number_for_text(n: f64) -> String {
   869	    if n == n.trunc() && n.abs() < 1e15 {
   870	        format!("{}", n as i64)
   871	    } else {
   872	        format!("{n}")
   873	    }
   874	}
   875	
   876	/// `LEN(text)` — character count of the text representation (UTF-8 chars).
   877	/// Excel treats it as character count, not byte count.
   878	pub fn len(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   879	    if args.len() != 1 {
   880	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   881	    }
   882	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
   883	        Ok(s) => s,
   884	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   885	    };
   886	    Value::Number(text.chars().count() as f64)
   887	}
   888	
   889	/// `UPPER(text)` — ASCII + Unicode uppercase. Excel's localization
   890	/// (Turkish dotted/dotless I) lands Phase 4.9.
   891	pub fn upper(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   892	    if args.len() != 1 {
   893	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   894	    }
   895	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
   896	        Ok(s) => s,
   897	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   898	    };
   899	    Value::text(text.to_uppercase())
   900	}

 succeeded in 259ms:
     1	//! Function registry — name → implementation dispatch.
     2	//!
     3	//! Names are stored uppercase per Excel canon. Lookup is case-insensitive: the caller
     4	//! can pass `"sum"`, `"SUM"`, or `"Sum"` and get the same function.
     5	
     6	use std::collections::HashMap;
     7	
     8	use ql_types::Value;
     9	
    10	use crate::{scalar_fns, volatile};
    11	
    12	/// Function signature: pre-evaluated args → result Value.
    13	pub type ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value;
    14	
    15	/// Phase 0 function registry. Built by `default_registry()` with the W4-4 function set.
    16	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
    17	pub struct FunctionRegistry {
    18	    fns: HashMap<&'static str, ScalarFn>,
    19	}
    20	
    21	impl Default for FunctionRegistry {
    22	    fn default() -> Self {
    23	        Self::new()
    24	    }
    25	}
    26	
    27	impl FunctionRegistry {
    28	    /// Empty registry — caller adds functions via `register`. Use `default_registry()`
    29	    /// for the Phase 0 built-in set.
    30	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    31	        Self {
    32	            fns: HashMap::new(),
    33	        }
    34	    }
    35	
    36	    /// Register a function under `name`.
    37	    ///
    38	    /// Phase 2A.7 audit M5: `name` MUST already be canonical upper-case. This is
    39	    /// asserted at registration time so a stray `register("sum", ...)` doesn't
    40	    /// silently create an unreachable entry (lookups uppercase the query, so a
    41	    /// lower-case key would never be found). Duplicate registrations also
    42	    /// panic — silent override would let a typo replace a built-in with a buggy
    43	    /// shim. Phase 2 has a closed default function set; if dynamic registration
    44	    /// ever becomes a real use case, swap this for `Result<(), RegisterError>`.
    45	    pub fn register(&mut self, name: &'static str, f: ScalarFn) {
    46	        assert!(
    47	            !name.is_empty(),
    48	            "FunctionRegistry::register: name must not be empty"
    49	        );
    50	        assert!(
    51	            name.bytes().all(|b| !b.is_ascii_lowercase()),
    52	            "FunctionRegistry::register: name {name:?} must be canonical upper-case; \
    53	             lookups uppercase the query, so a lower-case key is unreachable"
    54	        );
    55	        let prior = self.fns.insert(name, f);
    56	        assert!(
    57	            prior.is_none(),
    58	            "FunctionRegistry::register: duplicate registration for {name:?} — \
    59	             silent override would let a typo replace a built-in"
    60	        );
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// Case-insensitive lookup. Returns `None` if not registered.
    64	    pub fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ScalarFn> {
    65	        // Allocate a single uppercase key for the lookup; the registry holds &'static
    66	        // uppercase names, so we compare on uppercase form.
    67	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
    68	        self.fns.get(upper.as_str()).copied()
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    pub fn names(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &&'static str> {
    72	        self.fns.keys()
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
    76	        self.fns.len()
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    80	        self.fns.is_empty()
    81	    }
    82	}
    83

    87

   767	
   768	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 5/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   769	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::add_sheet`. Phase 3.1 stub still: bumps
   770	    /// counter. No dirty effect — a new sheet contains no formulas
   771	    /// yet. Phase 4.6 (cross-sheet references) will use this to update
   772	    /// the graph's sheet-id tracking.
   773	    pub fn on_add_sheet(&mut self, _new_sheet: SheetId) {
   774	        self.hook_counts.add_sheet = self.hook_counts.add_sheet.saturating_add(1);
   775	    }
   776	
   777	    /// Phase 3.3 (single-hop) + Phase 3.4 (BFS): internal fanout —
   778	    /// for a write at `(sheet, row, col)`, mark every formula node
   779	    /// that TRANSITIVELY depends on this cell dirty. Two reverse-
   780	    /// direction lookups per visited node:
   781	    ///
   782	    /// 1. `Graph::dependents_for_cell` returns range-dep candidates,
   783	    ///    already filtered by the precision check (DIR-3-02).
   784	    /// 2. `cell_to_formulas[(s,r,c)]` returns direct-cell-dep
   785	    ///    formulas.
   786	    ///
   787	    /// Phase 3.4 added the BFS pass: for each newly-marked formula
   788	    /// `F`, look up `F`'s cell address and recurse. A chain
   789	    /// `=A1 → =B1=A1+1 → =C1=B1+1` thus marks both B1 AND C1 dirty
   790	    /// from a single edit at A1. The `dirty.insert(node)` check
   791	    /// returns false on duplicate insert → BFS visits each node at
   792	    /// most once → cycles in the dep graph are safe.
   793	    fn mark_dirty_from_cell_write(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   794	        use std::collections::VecDeque;
   795	        let mut queue: VecDeque<NodeId> = VecDeque::new();
   796	
   797	        // Phase 3.6 (AGG-3-02): invalidate any cached aggregate whose
   798	        // range contains this cell. Done BEFORE the dirty fanout so a
   799	        // formula whose recompute would otherwise hit a stale cache
   800	        // sees a fresh miss + recomputation. Precision is exact —
   801	        // writes outside every cached range are O(cache_size) with no
   802	        // entry removal.
   803	        self.aggregate_cache.invalidate_at(sheet, row, col);
   804	
   805	        // Seed with the direct fanout from the edited cell (which may
   806	        // not itself be a formula).
   807	        for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(sheet, row, col) {
   808	            if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   809	                queue.push_back(dep);
   810	            }
   811	        }
   812	        if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(sheet, row, col)) {
   813	            for &dep in set {
   814	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   815	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   816	                }
   817	            }
   818	        }
   819	
   820	        // BFS: for every newly-marked formula, find what depends on
   821	        // ITS cell and propagate. This is the transitive closure of
   822	        // the reverse-dep relation, scoped to formula nodes.
   823	        while let Some(node) = queue.pop_front() {
   824	            let Some((ns, nr, nc)) = self.cell_address_for(node) else {
   825	                continue;
   826	            };
   827	            for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(ns, nr, nc) {
   828	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   829	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   830	                }
   831	            }
   832	            if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(ns, nr, nc)) {
   833	                let snapshot: Vec<NodeId> = set.iter().copied().collect();
   834	                for dep in snapshot {
   835	                    if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   836	                        queue.push_back(dep);
   837	                    }
   838	                }
   839	            }
   840	        }
   841	    }
   842	
   843	    /// Phase 3.3: read-only view of the dirty set. Caller may iterate
   844	    /// to schedule recompute. Use `take_dirty` to atomically claim +
   845	    /// clear.
   846	    pub fn dirty_formulas(&self) -> &HashSet<NodeId> {
   847	        &self.dirty
   848	    }
   849	
   850	    /// Phase 3.3: is this formula currently queued for recompute?
   851	    pub fn is_dirty(&self, node: NodeId) -> bool {
   852	        self.dirty.contains(&node)
   853	    }
   854	
   855	    /// Phase 3.3: claim + clear the dirty set in one move. The Phase
   856	    /// 3.4 Tarjan SCC scheduler will call this once per recompute
   857	    /// cycle. Returning `HashSet<NodeId>` rather than a slice lets
   858	    /// callers re-sort / topologically order without re-allocating.
   859	    pub fn take_dirty(&mut self) -> HashSet<NodeId> {
   860	        std::mem::take(&mut self.dirty)
   861	    }
   862	
   863	    /// **Phase 3.7 (2026-05-12) — VOL-3-01/02 entry point.** Mark every
   864	    /// volatile formula (NOW / RAND / TODAY / RANDBETWEEN / RANDARRAY
   865	    /// / INDIRECT / OFFSET / INFO / CELL, per `is_volatile_function`)
   866	    /// dirty AND fan out the transitive reverse-dep graph from each
   867	    /// volatile cell so downstream formulas recompute too. This is
   868	    /// the "F9 / explicit recalc" trigger.
   869	    ///
   870	    /// Returns the number of volatile formulas marked. Zero means no
   871	    /// volatile formulas exist in the workbook — `recompute_dirty`
   872	    /// after this call will be a no-op.
   873	    pub fn mark_volatile_dirty(&mut self) -> usize {
   874	        let volatile: Vec<NodeId> = self.volatile_formulas.iter().copied().collect();
   875	        let count = volatile.len();
   876	        for v in volatile {
   877	            self.dirty.insert(v);
   878	            // Fan out from the volatile cell's address so downstream
   879	            // formulas reading its value also recompute (VOL-3-02).
   880	            // `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` does BFS via the reverse-
   881	            // dep + stripe path, so any chain or range dep is reached.
   882	            if let Some((s, r, c)) = self.cell_address_for(v) {
   883	                self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(s, r, c);
   884	            }
   885	        }
   886	        count
   887	    }
   888	
   889	    /// Phase 3.7: count of formulas currently marked volatile (Phase
   890	    /// 3.2 populates via `is_volatile_function`). Test-only
   891	    /// observability; product code consumes `volatile_formulas()` or
   892	    /// calls `mark_volatile_dirty()` directly.
   893	    pub fn volatile_count(&self) -> usize {
   894	        self.volatile_formulas.len()
   895	    }
   896	
   897	    /// Phase 3.6 — borrow the aggregate cache. The runtime passes this
   898	    /// to `eval_scalar_with_cache` during `recompute_dirty` /
   899	    /// `set_formula` so aggregate-over-range evals hit the cache when
   900	    /// nothing in the range changed since the last computation.
   901	    pub fn aggregate_cache(&self) -> &InMemAggregateCache {
   902	        &self.aggregate_cache
   903	    }
   904	
   905	    /// Phase 3.6 — snapshot of `(hits, misses, invalidations)` for
   906	    /// observability + tests (AGG-3-01 asserts hits > 0 after an
   907	    /// unrelated edit; AGG-3-02 asserts invalidations > 0 after an
   908	    /// intersecting edit).
   909	    pub fn aggregate_cache_stats(&self) -> AggregateCacheStats {
   910	        self.aggregate_cache.stats()
   911	    }
   912	
   913	    /// Phase 3.3 introspection: how many cells have at least one
   914	    /// formula referencing them via a direct `ExprPlan::CellRef`?
   915	    /// Used by tests + future ql-profile observability. Range deps
   916	    /// live on the Graph's stripe index — query
   917	    /// `graph().stripe_index()` for those.
   918	    pub fn cell_dep_count(&self) -> usize {
   919	        self.cell_to_formulas.len()
   920	    }
   921	
   922	    /// Phase 3.4: address lookup for a formula node. The graph's
   923	    /// `Node::Cell` payload carries `(sheet, row, col)` — this is the
   924	    /// inverse of `cell_node_for`. Returns `None` if the node is not
   925	    /// a Cell variant (today every NodeId in `cell_index` is a Cell;
   926	    /// Phase 4 may add Range/Region nodes that aren't formula cells).
   927	    pub fn cell_address_for(&self, node: NodeId) -> Option<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> {
   928	        match self.graph.node(node) {
   929	            Node::Cell(CellNode { sheet, row, col }) => Some((*sheet, *row, *col)),
   930	            _ => None,
   931	        }
   932	    }
   933	
   934	    /// **Phase 3.4 acceptance (SCH-3-01..04 entry point).** Atomically
   935	    /// claim the dirty set and run the Phase 0 W3-3 iterative Tarjan
   936	    /// scheduler over it. The returned [`Schedule`] partitions nodes:
   937	    ///
   938	    /// - `sorted`: dependency-first topological order. Evaluate
   939	    ///   `sorted[0]` before `sorted[1]`, etc.
   940	    /// - `cycled`: nodes in a non-trivial strongly connected component
   941	    ///   (size > 1) or with a self-loop. Per Phase 0 spec these get
   942	    ///   `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`.
   943	    ///
   944	    /// The dirty set is cleared by this call. Edges to non-dirty
   945	    /// nodes are not traversed (those nodes aren't being recomputed;
   946	    /// their current values are read as-is). Input ordering is
   947	    /// sorted-by-NodeId before the scheduler runs so SCH-3-03
   948	    /// determinism holds: same workbook + same edits → same schedule
   949	    /// across runs (the underlying scheduler is already deterministic
   950	    /// in its input slice order; we just normalize HashSet iteration).
   951	    pub fn schedule_dirty(&mut self) -> Schedule {
   952	        let mut dirty_vec: Vec<NodeId> = self.dirty.drain().collect();
   953	        dirty_vec.sort();
   954	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-03 closure): inject supplemental adjacency so
   955	        // Tarjan can see range-induced ordering / cycles. The Phase 0
   956	        // graph stores range deps in stripes + `formula_to_range_deps`
   957	        // rather than as `add_edge` edges (the A4 acceptance: SUM(A:A)
   958	        // must compress to ONE RangeRef, not 25M edges). For Tarjan we
   959	        // expand range deps lazily into temp edges F → G where G is a
   960	        // dirty formula inside one of F's ranges.
   961	        let supplemental = self.build_range_supplemental(&dirty_vec);
   962	        schedule_with_supplemental(&self.graph, &dirty_vec, &supplemental)
   963	    }
   964	
   965	    /// W5-50 — build the supplemental adjacency for
   966	    /// `topo::schedule_with_supplemental`. For each dirty formula `F`
   967	    /// with at least one range dep, and each OTHER (or same — for
   968	    /// self-range cycles) dirty formula `G` whose cell address falls
   969	    /// inside one of F's ranges, emit a temp edge `F → G`.

commit 9cb63c86cc5bf581a2954fd2c2ad001752a2c734
Author: steppen0mad <nurtaymergaliyev@gmail.com>
Date:   Wed May 13 12:19:04 2026 +0100

    W5-51 / Engine Phase 4.3 V2 batch #1: trigonometry (7 functions)
    
    First Phase 4.3 V2 batch on top of the W5-50 GAP-G-01/03 closure.
    Scalar-only — does NOT exercise the new range-aware path. The
    SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup batches (which DO exercise it) need a function-
    signature refactor that wasn't called out in the W5-49 decision doc;
    batch ordering revised to lead with the scalar-only work that fits
    the existing `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` contract.
    
    Workspace: 1016 → 1029 (+13). Function library: 52 → 59 entries.
    All 7 gates green: fmt, clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings,
    workspace tests, build-flags, cargo-lock pin, multiversion clones
    (aarch64 NEON verified), cargo audit (1 pre-existing allowed warning).
    
    # Functions added (crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs)
    
    - SIN(angle_radians)
    - COS(angle_radians)
    - TAN(angle_radians)  — Excel canon: TAN(π/2) returns huge-finite,
      not error. The IEEE 754 result of `(π/2).tan()` is finite because
      π/2 cannot be exactly represented in f64.
    - ASIN(value) — returns radians in [-π/2, π/2]; domain `|x|>1` →
      `#NUM!`. We explicit-check before `f64::asin` rather than relying
      on NaN propagation through sanitize_f64.
    - ACOS(value) — returns radians in [0, π]; same domain check.
    - ATAN(value) — returns radians in (-π/2, π/2); total domain.
    - ATAN2(x, y) — returns radians in (-π, π]. Excel arg order is
      `(x, y)`; Rust's `f64::atan2` is `(y, x)`, so we internally call
      `y.atan2(x)`. `ATAN2(0, 0)` → `#DIV/0!` per Excel (Rust would
      return 0).
    
    All single-arg fns route through `one_number(args, 1, 1)` +
    `sanitize_f64`. ATAN2 has its own arity check (must be exactly 2)
    + both-zero check.
    
    # Registration (crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs)
    
    7 new entries appended to `default_registry`; expected-count test
    updated 52 → 59.
    
    # Tests (crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs::tests)
    
    13 new tests covering:
    - basic values (sin/cos/tan at 0, π/2, π, π/4)
    - tan_near_half_pi_is_huge_finite_not_error (Excel canon)
    - asin/acos/atan basic inverses
    - asin/acos domain errors (|x|>1 → #NUM!)
    - asin/acos boundary values (|x|=1 ok)
    - atan saturation at extreme inputs
    - atan2 basic quadrants (0/π/2/π/3π/4)
    - atan2(0,0) → #DIV/0!
    - arity errors (single-arg fns: 0 args or 2+; ATAN2: ≠2)
    - error propagation
    - text → #VALUE!
    - Blank coerces to 0
    
    # Excel matrix (docs/compat/excel-matrix.md)
    
    SIN/COS/TAN/ASIN/ACOS/ATAN/ATAN2 row flipped ❌ → ✅ with notes
    covering ATAN2 arg-order, ATAN2(0,0) DIV/0, ASIN/ACOS domain,
    TAN(π/2) huge-finite-not-error.
    
    # What this commit does NOT do
    
    - No range-aware functions (SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup family) — those
      need the function-signature refactor.
    - No hyperbolic trig (SINH/COSH/TANH); deferred to wave 2 per matrix.
    - No FN4-01 closure — we're at 59/100; lookup/conditional-aggregate
      batches needed for full close.
    - No push.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
