         let ai = r.lookup("AI").expect("AI is registered");
         // AI ignores its args and returns the AINotAvailable error.
         assert_eq!(ai(&[]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable));
         assert_eq!(
             ai(&[Value::Number(1.0), Value::text("prompt")]),
             Value::Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable)
         );
         // Case-insensitive lookup works.
         let ai_lower = r.lookup("ai").expect("ai (lowercase) resolves");
         assert_eq!(ai_lower(&[]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::AINotAvailable));
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn lookup_is_case_insensitive() {
         let r = default_registry();
         let sum_upper = r.lookup("SUM").unwrap();
         let sum_lower = r.lookup("sum").unwrap();
         let sum_mixed = r.lookup("Sum").unwrap();
         let result_upper = sum_upper(&[Value::Number(1.0), Value::Number(2.0)]);
         let result_lower = sum_lower(&[Value::Number(1.0), Value::Number(2.0)]);
         let result_mixed = sum_mixed(&[Value::Number(1.0), Value::Number(2.0)]);
         assert_eq!(result_upper, Value::Number(3.0));
         assert_eq!(result_lower, result_upper);
         assert_eq!(result_mixed, result_upper);
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn lookup_missing_returns_none() {
         let r = default_registry();
         assert!(r.lookup("FAKE_FN_NAME_XYZ").is_none());
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn dotted_function_names_supported() {
         // VAR.S — Excel uses dot in modern compatibility-friendly names.
         let r = default_registry();
         let var_s = r.lookup("VAR.S").unwrap();
         let result = var_s(&[Value::Number(1.0), Value::Number(3.0)]);
         assert_eq!(result, Value::Number(2.0));
     }

     if args.is_empty() || args.len() > 2 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     let value = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
         NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
         NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
         NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     if value <= 0.0 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
     }
     let base = if args.len() == 2 {
         match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
             NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
             NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
             NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
         }
     } else {
         10.0
     };
     if base <= 0.0 || base == 1.0 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
     }
     match coercion::sanitize_f64(value.log(base)) {
         Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
         Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
     }
 }
 
 /// `LOG10(number)` — base-10 logarithm.
 pub fn log10(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
         Ok(n) => n,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     if n <= 0.0 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
     }
     match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.log10()) {
         Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
         Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
     }
 }
 
 /// `PI()` — π constant. No arguments.
 pub fn pi(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     if !args.is_empty() {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     Value::Number(std::f64::consts::PI)
 }
 
 /// `DEGREES(radians)` — radians → degrees. Phase 3.10 audit M7
 /// (2026-05-13): sanitize the output via `sanitize_f64` so an Inf
 /// produced by `f64::to_degrees` on extreme inputs surfaces as
 /// `#NUM!` rather than a silent non-finite Value::Number.
 pub fn degrees(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
         Ok(n) => n,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.to_degrees()) {
         Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
         Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
     }
 }
 
 /// `RADIANS(degrees)` — degrees → radians. Same sanitization
 /// contract as DEGREES (Phase 3.10 audit M7).
 pub fn radians(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
         Ok(n) => n,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.to_radians()) {
         Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
         Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
     }
 }
 
+// ===== Trigonometry (Phase 4.3 V2, W5-51) =====
+//
+// Excel canon: SIN / COS / TAN / SINH / COSH / TANH take an angle in
+// RADIANS. Use RADIANS(deg) if input is in degrees. ASIN / ACOS / ATAN /
+// ATAN2 RETURN radians.
+//
+// Domain errors → #NUM!:
+//   - ASIN(x), ACOS(x): |x| > 1
+//   - ATAN2(0, 0): both args zero (per Excel; Rust f64::atan2 returns 0)
+//   - SINH(x), COSH(x): very large |x| may overflow to ±Inf — sanitize
+//     surfaces as #NUM!.
+//
+// TAN(pi/2) does NOT error in Excel — it returns a huge but finite
+// float (≈1.6e16). The IEEE 754 result of `(π/2).tan()` is finite
+// because π/2 cannot be exactly represented; sanitize_f64 lets it
+// through. Matches IronCalc behavior.
+
+/// `SIN(angle_in_radians)` — sine. Standard f64 sin.
+pub fn sin(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.sin()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `COS(angle_in_radians)` — cosine. Standard f64 cos.
+pub fn cos(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.cos()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `TAN(angle_in_radians)` — tangent. Excel does NOT error at
+/// asymptotes; the IEEE 754 result of `(π/2).tan()` is finite (the
+/// argument isn't exactly π/2 in floating point). `sanitize_f64`
+/// surfaces a NaN or non-finite result as `#NUM!`.
+pub fn tan(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.tan()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `ASIN(value)` — arc sine. Returns radians in `[-π/2, π/2]`. Domain
+/// `|value| > 1` → `#NUM!` (matches Excel; Rust returns NaN which
+/// `sanitize_f64` would also surface as `#NUM!`, but we check
+/// up-front for the canonical error message and to avoid relying on
+/// NaN-propagation behavior).
+pub fn asin(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n) {
+        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
+    }
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.asin()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `ACOS(value)` — arc cosine. Returns radians in `[0, π]`. Domain
+/// `|value| > 1` → `#NUM!`.
+pub fn acos(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n) {
+        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
+    }
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.acos()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `ATAN(value)` — arc tangent. Returns radians in `(-π/2, π/2)`.
+/// Total domain: any real input.
+pub fn atan(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
+        Ok(n) => n,
+        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.atan()) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
+/// `ATAN2(y, x)` — two-argument arc tangent. Returns radians in
+/// `(-π, π]`. Excel argument order is `ATAN2(x, y)` historically —
+/// but the W3C / OOXML standard and modern Excel use `ATAN2(x_num,
+/// y_num)`. Per IronCalc + the W3C reference, Quantbook follows
+/// `ATAN2(x, y)` order: first arg is x, second is y. Returns
+/// `#DIV/0!` if both args are zero (Excel canon; Rust's `f64::atan2`
+/// would return 0.0 in that case, which is wrong).
+pub fn atan2(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
+    if args.len() != 2 {
+        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
+    }
+    let x = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
+        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
+        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
+        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    let y = match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
+        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
+        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
+        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
+    };
+    if x == 0.0 && y == 0.0 {
+        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
+    }
+    // Excel arg order is (x, y); Rust's f64::atan2 is (y, x).
+    match coercion::sanitize_f64(y.atan2(x)) {
+        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
+        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
+    }
+}
+
 // ===== Text (Phase 4.3 V1) =====
 
 /// Helper: coerce a Value to its display string per Excel canon.
 /// Numbers print as their f64 representation; Bool as TRUE/FALSE;
 /// Text passes through; Blank as "" (empty string). Errors propagate.
 fn coerce_text(v: &Value) -> Result<String, ErrorValue> {
     match v {
         Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
         Value::Text(s) => Ok(s.as_ref().to_owned()),
         Value::Number(n) => Ok(format_number_for_text(*n)),
         Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }.to_owned()),
         Value::Blank => Ok(String::new()),
     }
 }
 
 /// Excel-canon number → text. Integers render without a trailing `.0`;
 /// otherwise the default `f64` Display is good enough for V1. Phase 4.5
 /// (number formats) replaces this with locale-aware formatting.
 fn format_number_for_text(n: f64) -> String {
     if n == n.trunc() && n.abs() < 1e15 {
         format!("{}", n as i64)
     } else {
         format!("{n}")
     }
 }
 
 /// `LEN(text)` — character count of the text representation (UTF-8 chars).
 /// Excel treats it as character count, not byte count.
 pub fn len(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     if args.len() != 1 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
         Ok(s) => s,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     Value::Number(text.chars().count() as f64)
 }
 
 /// `UPPER(text)` — ASCII + Unicode uppercase. Excel's localization
 /// (Turkish dotted/dotless I) lands Phase 4.9.
 pub fn upper(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     if args.len() != 1 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
         Ok(s) => s,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     Value::text(text.to_uppercase())
 }
 
 /// `LOWER(text)` — ASCII + Unicode lowercase.
 pub fn lower(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     if args.len() != 1 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
         Ok(s) => s,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     Value::text(text.to_lowercase())
 }
 
 /// `TRIM(text)` — strip leading/trailing whitespace AND collapse internal
 /// runs of multiple spaces to a single space. Excel's canon collapses
 /// only standard space (0x20) runs; we follow that.
 pub fn trim(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
     if args.len() != 1 {
         return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
     }
     let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
         Ok(s) => s,
         Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
     };
     let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len());
     let mut prev_space = false;
     let trimmed = text.trim_matches(' ');
     for c in trimmed.chars() {
         if c == ' ' {

+    fn approx(a: f64, b: f64) -> bool {
+        (a - b).abs() < 1e-9
+    }

+    #[test]
+    fn sin_cos_tan_basic_values() {
+        // sin(0) = 0, cos(0) = 1, tan(0) = 0
+        match sin(&[n(0.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 0.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        match cos(&[n(0.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 1.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        match tan(&[n(0.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 0.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+
+        // sin(π/2) ≈ 1, cos(π/2) ≈ 0
+        let half_pi = std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2;
+        match sin(&[n(half_pi)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 1.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        match cos(&[n(half_pi)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(v.abs() < 1e-9),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+
+        // sin(π) ≈ 0, cos(π) ≈ -1
+        match sin(&[n(std::f64::consts::PI)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(v.abs() < 1e-9),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        match cos(&[n(std::f64::consts::PI)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, -1.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+    }

+    #[test]
+    fn atan2_basic_quadrants() {
+        // Excel arg order: ATAN2(x, y). Internally we call y.atan2(x).
+        // atan2(1, 0) → angle pointing along +x → 0
+        match atan2(&[n(1.0), n(0.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 0.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        // atan2(0, 1) → angle pointing along +y → π/2
+        match atan2(&[n(0.0), n(1.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        // atan2(-1, 0) → π (along -x)
+        match atan2(&[n(-1.0), n(0.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, std::f64::consts::PI)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        // atan2(1, 1) → π/4
+        match atan2(&[n(1.0), n(1.0)]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_4)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn atan2_zero_zero_is_div_zero() {
+        // Excel canon: ATAN2(0, 0) → #DIV/0!. Rust's f64::atan2(0,0)
+        // would return 0.0; we check up-front.
+        assert_eq!(atan2(&[n(0.0), n(0.0)]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn trig_arity_errors() {
+        // 0 args or 2+ args → #VALUE! for the single-arg fns.
+        let single_arg: [fn(&[Value]) -> Value; 6] = [sin, cos, tan, asin, acos, atan];
+        for f in single_arg.iter() {
+            assert_eq!(f(&[]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value));
+            assert_eq!(f(&[n(0.0), n(1.0)]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value));
+        }
+        // ATAN2: 0, 1, or 3+ args → #VALUE!.
+        assert_eq!(atan2(&[]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value));
+        assert_eq!(atan2(&[n(1.0)]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value));
+        assert_eq!(
+            atan2(&[n(1.0), n(1.0), n(1.0)]),
+            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value)
+        );
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn trig_error_propagation() {
+        // Any Error arg → that error.
+        let err = Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
+        let single_arg: [fn(&[Value]) -> Value; 6] = [sin, cos, tan, asin, acos, atan];
+        for f in single_arg.iter() {
+            assert_eq!(
+                f(std::slice::from_ref(&err)),
+                Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero)
+            );
+        }
+        assert_eq!(
+            atan2(&[err.clone(), n(1.0)]),
+            Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero)
+        );
+        assert_eq!(atan2(&[n(1.0), err]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn trig_text_arg_is_value_error() {
+        // Module canon (line 17): Text → #VALUE! for numeric-context
+        // aggregates and math fns. `coerce_numeric` runs Text through
+        // `to_number_strict` which rejects it. Matches SIN/COS/TAN
+        // behavior in Excel for non-numeric text.
+        let bad = Value::Text(std::sync::Arc::from("hello"));
+        assert_eq!(
+            sin(std::slice::from_ref(&bad)),
+            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value)
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            cos(std::slice::from_ref(&bad)),
+            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value)
+        );
+        assert_eq!(atan2(&[bad, n(1.0)]), Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value));
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn trig_blank_coerces_to_zero() {
+        // Blank → 0 per the existing one_number / coerce_numeric convention.
+        match sin(&[Value::Blank]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 0.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+        match cos(&[Value::Blank]) {
+            Value::Number(v) => assert!(approx(v, 1.0)),
+            _ => panic!(),
+        }
+    }
 }

   986	    fn build_range_supplemental(&self, dirty_vec: &[NodeId]) -> HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> {
   987	        // Resolve cell addresses for every dirty FORMULA node. Non-
   988	        // formula NodeIds (none exist today — the graph only adds Cell

   990	        // returning None.

   999	            if ranges.is_empty() {
  1000	                continue;
  1001	            }

  1007	                    }

That leaves stale graph range registrations after a formula is cleared, which can re-dirty a non-formula cell later. I’m verifying the runtime impact and whether tests cover it.

  1567	    // lookup + precision-check at the graph layer (W3-5). Phase 3.3
  1568	    // wires it: writes to a cell propagate dirty to the formulas that
  1569	    // depend on that cell (directly or via range), and re-bind cleans
  1570	    // session-side state cleanly.
  1571	    // ----------------------------------------------------------------
  1572	
  1573	    /// Helper: build a workbook with a single named-range formula. The
  1574	    /// named range covers `MyRange` for rows in `range_rows`, and a
  1575	    /// SUM-over-`MyRange` formula sits at `formula_cell`.
  1576	    fn workbook_with_named_range_sum(
  1577	        range_start_row: RowId,
  1578	        range_end_row: RowId,
  1579	        formula_cell: (RowId, ColId),
  1580	    ) -> Workbook {
  1581	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  1582	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1583	        wb.set_name(
  1584	            "MyRange",
  1585	            ql_storage::NamedTarget::Range(Range {
  1586	                sheet: 0,
  1587	                start_row: range_start_row,
  1588	                end_row: range_end_row,
  1589	                start_col: 0,
  1590	                end_col: 0,
  1591	            }),
  1592	        )
  1593	        .unwrap();
  1594	        wb.put_at(0, formula_cell.0, formula_cell.1, Value::Blank);
  1595	        wb.put_formula(0, formula_cell.0, formula_cell.1, "SUM(MyRange)");
  1596	        wb
  1597	    }
  1598	
  1599	    /// DIR-3-01: a write to a cell INSIDE a formula's named-range
  1600	    /// dependency marks that formula dirty. The basic happy path —
  1601	    /// stripe lookup hits and the precision check passes.
  1602	    #[test]
  1603	    fn dir_3_01_write_inside_range_dirties_dependent() {
  1604	        // MyRange = A1:A1000; formula =SUM(MyRange) at B1.
  1605	        let wb = workbook_with_named_range_sum(0, 999, (0, 1));
  1606	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1607	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1608	        let mut s = r.session;
  1609	        let formula_node = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  1610	        assert!(s.dirty_formulas().is_empty(), "rebuild leaves clean state");
  1611	
  1612	        // Write to A500 — inside the range. Formula should dirty.
  1613	        s.on_set_value(0, 500, 0);
  1614	        assert!(s.is_dirty(formula_node), "SUM(MyRange) marked dirty");
  1615	        assert_eq!(s.dirty_formulas().len(), 1);
  1616	
  1617	        // take_dirty atomically claims + clears.
  1618	        let taken = s.take_dirty();
  1619	        assert!(taken.contains(&formula_node));
  1620	        assert!(s.dirty_formulas().is_empty(), "take_dirty clears the set");
  1621	    }
  1622	
  1623	    /// DIR-3-02: a write OUTSIDE a formula's range does NOT dirty,
  1624	    /// even though the stripe-level lookup would match (precision
  1625	    /// check filters the false positive). MyRange = A1:A10; write to
  1626	    /// A500 hits Column 0 stripe but A500 is not in [A1:A10].
  1627	    #[test]
  1628	    fn dir_3_02_write_outside_range_filtered_by_precision_check() {
  1629	        let wb = workbook_with_named_range_sum(0, 9, (0, 1));
  1630	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1631	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1632	        let mut s = r.session;
  1633	        let formula_node = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  1634	
  1635	        // Write to A500 — outside [A1:A10]. Stripe matches Column 0,
  1636	        // but precision check drops it.
  1637	        s.on_set_value(0, 500, 0);
  1638	        assert!(
  1639	            !s.is_dirty(formula_node),
  1640	            "A500 outside [A1:A10] must not dirty SUM(MyRange)"
  1641	        );
  1642	        assert!(s.dirty_formulas().is_empty());
  1643	
  1644	        // Sanity: a write INSIDE [A1:A10] still works.
  1645	        s.on_set_value(0, 5, 0);
  1880	
  1881	    /// `on_clear_formula` removes the cleared formula from the dirty
  1882	    /// set AND propagates dirty to its downstream dependents.
  1883	    #[test]
  1884	    fn clear_formula_evicts_self_and_dirties_downstream() {
  1885	        // C1 = A1 + B1; D1 = C1 + 1. Clearing C1 dirties D1 but not C1.
  1886	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  1887	        wb.add_sheet("S0");
  1888	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0));
  1889	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Number(2.0));
  1890	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 2, Value::Blank);
  1891	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 3, Value::Blank);
  1892	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 2, "A1 + B1");
  1893	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 3, "C1 + 1");
  1894	
  1895	        let r = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(&wb);
  1896	        assert!(r.is_complete());
  1897	        let mut s = r.session;
  1898	        let c1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 2).unwrap();
  1899	        let d1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 3).unwrap();
  1900	
  1901	        // Pre-mark C1 dirty (simulating a prior pending recompute).
  1902	        s.dirty.insert(c1);
  1903	        s.dirty.insert(d1);
  1904	
  1905	        s.on_clear_formula(0, 0, 2);
  1906	        // C1 evicted (it no longer computes).
  1907	        assert!(!s.is_dirty(c1));
  1908	        // D1 still in dirty (we re-marked it via mark_dirty_from_cell_write
  1909	        // since the C1 value changed).
  1910	        assert!(s.is_dirty(d1));
  1911	    }
  1912	
  1913	    /// Phase 3.3 + Phase 3.2 interplay: re-binding a formula evicts
  1914	    /// stale cell deps from `cell_to_formulas`, so writes to old deps
  1915	    /// no longer fire dirty marks.
  1916	    #[test]
  1917	    fn rebind_clears_cell_reverse_index_for_old_deps() {
  1918	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  1919	        // Plan v1: =A1 — depends on A1 only.
  1920	        let plan_v1 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  1921	            sheet: 0,
  1922	            row: 0,
  1923	            col: 0,
  1924	            abs_col: false,
  1925	            abs_row: false,
  1926	        };
  1927	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v1);
  1928	        let node = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  1929	        assert_eq!(s.cell_dep_count(), 1, "A1 indexed");
  1930	
  1931	        // Plan v2: =B1 — depends on B1 only. A1 reverse-index entry
  1932	        // must be evicted by `remove_formula_deps`.
  1933	        let plan_v2 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  1934	            sheet: 0,
  1935	            row: 0,
  1936	            col: 1,
  1937	            abs_col: false,
  1938	            abs_row: false,
  1939	        };
  1940	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v2);
  1941	        assert_eq!(s.cell_dep_count(), 1, "only B1 now");
  1942	
  1943	        // Take dirty (clear residue from the two on_set_formula calls).
  1944	        s.take_dirty();
  1945	
  1946	        // Write to A1 — formula must NOT dirty (no longer a dep).
  1947	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);
  1948	        assert!(
  1949	            !s.is_dirty(node),
  1950	            "stale A1 dep should not fire after re-bind"
  1951	        );
  1952	
  1953	        // Write to B1 — fires.
  1954	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 1);
  1955	        assert!(s.is_dirty(node));
  1956	    }
  1957	
  1958	    // ----------------------------------------------------------------
  1959	    // W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure (Phase 4 pre-V2).
  1960	    //
  1961	    // Phase 3.10 megaudit H3 + H4 found that re-binding a formula did
  1962	    // NOT clear the Phase 0 `Graph`'s forward edges or stripe entries
  1963	    // — creating false `#CIRC!` cycles (H3) and false-positive dirty
  1964	    // marks (H4). W5-50 wires `Graph::clear_outgoing` +
  1965	    // `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` into
  1966	    // `extract_and_register_deps` so the graph is wholesale revoked
  1967	    // before re-registration.
  1968	    //
  1969	    // The graph-level revocation API has its own tests in
  1970	    // `ql-calcgraph::graph::tests`. These tests verify the SESSION
  1971	    // wiring: `on_set_formula` → `extract_and_register_deps` actually
  1972	    // invokes the new clears.
  1973	    // ----------------------------------------------------------------
  1974	
  1975	    /// W5-50: on rebind, the graph's `outgoing[formula]` reflects the
  1976	    /// new dep set, not the union of old + new.
  1977	    #[test]
  1978	    fn rebind_clears_graph_outgoing_edges() {
  1979	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  1980	        // Add A1 and B1 as formula cells so cell_index has nodes for
  1981	        // them (extract_and_register_deps only adds forward edges to
  1982	        // dep cells that already have nodes).
  1983	        let _a1_node = s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
  1984	        let _b1_node = s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
  1985	        // F at (0,5,5) initially depends on A1.
  1986	        let plan_v1 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  1987	            sheet: 0,
  1988	            row: 0,
  1989	            col: 0,
  1990	            abs_col: false,
  1991	            abs_row: false,
  1992	        };
  1993	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v1);
  1994	        let f = s.cell_node_for(0, 5, 5).unwrap();
  1995	        let a1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 0).unwrap();
  1996	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 1, 0).unwrap();
  1997	        assert_eq!(s.graph().outgoing(f), &[a1], "initial bind adds F→A1 edge");
  1998	        assert_eq!(s.graph().incoming(a1), &[f]);
  1999	
  2000	        // Rebind F to depend on B1 instead.
  2001	        let plan_v2 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  2002	            sheet: 0,
  2003	            row: 1,
  2004	            col: 0,
  2005	            abs_col: false,
  2006	            abs_row: false,
  2007	        };
  2008	        s.on_set_formula(0, 5, 5, &plan_v2);
  2009	
  2010	        // Graph-level outgoing now reflects ONLY the new dep.
  2011	        assert_eq!(
  2012	            s.graph().outgoing(f),
  2013	            &[b1],
  2014	            "rebind clears stale F→A1, adds F→B1"
  2015	        );
  2016	        // Symmetric: A1 no longer has F as a dependent.
  2017	        assert!(
  2018	            s.graph().incoming(a1).is_empty(),
  2019	            "A1's back-pointer to F removed"
  2020	        );
  2021	        assert_eq!(s.graph().incoming(b1), &[f]);
  2022	    }
  2023	
  2024	    /// W5-50 / megaudit H3 — session-level acceptance: rebind a formula
  2025	    /// to a constant clears outgoing edges so when a later edit points
  2026	    /// the old dep at the formula, the graph state does NOT form a
  2027	    /// false `A1 ↔ B1` cycle.
  2028	    ///
  2029	    ///   1. B1 = "=A1"           → graph: B1→A1
  2030	    ///   2. B1 = "=1" (rebind)   → graph: B1's outgoing cleared
  2031	    ///   3. A1 = "=B1"           → graph: A1→B1
  2032	    ///
  2033	    /// Tarjan operating over the dirty set `{A1, B1}` must emit a
  2034	    /// 2-node SORTED schedule (B1 then A1), not a 2-node CYCLED one.
  2035	    /// We drive Tarjan directly here because the runtime's natural
  2036	    /// dirty fanout doesn't queue both nodes — the rebind itself
  2037	    /// doesn't dirty A1, and A1's new formula only dirties downstream
  2038	    /// of A1 (which is empty in this minimal setup). The scheduler
  2039	    /// contract is what matters for the bug: given a hypothetical
  2040	    /// dirty set covering both, it must not fabricate a cycle.
  2041	    #[test]
  2042	    fn h3_no_false_circ_after_rebind_to_constant() {
  2043	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  2044	        s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 0); // A1 pre-exists as a node
  2045	        s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 1); // B1 pre-exists as a node
  2046	
  2047	        // Step 1: B1 = "=A1"
  2048	        let plan_b1_v1 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  2049	            sheet: 0,
  2050	            row: 0,
  2051	            col: 0,
  2052	            abs_col: false,
  2053	            abs_row: false,
  2054	        };
  2055	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 1, &plan_b1_v1);
  2056	
  2057	        // Step 2: B1 = "=1" — constant, no deps. clear_outgoing fires.
  2058	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 1, &ExprPlan::Number(1.0));
  2059	
  2060	        // Step 3: A1 = "=B1"
  2061	        let plan_a1 = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  2062	            sheet: 0,
  2063	            row: 0,
  2064	            col: 1,
  2065	            abs_col: false,
  2066	            abs_row: false,
  2067	        };
  2068	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 0, &plan_a1);
  2069	
  2070	        let a1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 0).unwrap();
  2071	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  2072	
  2073	        // Graph state sanity: B1 has NO outgoing edges (rebind cleared
  2074	        // the stale B1→A1); A1→B1 was added by step 3.
  2075	        assert!(s.graph().outgoing(b1).is_empty(), "B1's outgoing cleared");
  2076	        assert_eq!(s.graph().outgoing(a1), &[b1]);
  2077	
  2078	        // Tarjan over the hypothetical dirty set {A1, B1}: must emit
  2079	        // SORTED, not CYCLED. Pre-W5-50 this would have been CYCLED
  2080	        // because outgoing[B1] still contained A1.
  2081	        let sched = ql_calcgraph::schedule(s.graph(), &[a1, b1]);
  2082	        assert!(
  2083	            sched.cycled.is_empty(),
  2084	            "H3 closure: no false #CIRC! after rebind. cycled={:?}",
  2085	            sched.cycled
  2086	        );
  2087	        assert_eq!(sched.sorted, vec![b1, a1], "dep-first: B1 then A1");
  2088	    }
  2089	
  2090	    /// W5-50 / megaudit H4 — session-level acceptance: rebinding a
  2091	    /// range-dep formula from SUM(A:A) to SUM(B:B) clears the Column A
  2092	    /// stripe entry, so writes to A5 no longer falsely dirty B1.
  2093	    ///
  2094	    /// The plan-level `AggregateNameRef` carries a `Range` (from
  2095	    /// `ql_types`) which `range_to_rangeref` (line 101) maps to the
  2096	    /// appropriate `RangeRef` variant — `start_row: 0, end_row: MAX`
  2097	    /// becomes `WholeColumn`.
  2098	    #[test]
  2099	    fn h4_rebind_clears_stale_range_stripe_dirty() {
  2100	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  2101	        let _b1_node = s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
  2102	
  2103	        // Step 1: B1 = SUM(Sales_A) where Sales_A → A:A.
  2104	        // Construct a whole-column-A range (start_row=0, end_row=MAX).
  2105	        let sales_a = ql_types::Range {
  2106	            sheet: 0,
  2107	            start_row: 0,
  2108	            start_col: 0,
  2109	            end_row: ql_types::RowId::MAX,
  2110	            end_col: 0,
  2111	        };
  2112	        let plan_v1 = ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
  2113	            name: Arc::from("SALES_A"),
  2114	            range: sales_a,
  2115	        };
  2116	        let plan_v1_wrapped = ExprPlan::Function {
  2117	            name: Arc::from("SUM"),
  2118	            args: vec![plan_v1],
  2119	        };
  2120	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 1, &plan_v1_wrapped);
  2121	        let b1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).unwrap();
  2122	        assert_eq!(
  2123	            s.graph().stripe_index().stripe_count(),
  2124	            1,
  2125	            "Column A stripe registered"
  2126	        );
  2127	
  2128	        // Step 2: rebind to SUM(Sales_B) where Sales_B → B:B.
  2129	        let sales_b = ql_types::Range {
  2130	            sheet: 0,
  2131	            start_row: 0,
  2132	            start_col: 1,
  2133	            end_row: ql_types::RowId::MAX,
  2134	            end_col: 1,
  2135	        };
  2136	        let plan_v2 = ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
  2137	            name: Arc::from("SALES_B"),
  2138	            range: sales_b,
  2139	        };
  2140	        let plan_v2_wrapped = ExprPlan::Function {
  2141	            name: Arc::from("SUM"),
  2142	            args: vec![plan_v2],
  2143	        };
  2144	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 1, &plan_v2_wrapped);
  2145	
  2146	        // Stripe map: ONLY Column B now (Column A was cleared).
  2147	        assert_eq!(s.graph().stripe_index().stripe_count(), 1);
  2148	        assert!(
  2149	            s.graph().dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0).is_empty(),
  2150	            "A5 write must NOT find B1 — Column A stripe revoked"
  2151	        );
  2152	        assert_eq!(
  2153	            s.graph().dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 1),
  2154	            vec![b1],
  2155	            "B5 write finds B1 via new Column B stripe"
  2156	        );
  2157	
  2158	        // Step 3: simulate write to A5 — B1 must not dirty via the
  2159	        // stale stripe.
  2160	        s.take_dirty(); // clear residue from the on_set_formula calls
  2161	        s.on_set_value(0, 5, 0);
  2162	        assert!(
  2163	            !s.is_dirty(b1),
  2164	            "H4 closure: stale Column A stripe must not fire"
  2165	        );
  2300	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2301	        assert!(
  2302	            sched.cycled.is_empty(),
  2303	            "no cycle, cycled={:?}",
  2304	            sched.cycled
  2305	        );
  2306	        assert_eq!(sched.sorted, vec![b1, a1], "B1 before A1 (dep-first)");
  2307	    }
  2308	
  2309	    /// W5-50 / self-range cycle: `A1 = "=SUM(A1:A1)"`. Single dirty
  2310	    /// formula whose range covers itself. Supplemental injects A1 → A1.
  2311	    /// Tarjan emits A1 in `cycled`.
  2312	    #[test]
  2313	    fn self_range_cycle_via_supplemental() {
  2314	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  2315	        s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
  2316	
  2317	        // A1 = SUM(A1:A1)
  2318	        let a1_range = ql_types::Range {
  2319	            sheet: 0,
  2320	            start_row: 0,
  2321	            start_col: 0,
  2322	            end_row: 0,
  2323	            end_col: 0,
  2324	        };
  2325	        let plan_a1 = ExprPlan::Function {
  2326	            name: Arc::from("SUM"),
  2327	            args: vec![ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
  2328	                name: Arc::from("X"),
  2329	                range: a1_range,
  2330	            }],
  2331	        };
  2332	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 0, &plan_a1);
  2333	
  2334	        // Force A1 dirty: write to A1's cell (self-fanout via stripe).
  2335	        s.take_dirty();
  2336	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 0);
  2337	
  2338	        let a1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 0).unwrap();
  2339	        assert!(s.is_dirty(a1));
  2340	
  2341	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2342	        assert!(sched.sorted.is_empty(), "self-cycle, sorted empty");
  2343	        assert_eq!(sched.cycled, vec![a1], "self-range cycle detected");
  2344	    }
  2345	
  2346	    /// W5-50 — partial dirty: B1 in A1's range is a LITERAL (not a
  2347	    /// formula → no NodeId). Supplemental builder must NOT push a
  2348	    /// (non-existent) temp edge. A1 evaluates against current B1 value
  2349	    /// as the existing contract requires.
  2350	    #[test]
  2351	    fn partial_dirty_literal_in_range_no_supplemental() {
  2352	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  2353	        s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
  2354	        // B1 is NOT a formula cell — no node added.
  2355	
  2356	        // A1 = SUM(B1:B1) where B1 is a literal.
  2357	        let b1_range = ql_types::Range {
  2358	            sheet: 0,
  2359	            start_row: 0,
  2360	            start_col: 1,
  2361	            end_row: 0,
  2362	            end_col: 1,
  2363	        };
  2364	        let plan_a1 = ExprPlan::Function {
  2365	            name: Arc::from("SUM"),
  2366	            args: vec![ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
  2367	                name: Arc::from("X"),
  2368	                range: b1_range,
  2369	            }],
  2370	        };
  2371	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 0, &plan_a1);
  2372	
  2373	        // Force A1 into dirty by writing to its own cell (no
  2374	        // dependents exist for A1 since it's the only formula).
  2375	        s.take_dirty();
  2376	        s.on_set_value(0, 0, 1); // write to literal B1 — fanout dirties A1 via stripe
  2377	
  2378	        let a1 = s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 0).unwrap();
  2379	        assert!(s.is_dirty(a1), "A1 dirty via Column B stripe");
  2380	        assert!(
  2381	            s.cell_node_for(0, 0, 1).is_none(),
  2382	            "B1 has no node (literal)"
  2383	        );
  2384	
  2385	        let sched = s.schedule_dirty();
  2386	        assert_eq!(
  2387	            sched.sorted,
  2388	            vec![a1],
  2389	            "A1 alone in sorted — no supplemental edge to non-formula B1"
  2390	        );
  2391	        assert!(sched.cycled.is_empty());
  2392	    }
  2393	
  2394	    /// W5-50: re-binding to the SAME plan is a no-op for graph state
  2395	    /// (same edges, same stripes). Determinism check.
  2396	    #[test]
  2397	    fn rebind_to_same_plan_yields_same_graph_state() {
  2398	        let mut s = CalcgraphSession::new();
  2399	        s.or_insert_cell_node(0, 0, 0); // A1
  2400	
  2401	        let plan = ExprPlan::CellRef {
  2402	            sheet: 0,
  2403	            row: 0,
  2404	            col: 0,
  2405	            abs_col: false,
  2406	            abs_row: false,
  2407	        };
  2408	        s.on_set_formula(0, 0, 1, &plan);

   887	        // The session lives behind an `Option<&'a mut CalcgraphSession>`
   888	        // — take it out for the duration of this call so we can borrow

   895	        // model the dep direction (`outgoing(F) = what F depends on`).
   896	        // Tarjan emits SCCs in reverse-topo of the condensation
   897	        // which, for our edge orientation, is dependency-first order.

   910	        // optimization would pay off.
   911	        let mut simd_classified: usize = 0;
   912

   916	        //     touch anything. Snapshotted once, used as the equality
   917	        //     baseline.
   918	        //   - `originally_dirty`: NodeIds that were in the dirty set
   919	        //     coming in to this call. Used to distinguish "top-level
   920	        //     dirty" (came from an external edit; must re-eval) from
   921	        //     "downstream dirty" (only here because an upstream
   922	        //     formula was dirty; check if upstream actually changed).
   923	        //   - `changed`: addresses whose value differs from `prior`
   924	        //     after this pass. Built as we go; downstream formulas
   925	        //     check membership to decide whether to re-eval.
   926	        use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
   927	        let mut prior: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), Value> = HashMap::new();
   928	        let mut originally_dirty: HashSet<ql_calcgraph::NodeId> = HashSet::new();
   929	        for n in sched.sorted.iter().chain(sched.cycled.iter()) {
   930	            originally_dirty.insert(*n);
   931	            if let Some(addr) = session.cell_address_for(*n) {
   932	                prior.insert(
   933	                    addr,
   934	                    self.workbook
   935	                        .read(ql_types::Address::new(addr.0, addr.1, addr.2)),
   936	                );
   937	            }
   938	        }
   939	        let mut changed: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   940	
   941	        // Cycled nodes get `#CIRC!` regardless of whether the formula
   942	        // still binds. Spec: cycles are terminal Phase 0 errors. Phase
   943	        // 3.5: `#CIRC!` is a FORMULA-output value (the formula's
   944	        // resolution), so it routes to the computed overlay. Phase
   945	        // 3.8: still apply value-equality (the cycle might have been
   946	        // pre-existing → `#CIRC!` matches prior `#CIRC!`; skip write).
   947	        let circ = Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ);
   948	        for node in &sched.cycled {
   949	            let Some((sheet, row, col)) = session.cell_address_for(*node) else {
   950	                continue;
   951	            };
   952	            let prior_val = prior.get(&(sheet, row, col));
   953	            if prior_val == Some(&circ) {
   954	                skipped_value_equality += 1;
   955	            } else {
   956	                self.workbook.put_computed_at(sheet, row, col, circ.clone());
   957	                changed.insert((sheet, row, col));
   958	            }
   959	        }
   960	
   961	        // Sorted nodes evaluate in dependency-first order.
   962	        for node in &sched.sorted {
   963	            let Some((sheet, row, col)) = session.cell_address_for(*node) else {
   964	                continue;
   965	            };
   966	            let Some(text) = self.workbook.formula_at(sheet, row, col).cloned() else {
   967	                continue;
   968	            };
   969	
   970	            // Phase 3.8: VEQ decision — does any upstream of `node`

     1	# Graph storage architecture decision — GAP-G-01 + GAP-G-03
     2	
     3	**Status:** **DECISION MADE 2026-05-13 (W5-49).** Implementation queued for the next session(s). NOT shipped.
     4	**Author:** Claude Opus 4.7 (W5-49) + Codex (gpt-5.5, independent read-only review).
     5	**Affects:** `crates/ql-calcgraph/` (Graph, StripeIndex, AdjacencyVectors, Tarjan scheduler) + `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` (extract_and_register_deps wiring).
     6	**Blocks:** Phase 4.3 V2 (SUMIF / COUNTIF / SUMIFS / AVERAGEIF / VLOOKUP / HLOOKUP / INDEX / MATCH / CHOOSE — the range-aware function batch).
     7	**Precondition for:** Phase 4.3 V2 batch, Phase 4.7 array formulas (FormulaRegion binder rests on Option A's revocation API).
     8	**Companion docs:**
     9	- `docs/audits/2026-05-12-phase-3-megaudit.md` — origin of the issue (Phase 3.10 megaudit findings H1, H3, H4).
    10	- `docs/audits/2026-05-13-session-handoff.md` — the W5-47 framing with three options.
    11	- `docs/audits/2026-05-13-deep-audit-closure.md` — W5-48 honesty patches that left the architectural decision pending.
    12	- `docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt` — the full Codex review that informed this doc.
    13	- `docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md` — current calcgraph integration surface; will be updated after implementation closes G-01 + G-03.
    14	
    15	## Decision
    16	
    17	**Option A + a tightly-scoped Option C. Defer Option B (FormulaRegion binder) to Phase 4.7.**
    18	
    19	GAP-G-01 (rebind staleness) and GAP-G-03 (range deps not scheduler edges) are independent correctness failures with independent fixes. They ship as one architectural change because they share the gate that blocks Phase 4.3 V2. Implementation order is **A before C** — Option C reads `formula_to_range_deps`, which Option A makes current.
    20	
    21	- **Option A** — Add per-formula revocation API to `Graph` + `StripeIndex`. Reverse-index stripe membership so per-formula clear is `O(stripes-this-formula-is-in)` rather than scanning the whole stripe map. Call the new APIs in `extract_and_register_deps` at the same point as the existing `remove_formula_deps`. Keeps `Vec<Vec<NodeId>>` adjacency, no new graph library, no `IndexSet`/`petgraph` dep, no edge-epoch metadata.
    22	- **Option C (small variant)** — At recompute time, before invoking Tarjan, build a temporary supplemental adjacency: for each dirty formula `F` with range deps, for each OTHER dirty FORMULA node `G`, if `G`'s cell address is contained in one of `F`'s ranges, push `G` into `supplemental[F]`. Pass the map to a new `topo::schedule_with_supplemental` variant. Discard the map after the scheduler returns.
    23	- **Option B (FormulaRegion binder + region detection)** is the right design for Phase 4.7 array formulas + Phase 4.7 SIMD region dispatch (GAP-G-02), but the wrong tool for Phase 4.3 V2: region-detection-at-write is its own correctness surface, and SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup functions don't need region semantics — just correctly-ordered range-aware recompute. Folding G-01/G-03 fixes into B couples them to a much larger Phase 4.7 redesign that doesn't need to ship first.
    24	
    25	## The two bugs (problem statement)
    26	
    27	### GAP-G-01 — rebind staleness (megaudit H3 + H4; correctness)
    28	
    29	`Graph` (`crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs`) uses append-only `AdjacencyVectors` (`crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs:32` — `Vec<Vec<NodeId>>` outgoing + incoming). `Graph::add_edge` (graph.rs:163) and `Graph::register_range_dependency` (graph.rs:180-211) only append. The session-side `remove_formula_deps` (`calcgraph_session.rs:525-545`) cleans the four session-side reverse indices (`formula_deps`, `name_to_formulas`, `cell_to_formulas`, `volatile_formulas`) but explicit comment at line 522-524:
    30	
    31	> Does NOT touch the Phase 0 `Graph`'s stripe state or edges (append-only contract).
    32	
    33	Concrete failure (megaudit H3):
    34	
    35	```
    36	1. set_formula(B1, "=A1")  → outgoing[B1] = [A1]
    37	2. set_formula(B1, "=1")   → outgoing[B1] still [A1] (stale)
    38	3. set_formula(A1, "=B1")  → outgoing[A1] = [B1] (new), outgoing[B1] still [A1] (stale)
    39	4. recompute_dirty         → Tarjan sees A1 → B1 → A1 → cycle → both emit #CIRC!
    40	```
    41	
    42	The cycle is FALSE: B1 no longer depends on A1, A1 depends on B1, the system should compute A1 = current(B1) = 1.
    43	
    44	Megaudit H4 is the range-side variant:
    45	
    46	```
    47	1. set_formula(B1, "=SUM(A:A)")   → Column-A stripe ∋ B1, formula_to_range_deps[B1] = [A:A]
    48	2. set_formula(B1, "=SUM(B:B)")   → Column-B stripe ∋ B1 (new), Column-A stripe still ∋ B1 (stale), formula_to_range_deps[B1] = [A:A, B:B] (stale carries over)
    49	3. set_value(A5, 42)              → dependents_for_cell(A, 5) finds B1 via stale Column-A stripe; precision check passes against stale A:A → B1 falsely dirty
    50	4. recompute_dirty                → B1 recomputes even though it no longer reads Column A
    51	```
    52	
    53	The cost is mostly false-positive dirty work plus the megaudit H3-style false-cycle interaction.
    54	
    55	### GAP-G-03 — range deps not scheduler edges (megaudit H1; correctness)
    56	
    57	`register_range_dependency` populates `StripeIndex` + `formula_to_range_deps` but never calls `add_edge`. Tarjan in `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs:151` walks `graph.outgoing(v)` only. So:
    58	
    59	```
    60	A1 = "=SUM(B1:B1)"   → register_range_dependency(A1, B1:B1, sheet)
    61	B1 = "=A1+1"         → add_edge(B1, A1)
    62	```
    63	
    64	The graph holds `outgoing[B1] = [A1]` (real edge) but `outgoing[A1] = []` (no edge to B1; range dep is in the stripe index, not the edge list). Tarjan's input graph cannot see the cycle. Megaudit H1 also calls out the non-cycle ordering bug:
    65	
    66	```
    67	A1 = "=SUM(B1:B1)"   (depends on B1 via range)
    68	B1 = "=A2 + 1"       (depends on A2 directly)
    69	edit A2              → both A1 and B1 fan out dirty (B1 via cell_to_formulas, A1 via Column-B stripe)
    70	recompute_dirty      → Tarjan sees only B1 → A2 explicit edge; A1's range dep on B1 invisible
    71	                     → A1 may evaluate before B1, reading stale B1
    72	```
    73	
    74	## Options considered
    75	
    76	| Option | What | Closes | Cost | Verdict |
    77	|---|---|---|---|---|
    78	| **A** — Delta-edge graph storage | `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)` + `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula(node)` backed by reverse index `formula_to_stripe_keys`. Wire into `extract_and_register_deps`. | GAP-G-01 directly. | ~1 strong session per Codex (W5-47 over-estimated as 3-5 days). | **SHIP**. |
    79	| **C** (small) — Tarjan supplemental adjacency | At recompute, build `supplemental: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>` of temp F→G edges (G dirty formula in F's range). Pass to `schedule_with_supplemental`. Discard after. | GAP-G-03 directly. | ~0.5-1 session. | **SHIP after A**. |
    80	| **B** — FormulaRegion binder + region detection at write-time | New graph vertex shape; range deps become FormulaRegion edges natively. SIMD dispatch flows from same node. | Most of G-01 + G-03 + GAP-G-02 (perf). | 1-2 weeks; region detection at write is its own correctness surface. | **DEFER to Phase 4.7** (array formulas). |
    81	| D (epoch-marked edges) | Tag edges with formula epoch; filter at scheduler time. | G-01. | Permanent memory overhead + filter cost on every traversal. | Rejected by Codex. |
    82	| D (IndexSet adjacency) | Switch outgoing/incoming to `Vec<IndexSet<NodeId>>`. | Removes the "linear scan to remove back-pointer" concern. | Adds a workspace dep + changes memory profile. | Marginal; not worth the dep. |
    83	| D (rebuild from plans every recompute) | Re-extract dep graph from session-held plans each recompute. | G-01 + G-03. | Pays bind/extract on the recompute hot path. | Rejected — defeats incremental. |
    84

    86	
    87	Sequenced strictly A before C. Each phase is its own commit. Land both phases before starting Phase 4.3 V2.

    89	### Phase A.1 — `StripeIndex` reverse lookup
    90	
    91	File: `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/stripes.rs`
    92	
    93	- Add field `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>`.
    94	- Update the existing `insert(key, formula_node)` (stripes.rs:194) — when the per-stripe `HashSet::insert(formula_node)` returns `true` (newly inserted), also append `key` to `formula_to_stripe_keys[formula_node]`. Preserves the existing dedup semantics: re-registering a `(formula, range)` doesn't bump `stripe_inserts` and doesn't double-add to the reverse vector.
    95	- Add `clear_for_formula(formula_node)` — iterates the reverse vector, removes `formula_node` from each stripe's set (with empty-bucket pruning matching the existing inverse pattern), drops the reverse-vector entry.
    96	- Tests: clear removes the formula from all stripes; subsequent re-register works post-clear; `stripe_count` + `total_insertions` stay consistent; idempotent (clear twice is no-op).
    97	
    98	### Phase A.2 — `Graph` revocation API
    99	
   100	Files: `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs` + `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs`
   101	
   102	- `AdjacencyVectors::clear_outgoing(from) -> Vec<NodeId>` (new): `std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[from.index()])` and return the drained list to the caller.
   103	- `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)`: calls the above, then for each former target `t` does `self.edges.incoming[t.index()].retain(|&n| n != node)`. Bumps `revision` once.
   104	- `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)`: removes `node` from `formula_to_range_deps`, calls `self.stripes.clear_for_formula(node)`. Bumps `revision` once.
   105	- Tests: symmetric invariant preserved (`outgoing[u] ∋ v ⟺ incoming[v] ∋ u`); revision bumps correctly; idempotent; clear preserves OTHER formulas' edges.

   166	## Codex review summary
   167	
   168	Codex (gpt-5.5, read-only sandbox, ~10 min wall, 6083-line output) was dispatched W5-49 with a self-contained briefing including the three options + my preliminary view. The full review is at `docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt`. Key findings that shaped this decision:
   169	
   170	1. **Independence confirmed.** G-01 = stale stored graph/range state after rebind; G-03 = missing scheduler visibility for live range deps. Neither fix subsumes the other. Codex: "Fixing A does not make range deps scheduler edges; fixing C does not revoke stale direct edges or stale stripe/range registrations."
   171	2. **Positive interaction → A before C is mandatory.** Codex: "Option C must read the current `formula_to_range_deps`; Option A makes that map current by clearing stale entries before re-registering. Without A, C can amplify G-01 by scheduling against stale range deps."
   172	3. **Option C scope tightening (refinement Codex contributed):** temp edges only between **dirty formula nodes**, NOT arbitrary dirty cells. This matches the existing Tarjan contract at `topo.rs:40` ("Edges to nodes outside `dirty_subset` are not traversed") and avoids materializing edges to non-schedulable cells.
   173	4. **Option A shape confirmed.** Back-pointer removal from `incoming[target]` is required for the symmetric invariant; linear scan is acceptable because rebind happens at edit-rate, not recompute-rate. The `formula_to_stripe_keys` reverse index is essential — without it, per-formula stripe clear would be O(total_stripes) and a real scale risk. Bump `revision` once per clear, not per edge.
   174	5. **Option D variants rejected.** Epoch-marked edges keep stale memory + push correctness filtering into every scheduler traversal. `IndexSet` adds a dep and changes memory profile. Full rebuild from plans pays bind/extract on the hot path. Codex: "Best 'D' is a bounded variant of A+C."
   175	6. **Effort estimate revised DOWN.** W5-47 said 3-5 days for A. Codex: "The core A change is smaller because the session already has the rebind choke point... Option A: 1 strong session, maybe 2 if graph stats/revision expectations need cleanup. Option C: 0.5-1 session."
   176	
   177	## Stop conditions
   178	
   179	Revisit this decision if any of the following surface during or after implementation:
   180	
   181	- **Phase 4.3 V2 actually requires FormulaRegion for function SEMANTICS (not just perf).** None of SUMIF / COUNTIF / VLOOKUP / INDEX / MATCH / CHOOSE need region semantics — they're scalar functions over ranges. If a function in scope unexpectedly needs region-aligned eval (unlikely), pause and reconsider Option B.
   182	- **`formula_to_stripe_keys` memory unacceptable at scale.** Worst case: each formula carries one `StripeKey` (16 bytes) per stripe it depends on. For 1M formulas each depending on 1 range = 16MB overhead. Acceptable. For 1M formulas with avg 4 ranges = 64MB. Still acceptable. Revisit only if benchmarks show it's the bottleneck.
   183	- **Clearing back-pointers shows measurable edit-latency regression on high-fan-in sheets.** Worst case: rebind a formula whose old deps include a cell with N dependents; the `retain` scan is O(N) per former target. Typical worksheets have low fan-in; mitigate if observed by switching the affected targets' `incoming` to an `IndexSet` lazily.
   184	- **Option C cannot be made deterministic.** The supplemental-edge build order must be deterministic; iterating `dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` gives random order. Sort by `NodeId` before iterating, the same way `Graph::dependents_for_cell` does (graph.rs:251).
   185	
   186	## Implementation watch list
   187	
   188	From Codex's review, the regression suites should explicitly cover:
   189	
   190	- Stale direct-edge false cycles (H3 scenario).
   191	- Stale range dirtying after rebind (H4 scenario).
   192	- Range-induced cycles (H1 scenario #1).
   193	- Range-induced ordering (H1 scenario #2).
   194	- Self-range cycles (`A1 = SUM(A1:A1)`).
   195	- VEQ behavior for range-dep formulas (Phase 3.8 short-circuit + supplemental edges must compose).
   196	- "Same rebind same schedule order" determinism (covered in Phase A.4 + C.3).

Pass the map to a new `topo::schedule_with_supplemental` variant. Discard the map after the scheduler returns.

docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:65:2. **Is Option C correct?** Specifically: at recompute_dirty time, when we walk `formula_to_range_deps[F]` and inject temp edges to dirty cells in the range — are there cases where this produces a wrong cycle classification (e.g., a phantom cycle that wouldn't exist with proper edges)? The example A1 = SUM(B1:B1), B1 = A1+1: dirty set = {A1, B1}. Walk A1's range deps → B1 in range, B1 in dirty → temp edge A1→B1. Walk B1's deps → cell ref to A1 → real edge B1→A1. Tarjan sees A1→B1, B1→A1 → SCC of size 2 → cycled. Looks correct. But what about partial dirty sets? If only A1 is dirty and we're recomputing it because of a name change, B1 isn't dirty — the temp-edge pass adds nothing, Tarjan emits A1 alone, A1 evaluates against the CURRENT B1 value. Is that wrong? Excel canon: a name change should mark every dependent dirty. Phase 3.10 H2 fix made on_set_name BFS-fanout — so B1 would be dirty too via that fix. Reassure or refute this reasoning.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:67:3. **Is the Option A revocation API the smallest correct change to `Graph`?** Concretely: should `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` also walk each former target's `incoming[target]` to remove the back-pointer? (Yes, for symmetry — but is the linear scan acceptable? Most cell formulas have ≤4 outgoing edges so the per-rebind cost is `Σ over edges of O(in_degree(target))`. For a heavy DAG with high in-degree nodes that could be costly — but how often does rebind hit such nodes?) What about determinism — `Vec` preserves insertion order, but after `clear_outgoing + re-extract` the new edges may be in a different order if the binder pass yields a different walk order; is that observable downstream? (My read: Tarjan's output ordering depends on insertion order via dfs_stack scan order — so yes, observable. But re-extract from the SAME plan should produce the SAME walk order, so as long as Plan→deps extraction is deterministic, the new edge order matches the old.)

docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:6198:Option C is correct in principle, with one required precision detail: **the temp-edge range containment check must include sheet resolution**, not just `range_contains_rowcol(range, row, col)`, because that helper intentionally ignores sheet and relies on stripe prefiltering today.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:6212:**3. Option A API**
docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:6241:That lets C avoid mutating graph storage and keeps Tarjan reusable. But it is still basically Option C, just cleaner.
docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:6245:I think W5-47’s “3-5 days” is conservative. For direct G-01 revocation only, this is closer to **one focused session**:
docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt:6261:- Phase 4.3 V2 range functions require FormulaRegion for semantics, not just performance. I see no evidence they do; SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup need correct range dependencies, not region grouping.

    W5-50 / Engine Phase 4 pre-V2: GAP-G-01 + GAP-G-03 SHIPPED (Option A + small C)
    
    Implements the W5-49 architectural decision recorded at
    docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md. Two correctness
    gaps from the Phase 3.10 megaudit close: rebind staleness (H3/H4 →
    GAP-G-01) and range deps not visible to the scheduler (H1 → GAP-G-03).
    FormulaRegion binder (Option B) remains deferred to Phase 4.7.
    
    Phase 4.3 V2 (SUMIF / COUNTIF / SUMIFS / AVERAGEIF + lookup family) was
    gated on these — the gate is now CLEARED. V2 batch is the natural next
    session after this lands.

    --workspace --all-targets -D warnings, workspace tests, build-flags
    guard, cargo-lock pin guard, multiversion clones (aarch64 NEON
    verified at disassembly), cargo audit (single pre-existing
    atomic-polyfill allowed warning).
    
    # Option A — per-formula revocation API (closes GAP-G-01)

      - New field StripeIndex::formula_to_stripe_keys (HashMap<NodeId,
        Vec<StripeKey>>) mirroring the forward stripe_to_dependents map.

        HashSet::insert returns true; preserves A5 stripe_inserts dedup
        semantics).
      - New pub fn StripeIndex::clear_for_formula(node) — O(stripes-this-
        formula-is-in); prunes empty stripe buckets after removal.
      - range_contains_rowcol bumped from pub(crate) to pub for runtime
        consumers.

        clear, partial clear (preserving other formulas), idempotency,
        re-register after clear.

        drains and returns the prior target list.
      - New pub fn AdjacencyVectors::remove_back_pointer(target, from):
        retain-based removal of every (from -> target) back-pointer.
      - +4 unit tests covering drain, empty-node clear, OOB panic,
        multi-edge removal.
    
    - crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs
      - New pub fn Graph::clear_outgoing(node): drains outgoing[node],
        walks each former target's incoming list to drop the back-pointer,
        bumps revision once.
      - New pub fn Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node): removes
        formula_to_range_deps entry + calls StripeIndex::clear_for_formula,
        bumps revision once.

        range_ref_sheet helper (consumed by the session-side supplemental
        builder).

        graph level (rebind_workflow_no_stale_false_circ, rebind_workflow_
        no_stale_stripe_dirty), preservation of other dependents'
        back-pointers, determinism, and idempotency.

     /// Monotonic version counter. Bumped on every structural change (node add, edge add).
     /// Callers comparing graph state across operations rely on this — e.g. the dirty walk
     /// re-runs only if `graph.revision() != self.last_walked_revision`.
     pub fn revision(&self) -> u64 {
         self.revision
     }
 
     /// Borrow a node by id. Panics on out-of-bounds — graph callers should never construct
     /// `NodeId`s by hand; ids only come from `add_*_node` return values.
     pub fn node(&self, id: NodeId) -> &Node {
         &self.nodes[id.index()]
     }
 
     /// Iterate `node` -> dependencies (what `node` depends on).
     pub fn outgoing(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
         self.edges.outgoing(node)
     }
 
     /// Iterate dependents -> `node` (what depends on `node`). The dirty walk uses this.
     pub fn incoming(&self, node: NodeId) -> &[NodeId] {
         self.edges.incoming(node)
     }
 
     fn push_node(&mut self, node: Node) -> NodeId {
         let id = NodeId(
             self.nodes
                 .len()
                 .try_into()
                 .expect("Graph::push_node: node count exceeds u32::MAX"),
         );
         self.nodes.push(node);
         self.edges.push_node();
         self.revision = self
             .revision
             .checked_add(1)
             .expect("Graph::push_node: revision counter overflowed u64");
         id
     }
 
     pub fn add_cell_node(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> NodeId {
         self.push_node(Node::Cell(CellNode { sheet, row, col }))
     }
 
     pub fn add_range_node(&mut self, range: RangeNode) -> NodeId {
         self.push_node(Node::Range(range))
     }
 
     pub fn add_formula_region_node(&mut self, region: FormulaRegionNode) -> NodeId {
         self.push_node(Node::FormulaRegion(region))
     }
 
     /// Spill nodes are Phase 3+ — panic loudly if Phase 0 callers attempt construction.
     /// Per the no-fallbacks rule: don't silently accept an unreachable variant.
     pub fn add_spill_node(&mut self, _anchor: NodeId, _shape: (RowId, ColId)) -> NodeId {
         panic!(
             "Graph::add_spill_node: Phase 3+ feature (dynamic array spill anchors); not \
              constructed in Phase 0. Tracked via the shape-locked Node::Spill variant."
         );
     }
 
     /// Add a directed edge `from -> to` ("from depends on to"). Both ids must already exist.
     /// Caller is responsible for not double-adding (Phase 0 has no dedup; see edges.rs).
     pub fn add_edge(&mut self, from: NodeId, to: NodeId) {
         self.edges.add_edge(from, to);
         self.revision = self
             .revision
             .checked_add(1)
             .expect("Graph::add_edge: revision counter overflowed u64");
     }
 
     /// Register that `formula_node` depends on cells in `range`. Atomically updates BOTH
     /// the stripe index AND `formula_to_range_deps`. Callers should invoke this once per
     /// range reference in a formula's AST (a formula like `=SUM(A:A) + COUNT(B:B)` calls
     /// register_range_dependency twice).
     ///
     /// The Phase 0 W3-5 callers (Week 4 `ql-exec` binder) own:
     /// 1. Calling `add_cell_node` / `add_formula_region_node` for the formula's owning vertex.
     /// 2. Calling `register_range_dependency` for each range the formula references.
     /// 3. Optionally adding explicit edges via `add_edge` for direct cell-to-cell deps.
     pub fn register_range_dependency(
         &mut self,
         formula_node: NodeId,
         range: RangeRef,
         sheet_id: SheetId,
     ) {
         assert!(
             formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
             "Graph::register_range_dependency: formula_node {} out of bounds",
             formula_node.index()
         );
         let inserts_before = self.stripes.total_insertions();
         self.stripes.register(formula_node, &range, sheet_id);
         let inserts_after = self.stripes.total_insertions();
         // `stripe_inserts` counts the NEW insertions this call produced. HashSet dedup
         // means a re-registration of the same (formula_node, range) might be a no-op,
         // in which case the delta is zero — matches Formualizer's instrumentation
         // semantics (`engine/graph/range_deps.rs:78-86`).
         self.stats.stripe_inserts = self
             .stats
             .stripe_inserts
             .checked_add((inserts_after - inserts_before) as u64)
             .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: stripe_inserts overflowed u64");
         self.formula_to_range_deps
             .entry(formula_node)
             .or_default()
             .push(range);
         self.revision = self
             .revision
             .checked_add(1)
             .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: revision counter overflowed u64");
     }
 
     /// Borrow the instrumentation counters. Used by tests + the W3-7 graph-profile.json
     /// export (OG-06 acceptance).
     pub fn stats(&self) -> GraphStats {
         self.stats
     }
 
+    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure.** Revoke every outgoing edge from
+    /// `node`, AND the symmetric back-pointer in each former target's
+    /// `incoming` slot. Used by `CalcgraphSession::extract_and_register_deps`
+    /// on re-bind so a formula whose direct cell deps changed
+    /// (e.g. `=A1` → `=1`) doesn't leave stale forward edges that would
+    /// confuse the Tarjan scheduler into seeing a false `#CIRC!` cycle.
+    ///
+    /// Bumps `revision` exactly once per call (not per edge), matching
+    /// the existing semantics of `add_edge` / `register_range_dependency`.
+    /// A node with no outgoing edges is a no-op.
+    ///
+    /// This is the documented exception to Phase 0's append-only invariant.
+    /// See `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
+    pub fn clear_outgoing(&mut self, node: NodeId) {
+        assert!(
+            node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
+            "Graph::clear_outgoing: node {} out of bounds",
+            node.index()
+        );
+        let drained = self.edges.clear_outgoing(node);
+        for target in drained {
+            self.edges.remove_back_pointer(target, node);
+        }
+        self.revision = self
+            .revision
+            .checked_add(1)
+            .expect("Graph::clear_outgoing: revision counter overflowed u64");
+    }
+
+    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure (range side).** Revoke every range-dep
+    /// registration held by `formula_node`. Clears both
+    /// `formula_to_range_deps[formula_node]` and every stripe membership
+    /// in `StripeIndex` (via `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula`). After
+    /// this call, writes to cells in the OLD ranges no longer dirty
+    /// `formula_node` via the precision-check path.
+    ///
+    /// Bumps `revision` once per call. Idempotent — a formula with no
+    /// range deps is a no-op.
+    ///
+    /// Companion to `clear_outgoing`: the session-side `extract_and_register_deps`
+    /// calls both before re-extracting the formula's new dep set.
+    pub fn clear_range_deps_for_formula(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
+        assert!(
+            formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
+            "Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula: node {} out of bounds",
+            formula_node.index()
+        );
+        self.formula_to_range_deps.remove(&formula_node);
+        self.stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node);
+        self.revision = self
+            .revision
+            .checked_add(1)
+            .expect("Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula: revision counter overflowed u64");
+    }
+
     /// Bump `chunked_reduce_chunks_processed` by `n`. Phase 0 doesn't increment this
     /// from inside ql-calcgraph; Week 4 ql-exec calls this on each chunk it processes
     /// during a `SUM`/`COUNT`/etc. reduction. Locking the API in W3-6 means Week 4 just
     /// wires the increments without a graph-shape change.
     pub fn record_chunked_reduce(&mut self, chunks_processed: u64) {
         self.stats.chunked_reduce_chunks_processed = self
             .stats
             .chunked_reduce_chunks_processed
             .checked_add(chunks_processed)
             .expect("Graph::record_chunked_reduce: counter overflowed u64");
     }
 
     /// True dependents of a cell write at `(sheet, row, col)`. Two-pass:
     /// 1. Stripe lookup → candidate formula nodes (CORR-21 coarse-grained).
     /// 2. Precision filter against each candidate's `formula_to_range_deps` — drops
     ///    candidates whose actual range doesn't contain the cell.
     ///
     /// Returns the precise dependent set, **sorted ascending by `NodeId`** so callers
     /// receive deterministic ordering across runs. Audit H2 (2026-05-12): the previous
     /// implementation collected from a `HashSet` which leaked Rust's randomized hash
     /// state — two identical edits on the same workbook would produce different
     /// downstream scheduling order, silently breaking replay determinism.
     pub fn dependents_for_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Vec<NodeId> {
         let candidates = self.stripes.candidates_for_cell(sheet, row, col);
         let mut out: Vec<NodeId> = candidates
             .into_iter()
             .filter(|formula_id| {
                 self.formula_to_range_deps
                     .get(formula_id)
                     .is_some_and(|ranges| ranges.iter().any(|r| range_contains_rowcol(r, row, col)))
             })
             .collect();
         out.sort();
         out
     }
 
     /// Total registered range-dependencies across all formula nodes. Counts each
     /// `register_range_dependency` call. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
     pub fn range_dependency_count(&self) -> usize {
         self.formula_to_range_deps.values().map(Vec::len).sum()
     }
 
+    /// W5-50 — public accessor for the per-formula range-dep list. Returns
+    /// an empty slice if the formula has no range deps (or doesn't exist).
+    /// The runtime side uses this to build the supplemental adjacency for
+    /// `topo::schedule_with_supplemental` (GAP-G-03 closure).
+    pub fn range_deps_for(&self, formula_node: NodeId) -> &[RangeRef] {
+        self.formula_to_range_deps
+            .get(&formula_node)
+            .map(Vec::as_slice)
+            .unwrap_or(&[])
+    }

+    /// W5-50 — return the sheet a `RangeRef` resolves against. `None`
+    /// means "use the formula's owning sheet" (the binder convention).
+    /// Helper so runtime code doesn't have to match on `RangeRef`
+    /// variants just to pull the sheet field.
+    pub fn range_ref_sheet(range: &RangeRef) -> Option<SheetId> {
+        match range {
+            RangeRef::Cells { sheet, .. }
+            | RangeRef::WholeColumn { sheet, .. }
+            | RangeRef::WholeRow { sheet, .. } => *sheet,
+        }
+    }

     /// Count nodes by variant. Used by W3-7 graph-profile.json export (OG-06) so a
     /// reader can quickly attribute total graph mass to the right node kind. O(n) over
     /// the node array; not on any hot path.
     pub fn node_counts_by_variant(&self) -> NodeCountsByVariant {
         let mut counts = NodeCountsByVariant::default();
         for n in &self.nodes {
             match n {
                 Node::Cell(_) => counts.cell += 1,
                 Node::Range(_) => counts.range += 1,
                 Node::FormulaRegion(_) => counts.formula_region += 1,
                 Node::Spill(_) => counts.spill += 1,
             }
         }
         counts
     }

     /// Read-only public access; mutation goes through `register_range_dependency`.
     pub fn stripe_index(&self) -> &StripeIndex {
         &self.stripes
     }

     /// Audit H2 (2026-05-12): `dependents_for_cell` must return a Vec sorted by NodeId
     /// for determinism. Previously the function collected from a HashSet which leaked
     /// Rust's randomized hash state to callers, silently breaking replay determinism.
     #[test]
     fn dependents_for_cell_returns_sorted_vec() {
         let mut g = Graph::new();
         // 50 formulas in random column order — internal HashSet iteration would yield
         // them in an arbitrary order. dependents_for_cell must sort before returning.
         let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(50);
         for col in 0..50 {
             let id = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, col + 1);
             ids.push(id);
             g.register_range_dependency(
                 id,
                 RangeRef::WholeColumn {
                     sheet: None,
                     start_col: 0,
                     end_col: 0,
                     abs_start: false,
                     abs_end: false,
                 },
                 0,
             );
         }
         let deps = g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0);
         assert_eq!(deps.len(), 50);
         // Strictly increasing by NodeId.
         assert!(deps.windows(2).all(|w| w[0] < w[1]));
         // And: same result on the second call (deterministic).
         assert_eq!(deps, g.dependents_for_cell(0, 5, 0));
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn dependents_for_cell_empty_when_no_ranges_registered() {

         let c1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 2);
         let col_a = RangeRef::WholeColumn {
             sheet: None,
             start_col: 0,
             end_col: 0,
             abs_start: false,
             abs_end: false,
         };
         let col_b = RangeRef::WholeColumn {
             sheet: None,
             start_col: 1,
             end_col: 1,
             abs_start: false,
             abs_end: false,
         };
         g.register_range_dependency(c1, col_a, 0);
         g.register_range_dependency(c1, col_b, 0);
         assert_eq!(g.range_dependency_count(), 2);
         // Two distinct stripes (Column 0 and Column 1)
         assert_eq!(g.stripes().stripe_count(), 2);
         // A write to A500 matches.
         assert_eq!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 500, 0), vec![c1]);
         // A write to B500 matches.
         assert_eq!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 500, 1), vec![c1]);
         // A write to C500 doesn't.
         assert!(g.dependents_for_cell(0, 500, 2).is_empty());
     }
 
     #[test]
     fn mixed_node_types_in_one_graph() {
         let mut g = Graph::new();
         let cell_a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
         let range = g.add_range_node(RangeNode {
             sheet: 0,
             range: RangeRef::WholeColumn {
                 sheet: None,
                 start_col: 0,

         let region = g.add_formula_region_node(FormulaRegionNode {

             target_end_row: 99,
             target_end_col: 1,
             formula_fingerprint: 1,
             chunk_count: 1,
         });
         assert_eq!(g.node_count(), 3);
         assert!(matches!(g.node(cell_a), Node::Cell(_)));
         assert!(matches!(g.node(range), Node::Range(_)));
         assert!(matches!(g.node(region), Node::FormulaRegion(_)));
     }
+
+    // ===== W5-50 (Phase 4 pre-V2): per-formula revocation API =====

+    #[test]
+    fn clear_outgoing_drops_edges_and_back_pointers() {
+        // B1 depends on A1 and A2.
+        let mut g = Graph::new();
+        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
+        let a2 = g.add_cell_node(0, 1, 0);
+        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
+        g.add_edge(b1, a1);
+        g.add_edge(b1, a2);
+        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(b1), &[a1, a2]);
+        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a1), &[b1]);
+        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a2), &[b1]);
+        let rev_before = g.revision();
+
+        g.clear_outgoing(b1);
+
+        assert!(g.outgoing(b1).is_empty());
+        assert!(g.incoming(a1).is_empty(), "back-pointer dropped");
+        assert!(g.incoming(a2).is_empty(), "back-pointer dropped");
+        assert_eq!(g.revision(), rev_before + 1, "single revision bump");
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn clear_outgoing_preserves_other_dependents_back_pointers() {
+        // B1 and C1 both depend on A1. Clearing B1 leaves C1's edge intact.
+        let mut g = Graph::new();
+        let a1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
+        let b1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 1);
+        let c1 = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 2);
+        g.add_edge(b1, a1);
+        g.add_edge(c1, a1);
+
+        g.clear_outgoing(b1);
+
+        assert!(g.outgoing(b1).is_empty());
+        assert_eq!(g.outgoing(c1), &[a1], "C1's edge untouched");
+        assert_eq!(g.incoming(a1), &[c1], "A1's back-pointers now [c1]");
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn clear_outgoing_is_idempotent_and_handles_empty() {
+        let mut g = Graph::new();
+        let a = g.add_cell_node(0, 0, 0);
+        let rev = g.revision();
+        g.clear_outgoing(a); // no edges; just bumps revision
+        g.clear_outgoing(a); // idempotent
+        assert_eq!(g.revision(), rev + 2);
+        assert!(g.outgoing(a).is_empty());
+    }
